## 🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-15 (/wiki-ingest, 21:00 window) — **THE FUNDING SIDE FINALLY GETS A FULL ISSUANCE PATH FROM A CREDIT DESK ($278bn ISSUED, THEN $310bn / $215bn / $125bn IN 26-28E) AND IT SAYS THE BIG FOUR ARE ONLY 30-50% OF THE WAY THROUGH — WITH [[MSFT]] YET TO ISSUE A DOLLAR OF THE $75bn IT NEEDS. MEANWHILE THE DEMAND SIDE SPENT THE DAY REFUSING TO BLINK: HOCK RE-AFFIRMED FY27/FY28, BofA FOUND NO SLOWDOWN IN ORDERS OR LTAs, AND THE TAIWAN SUPPLY CHAIN CALLED IT SUPPLY-CONSTRAINED, NOT DEMAND-CONSTRAINED.**

- 🔴🔴🔴 **THE ISSUANCE PATH, WHICH IS THE THING THIS PAGE HAS BEEN MISSING A SERIES FOR.** BofA initiates hyperscaler credit coverage: **Overweight [[GOOG]]**, Marketweight [[MSFT]] / [[AMZN]] / [[META]]. *"Spreads are mainly impacted by supply concerns, but remain compelling against the broader AI vs Macro investment backdrop. To date, capex has been mainly funded by internal cash flows, though they've issued $278B to date. Expect issuance to continue to $310B in 2026, $215B in 2027, and $125B in 2028."* Progress through the need: **[[GOOG]] and [[AMZN]] furthest along at 45-50%, [[META]] closer to 30%, and [[MSFT]] yet to issue — $75bn needed through YE28** (**BofA Global Research · Tom Curcuruto**, 2026-09-15, relayed via the BofA TMT desk ⚠️ *sales relay of a research initiation*). ⚠️⚠️ **BASIS GUARD — THESE ARE DEBT-ISSUANCE FIGURES, NOT CAPEX.** Do not net the $310bn/$215bn/$125bn against the $950bn/$1.40tn/$1.55tn CSP capex series from JPM (09-07) or MS's *">$1 trillion"* 2027 mark: issuance is the *funding slice*, capex is the spend. ➤ **The shape is the story: issuance PEAKS IN 2026 and then falls by 60% to 2028 on BofA's own path — a credit desk modelling the debt-funded share of this cycle as front-loaded, while every capex series on this page still rises through 2028. Either operating cash flow closes the gap, or one of the two series is wrong.** The [[MSFT]] line is the single cleanest forward test on the page: a first-time issuer with $75bn to place inside 27 months.
- 🔴🔴 **THE BUYER'S OWN SUPPLIER RE-AFFIRMED UNDER MAXIMUM NARRATIVE PRESSURE, ON CAMERA, THE DAY AFTER THE SELL-OFF.** *"AVGO CEO says revenue targets have not changed despite AI Safety warnings, which are not changing infrastructure demand"*; Tan agreed AI needs safeguards but said the technology will *"create huge value"*, comparing its impact to the Industrial Revolution (CNBC "Mad Money", aired 2026-09-14, relayed by the **Morgan Stanley TMT desk**, **UBS tech daily** and **Bernstein tech daily**, all 2026-09-15). The specific numbers each desk attached: UBS — *"standing by forecasts of $115B in AI semiconductor revenue for FY 2027"*; Bernstein — *"Hock blessed AVGO's recent AI revenue targets… should assuage concern around their ability to hit $230B/$30 in rev/EPS for FY28"* (**Bernstein · Tyler Seidman**, 2026-09-15 ⚠️ *specialist sales*). ➤ See [[AVGO]] for the page-level treatment. **This is the highest-quality falsification test the pacing thesis got all week: the merchant ASIC supplier with the most order-book visibility into 2027-28 did not move a number.**
- 🔴 **AND THE INDEPENDENT ORDER-BOOK CHECK FROM THE SEMI ANALYST, SAME MORNING: no slowdown in customer orders, LTAs, capacity commitments or semis pricing**; memory pricing flattish week-on-week; **[[NVDA]] B200 GPU rental pricing only −10% off its March peak**; and **2027 *"remains much a fully booked/contracted year across all compute/networking/memory vendors"*** (**BofA · Vivek Arya**, "State of the Union", 2026-09-15). He also hosted **[[AMD]] Head of IR Matt Ramsay** on 09-14, who *"sees no specific signs of customer slowdown"*.
- 🔴🔴 **THE CONSUMPTION-SIDE TRACKER, AND IT PUTS A NUMBER ON WHY 4Q NON-AI CLOUD DECELERATES.** Bernstein's AWS web-metrics work: *"3Q growth continues to look good as ex-AI revenue growth is poised to accelerate once again (~50bps QoQ). That said, 4Q could see a more meaningful deceleration in non-AI revenue growth"* — which the desk reads as *"mak[ing] sense conceptually as [[AMZN]] lights up more capacity for [[OPENAI]]."* The same tracker: *"recent revenue acceleration at both AWS and Datadog was from AI revenue, with **[[OPENAI]] and [[ANTHROPIC]] the two largest AI-native customers for [[DDOG]]**… Momentum appears to have accelerated for OpenAI in 3Q at **20%+ month-over-month in July** (vs 18% QoQ in 2Q) while **Anthropic also showed impressive growth with ARR at over $65B in July, vs $47B in May and only $9B at the end of 2025**"* (**Bernstein Research**, relayed by **Tyler Seidman**, 2026-09-15 ⚠️ *third-party tracker read relayed by specialist sales — NOT a company disclosure*). ➤➤ **The mechanism matters more than the level: an AI-native customer does not add to cloud growth, it CROWDS OUT the non-AI cohort inside the same capacity envelope. That is the first clean statement on this page of capacity allocation as a revenue-mix decision rather than a capex decision.**
- 🔴 **THE SUPPLY SIDE, FROM THE SUPPLY CHAIN ITSELF — "CONSTRAINTS CONTINUE TO COME FROM SUPPLY RATHER THAN END DEMAND".** UBS's Taiwan Summit (65 companies incl. 40 semis, 340+ investors, day 1 on 2026-09-15) found the chain *"broadly constructive on underlying AI demand"* despite the weekend headlines. The datapoints with dates attached: **Inventec — server revenue growth of 40-50% with customer capacity reservations extending INTO 2028**; **Compal — AI servers going from a low-single-digit % of 2025 sales to a target 30-40% of sales by 2027**, and describing **DRAM and NAND as the industry's largest constraint**; **Lenovo and Inventec both naming server memory shortages as the primary factor limiting shipments**, with tight conditions expected *"well into 2027"*; **Delta — AI infrastructure growth increasingly constrained by power delivery and component availability rather than by customer demand**, with power ICs and semiconductors the bottleneck (UBS raised Delta to a Key Buy Call). Decision-making is migrating *"from the server level to the rack level"*, creating content in liquid cooling, power distribution, manifolds, busbars and integration; **Inventec also flagged growing customer interest in lower-cost inference platforms versus NVL72 clusters** (**UBS · Sunny Lin / Randy Abrams / Diana Chang**, 2026-09-15). ⚠️ **AND THE SHARE-SHIFT CONSEQUENCE NOBODY MODELS: *"larger manufacturers believe procurement scale is becoming an important competitive advantage, creating opportunities for share gains as smaller competitors struggle to secure sufficient component supply"* — a memory shortage that CONSOLIDATES the ODM/OEM layer rather than merely capping it.**
- ⚠️ **THE BINDING PHYSICAL CONSTRAINT THIS WEEK IS NOT MEMORY AND NOT GPUs — IT IS SUBSTRATE.** TrendForce's weekly supply-chain radar tracks GPU, DRAM, NAND, HDD, ABF and MLCC lead times against a balanced-market benchmark: *"**GPU is the only one of the six components sitting at a balanced lead time. ABF substrate runs at 48 to 56 weeks against a balanced 12, the widest gap on this week's table**"* (**TrendForce, relayed by Jefferies Asia TMT · Conor O'Mara**, 2026-09-15 ⚠️ *sales commentary, not research*). Corroborating on the passives leg: **Murata is considering new and potentially bigger MLCC capacity on strong server demand, reportedly investing ~$517M during FY2026-27 to expand MLCC production by >20%, with executive deputy president Masanori Minamide evaluating an additional three-to-five-year capacity plan**; *"AI servers are eating MLCC capacity, and Chinese suppliers are taking what's left"* (Digitimes, relayed by the MS TMT desk, 2026-09-15). ➤ **Four rungs of the bill of materials are now rationed and the accelerator is the loosest of them.**
- 🔭 **THE LONGEST-DATED SUPPLY CLAIM OF THE DAY, WITH UNUSUALLY CONCRETE UNITS.** [[CEREBRAS]] CEO Andrew Feldman *"expects chip and data-center shortages to persist for at least five years because AI demand grows at software speed while fabs, power grids, and real estate expand on multiyear physical timelines"*, and supplies the timing: **~15 months to add wafer capacity and roughly 18 months for a 50-MW data-centre block** (podcast, relayed by the **Morgan Stanley TMT desk**, 2026-09-15). ⚠️ *An interested party — a compute seller arguing compute stays scarce. Logged as a dated claim with a testable unit, not adopted.*
- 📌 **NEW US CAPACITY ON THE BUILD SIDE:** **Wiwynn opened an advanced manufacturing facility in Socorro, Texas — a US$1.6bn AI-server manufacturing hub** (Digitimes, 2026-09-15), and **ASE subsidiary SPIL is in the final stages of an Arizona advanced-packaging plan** after **ASE raised 2026 capex by $2bn to $10.5bn ($6.5bn on equipment)** (TrendForce / Rothschild & Co, 2026-09-15). Cross-ref [[SPCX]] for the same-day report that SpaceX has ordered **~4.5GW of large gas turbines from Doosan** for its own combined-cycle generation (**BofA · Andrew Obin**, 2026-09-15) — the buyer-builds-its-own-power leg of this theme now has a named turbine OEM and a unit count.

## 🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-14 (/run-inbox, 23:00 window) — **THE CONFERENCE-LEVEL CAPEX VERDICT, FROM THE BUYERS AND THEIR SUPPLIERS IN THE SAME WEEK: DEMAND ABOVE AVAILABLE CAPACITY INTO 2027-2028 WITH POWER, SHELLS AND PERMITTING — NOT CHIPS — AS THE BINDING CONSTRAINT, AND SEMICAP VISIBILITY NOW UNDERWRITTEN WITH CASH RATHER THAN FORECASTS**

⚠️ **SOURCE: GS Communacopia 2026 — the analyst's own small-group and broadcast notes (2026-09-14).** ⚠️ **MANAGEMENT SOURCES THROUGHOUT, NOT GOLDMAN SACHS RESEARCH: the conference is Goldman's, the speakers are company management. No rating, no price target and no estimate anywhere in this material.** ✅ **NOTHING SUPERSEDED — no capex aggregate, per-name capex figure, GW figure or financing total on this page was overwritten, and the Microsoft IR meeting already folded here on 2026-09-14 (the 09-09 room) REMAINS the primary for the Microsoft items below.**

- 🔴🔴 **THE CROSS-NAME STATEMENT, WHICH IS WHAT A CONFERENCE IS ACTUALLY FOR: _"AI infrastructure demand still exceeds supply everywhere we heard it: NVIDIA, Broadcom, Microsoft, CoreWeave and Nebius all described demand above available capacity into 2027-2028, with POWER, SHELLS AND PERMITTING REPLACING CHIPS AS THE BINDING CONSTRAINT"_** (GS Communacopia 2026 — conference takeaways, 2026-09-14). ➤ **Five independent managements across four layers of the stack — merchant silicon, custom silicon, hyperscale cloud and neocloud — naming one constraint in one week. For THIS page the consequence is the thing it has been circling since July: a rising capex line and a flat capacity line are not a contradiction, because the marginal dollar is buying ENERGISED REAL ESTATE with a multi-year lead time rather than silicon with a multi-quarter one.** ⚠️ **It is the note author's synthesis ACROSS sessions, not any single company's statement. No GW, no dollars and no dates attach to it, and it must not be quoted as a company disclosure.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE SUPPLIER-SIDE HALF, AND IT IS THE PART THAT CHANGES HOW THE CYCLE SHOULD BE CLASSIFIED: _"Semicap visibility is now underwritten with cash rather than forecasts"_ — [[ASML]] customer PREPAYMENTS, [[SNDK]] multi-year contracts with ESCROWED GUARANTEES, and [[TSM]]'s "significantly higher" 2026-2028 capex versus $101B in the prior three years, which the note reads as pointing to a MULTI-NODE, MULTI-YEAR CYCLE RATHER THAN A SPIKE** (GS Communacopia 2026 — conference takeaways, 2026-09-14). ➤ **The relevance to a HYPERSCALER-CAPEX page is the symmetry it exposes, and it cuts both ways. The hyperscalers are funding forward capacity with LEASES AND PREPAID POWER; their suppliers are being funded forward with CUSTOMER PREPAYMENTS AND ESCROW. The same de-risking mechanism is now running at both ends of the chain — bullish for order DURABILITY, and bearish for the informational content of any backlog, because money committed down the line is not money at risk.** ⚠️⚠️ **UNIT AND BASIS GUARD: the $101B is a TSMC THREE-YEAR HISTORICAL BASE, not a forward number, and "significantly higher" carries no figure. DO NOT construct a 2026-2028 TSMC capex estimate from that pair, and do not chain it into the hyperscaler aggregates on this page — foundry capex and cloud capex are different denominators.**
- 🔴 **[[MSFT]], NET-NEW BEYOND THE 09-09 IR MEETING ALREADY ON THIS PAGE — THE NEAR-TERM CAPACITY POSITION STATED AS A FAIT ACCOMPLI: demand is still improving quarter after quarter, Microsoft has STOPPED PREDICTING when supply and demand rebalance, and near-term capacity is "you got what you got" for next year** (GS Communacopia 2026 — Microsoft small group, 2026-09-14). ➤ **That is the sharpest available version of the point CFO Amy Hood made from the other direction at the same conference (very little can get built and come online in the next 12 months): the FY27 capacity base is ALREADY SET, so the FY27 revenue debate is an EFFICIENCY debate, not a capex debate. Today's decisions are described as primarily affecting FY28, FY29, FY30 and beyond, with the only FY27 lever being to pull kit-deployment dates forward and accelerate the fill of existing facilities.** ✅ **Financing posture restated and unchanged: NO PLANS TO RAISE EQUITY, free cash flow positive, debt markets tapped many times, and RISING LEASING COMMITMENTS TREATED AS ALTERNATIVE DEBT — the company's own framing of the uncommenced-lease stock this page tracks from the outside.**
- 🔴 **THE CAPITAL-LIGHT LEG NOBODY MODELS, AND IT CUTS AT THIS PAGE'S DENOMINATOR RATHER THAN AT ITS NUMBERS: when [[GOOG]] sells TPU systems into a customer's own data centre it does NOT fund the data centre, the power or the space, earns strong free cash flow, and diversifies monetisation** (GS Communacopia 2026 — Alphabet broadcast, 2026-09-14; the primary is the 09-08 Kurian transcript already on [[GOOG]] and on [themes/custom-asic-tpu](custom-asic-tpu.md)). ➤ **Hyperscaler capex measures WHO OWNS THE ASSET, not how much compute gets built. To the extent first-party silicon is SOLD rather than DEPLOYED, capacity growth and reported capex DECOUPLE — and the decoupling runs in the direction that makes Google's lower internal gigawatt allocation look like a business-model choice rather than a shortfall.** ⚠️ **UNSIZED IN EVERY DIRECTION: no TPU hardware gross margin, no size for the Blackstone neocloud, and no split of how much demand sits outside Google's own cloud was disclosed. It is a MECHANISM, not a number, and nothing on this page should be re-based on it.**
- **THE SAME TRADE IN A SMALLER BALANCE SHEET, RESTATED RATHER THAN RE-SCORED: [[NBIS]] says PREPAYMENTS FUND A MEANINGFUL SHARE OF CAPEX and that improving contract economics are shortening paybacks and opening cheaper debt and asset-backed financing, while [[CRWV]]'s latest facility lets LENDERS ASSUME RENEWAL RISK, making it possible to finance assets supporting contracts SHORTER than the traditional five-year term** (GS Communacopia 2026 — Nebius and CoreWeave broadcasts, 2026-09-14). ⚠️ **Both sit on their company pages and neither is re-scored here.** ➤ 🔴 **AND THE BEAR CASE THE NOTE ITSELF NAMES IS TIMING, NOT DEMAND: _"the late-2027/2028 overbuild scenario (cloud margins ~37% to 27%) and the spread of vendor-linked financing — Broadcom XPV residual-value guarantees, NVIDIA backstops, CoreWeave and Nebius contract-backed debt — are the items to watch for a derating."_** ⚠️⚠️ **THE ~37% TO 27% CLOUD-MARGIN PATH IS A SCENARIO SKETCH INSIDE A CONFERENCE SYNTHESIS — NO BASE, NO NAME, NO YEAR-BY-YEAR AND NO MODEL BEHIND IT. It is NOT adopted, it is NOT a house number, and it must NOT be placed beside the measured hyperscaler cloud operating-margin series this page already carries (1Q25 36.3% through 2Q26 38.9%, Jefferies AI Deep Dive, 09-02) as though the two were the same quantity. Logged as the SHAPE of the bear, not as an estimate.**

## 🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-14 (/wiki-ingest, 21:00 window) — **THE FIRST HOUSE TO COST "PACING" SAYS IT ADDS ~18% TO AI-LAB COMPUTE FROM 2027 AND EATS LAB MARGINS, NOT COMPUTE DEMAND — I.E. THE COMPLEX SOLD OFF 6% ON A MODEL THAT RAISES THE VOLUME IT BUYS. AND JPM's OWN CLIENTS SAY THE CAPEX CYCLE IS FINE AND THEY STILL WON'T BUY IT.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE NUMBER, VERBATIM: *"Pacing adds 18% to compute costs for AI labs from '27 onwards, reduces the currently frothy AI lab inference margins to the degree that these costs don't get passed through in higher token pricing, and we don't see much impact on training costs although visibility is low long term."*** (**Barclays · Ross Sandler, Alex Hughes, Michael DiSanto, Alexander Kessinger** — *"U.S. Internet: Compute Increases ~18% From Frontier Lab 'Pacing'"*, 2026-09-14, completed 22:54 GMT).
- ➤➤ **PARSE IT CAREFULLY, BECAUSE THE TAPE DID NOT: the 18% is an increase in the compute the labs CONSUME, and therefore in what they BUY from the names on this page. The damage lands on the LABS' OWN INFERENCE MARGIN, and only to the extent they cannot pass it through in token pricing. Training is *"not much impact."* ➤ On Barclays' own arithmetic, "pacing" is a demand TAILWIND for hyperscaler and semi revenue and a margin HEADWIND for privately-held AI labs — while the SOX fell 6% and optical fell 10% on the news.**
- ⚠️ **THREE LIMITS ON THE 18%, ALL FROM THE NOTE ITSELF: (1) it is a NEAR-TERM ('27) figure — pacing *"creates more uncertainty longer term"* and long-term *"visibility is low"*, so do NOT chain it into an out-year series the way this page chains capex aggregates; (2) the margin conclusion rests entirely on an UNTESTED pass-through assumption about token pricing; (3) only the emailed summary and key take-away were read — the full report was not retrieved, so nothing beyond the quoted text is attributable to Barclays.**
- 🔴 **THE DE-RATING MECHANISM, NAMED, AND THE ANALOGUE IS ON THIS WIKI: *"Given no frontier lab pure plays are public, the de-rating is happening downstream of them across AI infrastructure."* Barclays' comparison is *"when META went through its own safety/security issues back in '18, when its multiple de-rated by ~40%."* Their winners from a slower model cadence: *"any slowdown of model release cadence theoretically helps GOOGL/META shares (plus inference providers like [[AMZN]], [[MSFT]], xAI etc. in the race)"*** (Barclays, 2026-09-14). ➤ **Note the structural point this page should keep: the listed AI-infrastructure complex is now the TRADING PROXY for private-lab risk. Every piece of frontier-lab news reprices this page's names with no offsetting instrument.**
- ★★ **THE CAPEX QUESTION WAS PUT TO CLIENTS THE SAME DAY AND THE ANSWER WAS "UNCHANGED": *"The underlying AI capex cycle remain strong near term — and there remain clear bottlenecks in areas such as memory and opticals"*, while *"client willingness to add to Tech is weak — conviction levels are falling, the risks perceived to be rising, and hopes of any near term re-gross have been pushed back"*** (**J.P. Morgan APAC Thematics · Matthew See**, 2026-09-14). **Scott Silver (JPM EMEA Tech Spec Sales) states it flatly: *"Frustratingly… nobody I spoke really believes this ultimately means less AI spend. Once calmer heads prevail, the conclusion may well be capex is unchanged and this is as much about responsible development & regulatory positioning as economics."*** ➤➤ **FOR THIS PAGE SPECIFICALLY: not one capex ESTIMATE moved today. The gigawatt ladder logged in the 09:40 section directly below (380GW / 340GW / 488GW / 51GW) is untouched by any of this. What moved was the multiple applied to it.**
- ⚠️ **THE ONE GENUINE BEAR MECHANISM, AND IT IS NOT A CAPEX MECHANISM: relayed from TMTB Slack, *"Ed"* argues *"if slower model rollouts hit the inference-side revenue/compute ramp investors were underwriting, that matters more than any shift in training workloads"*, and *"Arun"* that safety spend is *"more likely to reshuffle GPU allocation than materially reduce inference demand, though RSI-dependent frontier ARR is the bigger risk."* **Gavin Baker** independently reaches Barclays' conclusion without the number: pacing *"likely means more spend on alignment, monitoring and evals, with the cost showing up in lower lab margins rather than lower overall compute demand"*** (all relayed by **TMT Breakout**, 2026-09-14 ⚠️⚠️ *independent substack / buy-side newsletter, NOT a broker — no rating, no PT, no house model; the author discloses he actively trades the names discussed; these are SECOND-HAND relays of Slack and X posts, named but unverified*). ➤ **The bear case that survives scrutiny is about the LABS' REVENUE RAMP, not about hyperscaler capex — which is precisely the pass-through this page has been building since the 08-28 Barclays AI-lab→hyperscaler P&L section.**
- ⚠️ **AND A DIRECT CONTRADICTION TO LOG, UNRESOLVED: JPM's client canvass names MEMORY and OPTICALS as the live bottlenecks; a TMTB reader poll the same evening (unsized, self-selected) puts CPUs first as the rotation destination and shows *"very little preference for ASICs or memory."* A sales desk's client canvass and a newsletter poll are not the same evidence class — the poll is recorded here only because it is the one datapoint pointing the other way.**
## 🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-14 (/wiki-ingest, 09:40 window) — **THE GIGAWATT LADDER NOW HAS FOUR RUNGS FROM FOUR HOUSES IN ONE MORNING (380GW / 340GW / 488GW / 51GW) AND NOT ONE PAIR IS MEASURED ON THE SAME BASIS — PLUS A CORRECTION TO THE SECTION DIRECTLY BELOW: MICROSOFT HAS DENIED THE 38GW-BY-2032 REPORT THROUGH TWO INDEPENDENT CHANNELS**

⚠️⚠️⚠️ **CORRECTION TO THE 2026-09-14 SECTION BELOW.** That section is headlined *"MICROSOFT PUTS A GIGAWATT NUMBER ON ITSELF FOR THE FIRST TIME (12GW → 38GW+ BY 2032)"*. **Microsoft has denied it, on the record, twice.** The [[MSFT]] page carries the first-party denial verbatim (*"We called Brody Ford and told him those numbers were not right. Those numbers are not right… He published anyway"*), and on 09-11 Microsoft IR repeated it to Jefferies: Bloomberg *"ran the article even after MSFT told them the numbers were not right,"* 2032 *"is a long ways away,"* and to think MSFT has publicly committed to a 2032 capacity number this far out *"is not correct."* ➤ **The 12GW→38GW figure must be carried as A PRESS REPORT DENIED BY THE COMPANY, not as a hyperscaler self-disclosure. Management offered NO alternative 2032 number and explicitly denied one exists.** ✅ **What survives from that section unchanged is the CFO near-term statement (nothing material can be added for ~12 months) and the MS Credit uncommenced-lease work — neither is disputed.**

🔴🔴🔴 **FOUR CAPACITY NUMBERS LANDED WITHIN THREE HOURS OF EACH OTHER. THEY ARE NOT RIVAL ESTIMATES OF ONE QUANTITY — THEY ARE FOUR DIFFERENT QUANTITIES, AND THE ONLY WAY TO MISUSE THEM IS TO RANK THEM.**

| Figure | Source | What it actually measures | Period / geography |
|---|---|---|---|
| **~380GW added** (C2026 ~30GW → C2030 ~120GW) | **UBS · Tim Arcuri + Karl Keirstead** | Capacity IMPLIED BY SEMIS REVENUE EXPECTATIONS — i.e. back-solved from what the sell-side already has in its models | Global, C2026-C2030 |
| **340GW cumulative** (51GW in 2027 → 105GW in 2030) | **UBS · Francois-Xavier Bouvignies** | Capacity implied by a BOTTOM-UP POWER-SEMIS BOM model (30+ xPU generations) | Global, 2026-2030E |
| **488GW nameplate, ~170GW "credible"** | **Bernstein · Madison** | US DEVELOPMENT PIPELINE as announced, with a credibility haircut applied | US only, pipeline (undated) |
| **~120GW of US power-grid additions** | **UBS · Bouvignies** | Actual GRID capacity additions, the physical supply side | US, 2026-2030E |

⚠️⚠️ **TWO TEAMS AT THE SAME BANK PUBLISHED 380GW AND 340GW FOR THE SAME WINDOW ON THE SAME MORNING — an ~12% internal spread, from a revenue-implied model and a BOM-implied model respectively. Neither note references the other. Logged as a house internal inconsistency, NOT as a range.**

🔴🔴 **THE USEFUL COMPARISON IS THE ONLY ONE THAT IS LIKE-FOR-LIKE, AND IT IS THE BEAR CASE: Bouvignies puts his own 340GW of 2026-2030 demand against *"c120GW of power grid additions in the US, c60-70% of global data center buildout, over the same period."*** Grossing the US grid figure up to global at his own 60-70% share gives roughly 170-200GW of grid supply against 340GW of modelled demand. ➤ **He names the bridge explicitly — *"behind-the-meter generation, lower retirements of energy generation facilities and faster and larger buildout of new facilities than expected"* — which is to say the model only closes if all three go right at once.** ⚠️ **Arcuri reaches the identical conclusion from the revenue side: the out-years *"require one to underwrite a BULL CASE in terms of conversion of currently unpermitted grid-connected capacity as well as heavy OFF-GRID capacity adds,"* and even using C2028 as the base *"one has to underwrite a solid upside scenario."* His conclusion is the sentence for this page: *"semis stocks CANNOT AFFORD ANY SLOWDOWN in powered shell infrastructure deployment — so this is clearly a big new risk factor."***

✅ **AND YET BOTH HOUSES REFUSE TO CUT NUMBERS, FOR THE SAME REASON: *"major frontier labs have VERY RECENTLY moved forward with LARGE NEW AI INFRASTRUCTURE COMMITMENTS. Ergo, it is hard to argue that this will drive a big fundamental slowdown in compute procurement and infrastructure deployment — EVEN IF IT MAY DRIVE MULTIPLES TO COMPRESS."*** ➤ **That is the cleanest statement of the 09-14 tape available: an earnings-unchanged, multiple-compression event. Bernstein · Rasgon independently agrees — pacing *"does not imply a spending slowdown"* because AI semi demand is *"driven primarily by INFERENCE workloads as agentic continues to gain traction."***

🔴 **CAPEX GROWTH MARKS, REVISED UP, from UBS · Randy Abrams: total hyperscaler cloud sales +67% (2026E) / +66% (2027E) y/y, with CAPEX REVISED UP to +95% / +43% y/y respectively post the CQ226 results and [[ORCL]] F1Q26 (August) print.** ➤ Note the shape — **capex growth nearly HALVES between 2026 and 2027 (+95% → +43%) while cloud revenue growth is flat (+67% → +66%)**, which is the deceleration-in-the-second-derivative that JPM $950bn/$1.40tn/$1.55tn ladder below already describes. ⚠️ Abrams adds the H226 constraint is *"managing supply bottlenecks from component tightness and stretching lead times RATHER THAN LACK OF ORDER VISIBILITY."*

🔴 **THE NEOCLOUD PRICE LADDER MOVED AND IT IS THE FIRST TIME THIS PAGE CAN SEE CONTRACT PRICING BY DURATION (JPM · Richard Choe, IREN upgrade to OW): neocloud contracts *"have gone from $10-15/W to $15-20/W AND HIGHER TODAY"*; 5-year deals signed ABOVE $15/W, 3-year deals ABOVE $20/W, active discussions at ~$25/W; customer PRE-PAYMENTS 25-50% of value.** ⚠️⚠️ **THESE ARE ANNUAL REVENUE PER WATT and they do NOT reconcile with the higher rungs on [[themes/ai-datacenter-power]]: JPM own arithmetic (IREN ~400MW supporting $4bn ARR) implies ~$10/W/yr, versus $40-50/W/yr from [[NBIS]], [[GOOG]]/[[SPCX]] and NVIDIA own $50bn/GW/yr. A 4-5x GAP with no published reconciliation — candidate causes (IT-MW vs facility-MW, contract vintage, services above bare GPU access) are UNTESTED. DO NOT CHAIN.**

## 🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-14 (/wiki-ingest) — **MICROSOFT PUTS A GIGAWATT NUMBER ON ITSELF FOR THE FIRST TIME (12GW → 38GW+ BY 2032) WHILE ITS CFO SAYS NOTHING CAN BE ADDED FOR TWELVE MONTHS; AND MORGAN STANLEY CREDIT FINDS UNCOMMENCED LEASE COMMITMENTS ACROSS FIVE IG HYPERSCALERS HAVE GONE FROM >$800bn TO >$1.1 TRILLION IN ONE QUARTER**
_(Bloomberg via The Information AM, **2026-09-11** · Amy Hood at GS Communacopia, **2026-09-09**, and GS · Gabriela Borges takeaways, 2026-09-10 · MS Credit · **Lindsay A Tyler**, "ORCL Credit: Off Balance Sheet, On the Rise — FY1Q27 10-Q Highlights", **2026-09-11** · JPM · **Doug Anmuth**, META upgrade, 2026-09-10)_

- 🔴🔴🔴 **A HYPERSCALER FINALLY STATES ITS OWN CAPACITY IN GIGAWATTS, ON BOTH ENDS OF A DATED RANGE.** [[MSFT]] plans to take Azure datacentre capacity from **12 GIGAWATTS TODAY to OVER 38 GIGAWATTS BY 2032**. ➤ **This page has spent months triangulating hyperscaler GW from capex dollars and $/GW assumptions. This is the denominator itself, from the company, with a base year and a target year.** ⚠️ Sourcing is Bloomberg → The Information; Microsoft did not comment.
- 🔴🔴 **AND THE NEAR TERM IS THE EXACT OPPOSITE, FROM THE CFO, ON THE RECORD.** Amy Hood: Microsoft *"likely won't be able to increase its planned data center capacity FOR AT LEAST A YEAR"* — ***"VERY LITTLE CAN GET BUILT AND COME ONLINE IN THE NEXT 12 MONTHS. What we're all focused on is making sure that you're making the right long-term investments into LAND AND POWER."*** GS's read of the same appearance puts the operational emphasis on *"how quickly it can get new supply **REVENUE READY**"* rather than on the quantum. ➤ **Together the two statements shape the curve: the 2027-2032 slope is steep, and 2026 is flat by physical constraint. This is the sharpest version yet of the "next year's capex cannot become next year's capacity" finding this page logged on 09-09 — now with both endpoints numbered.**
- ⚠️⚠️ **THE COST OF THE CONSTRAINT IS STATED IN LOST BUSINESS, NOT JUST IN DELAY:** the shortage *"has forced the company to TURN AWAY CUSTOMERS seeking to rent servers equipped with AI-specialized chips"*, and at times forced a choice *"between expanding its Azure cloud business and setting aside servers to run more of its Copilot AI software."* Microsoft employees *"with direct knowledge"* attribute the crunch internally to **Hood's own early-2025 pause on some datacentre construction** — ⚠️ unnamed-source attribution, NOT ADOPTED; the GW figures and the direct quotes are the reliable part.
- 🔴 **THE RELIEF VALVE IS THE NEOCLOUD TRADE, AND IT IS NOW NAMED:** Microsoft *"has more recently been inking deals to rent space from neoclouds like [[CRWV]] CoreWeave, NSCALE and [[NBIS]] Nebius, and has also rented space from cloud rival [[AMZN]] AWS."* ➤ **A hyperscaler renting from its own competitor is the clearest statement available that the constraint is physical rather than financial.** Corroborated from the other side the same week: [[SMCI]] management called the neocloud AI-server opportunity *"durable"*, and [[CRWV]]'s CEO described monetising *"higher value layers"* on top of GPU access.
- 🔴🔴🔴 **THE OFF-BALANCE-SHEET AGGREGATE IS THE BIGGEST NUMBER OF THE WINDOW AND IT IS NOT IN ANY EQUITY NOTE.** MS Credit, reading [[ORCL]]'s FY1Q27 10-Q: ***"total UNCOMMENCED lease commitments across FIVE IG HYPERSCALERS rose to >$1.1 TRILLION, from >$800bn at ORCL's FY4Q print."*** ➤ **~$300bn of additional contracted-but-not-yet-commenced datacentre obligation across the five, in a single quarter, none of it yet on a balance sheet.** Oracle's own line went to **$288bn** after a ~$260bn plateau, which MS reads as *"implying a ~1GW LEASE SIGNING and substantial 'debt' still to hit the balance sheet"*, with on-balance-sheet lease liability up only $6bn q/q net and *"treatment remains skewed to OPERATING vs. FINANCE."* ⚠️ **The five issuers are not individually named in the relayed summary; the $1.1tn is MS Credit's aggregate, not a disclosed figure.**
- 🔴 **AND A SINGLE-NAME CAPEX FORECAST FAR ABOVE CONSENSUS:** JPM · Anmuth, upgrading [[META]] to Overweight, models **capex of $243bn in 2027 (+70%) and $284bn in 2028 (+17%)**, *"ambos bem acima do consenso"*, with **FCF of −$65bn to −$70bn per year** across both. ⚠️⚠️ **PERIOD-BASIS DISCIPLINE REQUIRED: this wiki's documented META CY-sum-vs-annual capex wedge runs +8-9%, and the CY sum has previously breached management's own $130-145bn ceiling while the annual line sat inside it. $243bn is ~1.7x the top of that guided ceiling — a forecast of a future guide, not a restatement of one. NOT ADOPTED; compare only against 1FY/2FY annual lines.**

## 🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-14 (Communacopia-week Notion notes ingest; source dated 2026-09-09) — **[[MSFT]] IR IN CAPSTONE'S OWN ROOM: "NO SHIFT FROM CAUTIOUS TO AGGRESSIVE", THE LAND → COLD-SHELL → POWERED-SHELL STAGING MODEL IS CONCEDED UNACHIEVABLE AT THIS DEMAND, NEXT-12-MONTH SPEND IS "PRETTY HARD TO SHIFT", LAND BOUGHT TODAY IS FY28-30 REVENUE — AND LEASES ARE DESCRIBED BY THE COMPANY AS "ACCESSING THE DEBT MARKET IN A SLIGHTLY UNIQUE WAY"**
_(Microsoft IR, investor group meeting @ Communacopia week, **2026-09-09** — Capstone notes, Notion AI auto-transcript with unlabelled speakers, archived 2026-09-14: [transcript](../../MSFT/transcripts/MSFT_IR-investor-group-meeting-Communacopia-week_2026-09-09.md). ⚠️ **MANAGEMENT (IR) source — no rating, PT or estimate; nothing here is guidance unless stated. Speaker names as transcribed ("Jonathan Nielsen" = Jonathan Neilson, VP IR, per corpus; "Giles Shelton, chairman of IR" unverified). No GW figure of any basis was given. Full single-name treatment on [[MSFT]].**)_

- 🔴🔴 **THE POSTURE QUESTION THIS PAGE HAS BEEN ASKING ABOUT MICROSOFT SINCE THE BLOOMBERG "EARLY-2025 PAUSE" STORY (09-11) WAS PUT TO IR DIRECTLY — AND DENIED.** The questioner: brokers and co-owners see Microsoft *"meaningfully more aggressive than… 3, 6, certainly like 12 or 18 months ago."* IR: *"I don't know that we would say there's been some shift in going from being cautious to being more aggressive… We've continued to see demand meaningfully improve quarter over quarter over quarter. **We've even stopped trying to tell you when we think we're going to be more in balance**… The CapEx decisions we're making have always been based on the demand signal."* ➤ **For this theme the operative content is the WITHDRAWN BALANCE DATE: the 08-13 Microsoft IR call still said "at least through the end of the calendar year"; the company now declines to date supply/demand balance at all. Read with UBS · Abrams (09-14, above): the H2-26 constraint is component tightness and lead times, not order visibility — buyer and supply chain agree the limiter is physical.** ⚠️ **ASR: *"On a relative basis, Lambda's pretty cheap"* = LAND (context: kit is the expensive piece; matches the 09-11 fireside's *"Land is cheap"*), NOT the neocloud Lambda.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE STAGING MODEL — THE THING "PRE-BUILD" IS SUPPOSED TO BUY — IS CONCEDED TO BE UNACHIEVABLE, AND NEAR-TERM SPEND IS INFLEXIBLE.** *"In an ideal world you do have that kind of staging in place. Land, cold shells, powered shells, and then shells that are fully up and running. Because relatively speaking… the expensive piece here is the kits that go inside. So that's the optionality we would love to have… **We have not been able to do that given how fast demand continues to grow.**"* And: *"in the next 12 months, it's pretty hard to shift meaningfully what we're going to spend… there aren't empty shells waiting… to be snapped up and leased."* FY27's lever is velocity, not shells: *"what's exactly the kit we need. **Can we pull even forward some of the dates?**… start that kind of fill process to buy the GPU/CPU."* ➤ **Reconcile with MS's Guidebook (09-07): MS models ~37% of MSFT's '26 data-center capex as "pre-build" and MSFT as the ONLY hyperscaler whose pre-build keeps growing in '27/'28. IR's account says that pre-build has NOT yet delivered a staged, optioned pipeline — the money is going out ahead of revenue without the optionality that is supposed to justify it. That is the bear reading of pre-build, from the company. The bull reading survives too: capex cannot be cut fast either way, so the '27 number is sticky in both directions.**
- 🔴 **LAND BOUGHT TODAY IS FY28-30 REVENUE; RISK TOLERANCE IS PHASED; ENERGY AGREEMENTS TRAVEL WITH LAND.** *"There's no land you're buying today that's going to impact revenue in FY27* (ASR *"F/O 2017"*)*… decisions we're making today are all about what's going to happen in '28, '29, '30 and beyond."* GPUs/CPUs are bought on *"as late a binding call as you possibly can… exactly what region, exactly what mix… what workloads… which customers"*; land is cheap to hold idle; *"if I'm making land purchasing decisions, I want to make sure that I have energy agreements that can go alongside those."* Asked about '29-'30 growth: *"29 and 30 feels like an awfully long time."* ➤ **A dated lag for this theme's capex→capacity→revenue bridge, from the buyer: LAND decisions ≈ 2-4 fiscal years to revenue; KIT decisions late-binding. It is the same shape as Hood's *"very little can get built and come online in the next 12 months"* (09-09) with the far end now dated — and it is why a 2026 capex dollar and a 2026 revenue dollar should not be paired in any ROIC test on this page.**
- 🔴🔴 **FINANCING — EQUITY RULED OUT AS A LEVER EVER PULLED; LEASES DESCRIBED AS DEBT.** *"We said we'll be free cash flow positive. We're in a position where right now we don't really need to access the debt markets… **we've increased our leases as well, our commitments to leasing, and that in and of itself is basically accessing the debt market in a slightly unique way**… you haven't seen us raise equity. That's not a lever that we have pulled in the past. You've seen us raise debt. You've seen us access debt through leasing structures… we could use them opportunistically."* ➤ **Two consequences for the financing ledgers on this page. (1) The largest hyperscaler now describes its lease book as debt-market access in its own words — the premise under MS Credit's >$1.1tn five-name uncommenced-lease aggregate (09-11) and WSJ's ~$3tn off-balance-sheet figure (08-17). MSFT's own line is $329.1B of leases-not-yet-commenced (FY26 10-K, on [[MSFT]]). (2) ⚠️ PERIMETER: leases sit OUTSIDE every bond-issuance perimeter this page carries (GS 27%→33%→35% debt-financed share; MS ~$450bn YTD AI debt; JPM Cembalest ~$320bn) — do not add them to those figures; they are a SIXTH financing perimeter, not an increment to the fifth.** Also de-dramatises the 09-09 relay's *"openness to debt if ROIC is very high"*: per IR, Hood's answer was about capital-structure optionality, not an issuance signal.
- 🔴 **THE REVENUE SIDE OF THE UNIT ECONOMICS, FROM THE BUYER: ACCELERATION IS EFFICIENCY FIRST, PRICE SECOND — AND NO $/GW NUMBER.** *"First and foremost, the acceleration statement is on high levels of efficiency… Pricing will be a tailwind… And that will come in more gradually."* Efficiency = fungible-fleet peak-load management (*"making that same server available for others to monetize"*), dock-to-live *"upwards of 50% this calendar year"* (per Amy and *"Saki"* — ⚠ ASR, probably Satya), *"a 4X improvement in token throughput in our first-party applications"*, compounding quarter on quarter. Price = discount structure on new long-term deals first, then renewal pricing at *"current market context"* (short supply, higher cost, a higher spot market), then new SKUs above predecessors with old SKUs retired; *"We honor existing contracts"*, no blanket repricing, *"I don't want you to have a hockey stick moment in your mind."* ⚠️ **The questioner's $/GW premise — Azure's implied dollars per gigawatt *"somewhat lower than some contracts… out there"* — got NO number, as on 08-13. Azure's $/GW remains untriangulated; do NOT back-fill it from the neocloud $/W ladder above (JPM · Choe) or the $30bn/GW convergence (MS / Hock Tan) — different sellers, different contracts, different bases.**
- 🔴 **SILICON MIX, CONCEDED FROM THE BUYER SIDE: *"I KNOW WE DON'T HAVE THE SCALE YET OF A TRAINIUM* (ASR *"Tradium"*) *OR A TPU."*** Full Jalapeño IP ([[OPENAI]]'s Broadcom-designed accelerator) *"through 2032"* — *"We give back the IP come 2032"* — deployable in Microsoft's own datacentres; Maia 100 → 200 done, *"Maia 300 in the works"*; no first-party mix target (*"there's no badge of honor"*); silicon diversity across vendors AND generations. ➤ **Confirms, from the buyer, the MS Guidebook's *">50% of incremental '27 capacity (internally owned) still NVDA"* for MSFT (09-07) and the ASIC-mix split this page carries (34% → 66% of incremental compute by '28, led by GOOGL TPU and AMZN Trainium): Microsoft's '27 build is a merchant-GPU build by its own account — the [[NVDA]] share of MSFT capex should be modelled ABOVE the group average through '27. Detail on [custom-asic-tpu](custom-asic-tpu.md).** Also on velocity: new silicon generations have *"a burn-in period"* before promised efficiency arrives (*"you measure it against what the performance was promised to be… software optimizations on top"*) — a first-quarters haircut for any GW-to-revenue bridge on newly deployed Vera Rubin capacity (⚠ the Fairwater / Atlanta / Wisconsin sites were the questioner's premise, not confirmed by IR).

## 🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-09 (/wiki-ingest, 21:00 window) — **MORGAN STANLEY IS NOW CIRCULATING *THREE* DIFFERENT HYPERSCALER CAPEX TOTALS IN ONE WEEK ($1.2tn / $1.4tn / $1.5tn); MICROSOFT'S CFO SAYS NEXT YEAR'S SPEND *CANNOT* BECOME NEXT YEAR'S CAPACITY; GOOGLE'S CLOUD CEO PUTS AI-SERVER PAYBACK UNDER TWO YEARS AND *HALF THAT* ON TPUs; AND THREE AI-LEVERED ISSUERS WENT TO NON-USD OR IG MARKETS ON THE SAME DAY.**

The years may differ (2027 versus "next year"), the perimeters may differ ("Big Tech" versus "leading hyperscalers" versus a named hyperscaler set), and "into AI" is not the same as "CapEx." ESTABLISH WHICH NUMBER, WHICH YEAR AND WHICH PERIMETER BEFORE QUOTING ANY OF THEM — this is the same defect class as the CY-sum-versus-annual wedge and the six-basis $/GW ladder. Plus the only build-VELOCITY number anyone has given: "DOCK-TO-LIVE TIMES WERE CUT BY MORE THAN 50% OVER A YEAR."** ➤ **This resolves the tension this page has carried for months — capex guides rising while revenue capacity stays gated — and it says the marginal competitive act is a LONG-DATED POWER OR EQUIPMENT COMMITMENT, not a bigger annual number. It also independently agrees with three unrelated seats in the same week that the limiter is physical build: CLEAN-ROOM SPACE ([[LRCX]] CFO, "the industry's binding constraint through 2027"), CONSTRUCTION LABOUR (Bloomberg via [[TSM]]) and LABOR AND SWITCHGEAR (Jefferies REIT).** **Also new from Hood: MORE THAN 11,000 MODELS on the platform; COBALT IN ~25 DATA CENTRES THIS YEAR with "enthusiasm for scaling Maia"; OPENNESS TO DEBT IF ROIC IS VERY HIGH; and an admission Microsoft WAS LATE MOVING GITHUB TO USAGE BILLING.**

So we have strong payback period."** ➤ **Under one year on TPUs is the single most consequential ROIC disclosure on this page, because it is the first time a hyperscaler has quantified the custom-silicon advantage in PAYBACK rather than in perf/$ — and it lands the same week SemiAnalysis measured TPUv7 Ironwood at ~50% BETTER PERF/$ than Blackwell Ultra on the new TorchTPU stack. Two independent legs of the same argument, same window, one from the buyer and one from a third-party benchmark. ## 🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-09 (/wiki-ingest) — **THE BUYER IS NOW BEING PAID IN EQUITY TO BE A CUSTOMER: AWS TOOK 25M [[QCOM]] WARRANTS AT $161.26 AGAINST UP TO $60BN OF ITS OWN PURCHASES THROUGH 2036. - 🔴🔴 **THE STRUCTURAL NOVELTY, AND IT INVERTS THE VENDOR-FINANCING MECHANIC THIS THEME HAS BEEN TRACKING ALL YEAR: Amazon received 25M [[QCOM]] WARRANTS AT AN EXERCISE PRICE OF $161.26/SHARE, vesting in tranches tied to conditions including AMAZON'S OWN PRODUCT AND SERVICES PURCHASES, UP TO A MAXIMUM OF $60BN OVER THE NEXT 10 YEARS** — ~2% dilution, with the **first tranche of 3.75M shares (~15%) implying ~$9BN** of near-to-medium-term revenue and **~$2BN of QCOM FY27 revenue**. ➤ **Every other vendor-alignment structure on this page has the SUPPLIER taking equity or providing financing to pull demand forward. Here the BUYER captured the equity upside of its own demand. If this becomes the template, hyperscaler capex gets cheaper in economic terms than the reported dollar figure implies — and the supplier's share count is where it shows up.** Cross-filed [[AMZN]], [[QCOM]].
- 🔴 **MS'S "AGE OF INFERENCE" NUMBERS, RELAYED: "Data Center Capex growing +60% IN 2027 ($550B) but decel to +12% IN 2028 ($170B)"**; **"Compute capacity growing 4x TO 145GW IN 2028 and ASICs growing in mix TO 66% IN 2028 (from 34% IN 2025)"**; **"Hyperscaler 2027-2028 OCF expected to be +7-8[x] BIGGER THAN REQUIRED DEBT"** with **"significant capacity to absorb additional issuance beyond $450B of AI-related issuance"**; and a **"$30T CONSUMER SPEND TAM"** alongside a **"$20-30B Global Knowledge Work TAM."** ⚠️ **TWO INTERNAL DEFECTS IN THE RELAY, LOGGED AND NOT ADOPTED: (a) "+12% in 2028 ($170B)" cannot describe growth ON a $550bn 2027 base — $170bn is almost certainly the INCREMENTAL dollar add, not the total; (b) the "$20-30B Global Knowledge Work TAM" sits beside a "$30T Consumer spend TAM" and is almost certainly $20-30 TRILLION given MS's own white-collar-wage framing elsewhere. Both figures are recorded as printed with the defect flagged; the ASIC mix (34% → 66%) and the 145GW compute figure are the usable parts.**
- 🔴 **THE DEMAND EVIDENCE FROM THE SUPPLY SIDE, WHICH IS THE CLEANEST CORROBORATION IN THIS RUN: JPM's Asia team — "major US hyperscalers reported ACCELERATING 2Q26 cloud revenue growth (2Q26 combined AWS/[[MSFT]]/GCP revenue at $106BN, +43% Y/Y, ACCELERATING FROM +35% Y/Y last quarter)"**, and **"within AI, demand is BROADENING OUT with NEOCLOUDS (INCLUDING SPACEX) NOW REPRESENTING A NEW VECTOR OF COMPUTE DEMAND IN ADDITION TO strong CSP buying intent."** ➤ **Cloud revenue ACCELERATING 8 points while capex growth is expected to decelerate in 2028 is the single most important tension on this page — it is the ROIC argument arriving as data rather than as debate.**
- **AND THE SELL-SIDE CONSENSUS MARK FROM THE DESKS: Susquehanna's 09-09 market summary has "hyperscaler capex estimates now EXCEED $1 TRILLION FOR 2027/28"**, with conference commentary pointing to **"constraints increasingly centered on POWER, SUPPLY AND DATA-CENTER CAPACITY RATHER THAN END DEMAND"**, and the debate shifting to **"WHO CAPTURES THE ECONOMICS AND MARGINS."** ⚠️ **Desk commentary, not research — logged as the sentiment mark it is, and note it sits BELOW the JPM CSP capex numbers already on this page ($950bn 2026 / $1.40tn 2027 / $1.55tn 2028).**

## 🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-07 — **J.P. MORGAN PUTS CSP CAPEX AT $950bn (2026) / $1.40tn (2027) / $1.55tn (2028) — AND THE GROWTH RATE COLLAPSES FROM +94% TO +47% TO +11% ACROSS THOSE THREE YEARS.**

**(J.P. Morgan tech team, cited in JPM APAC Thematics · Matthew See, "Asian Semis: Half done, half to go", 2026-09-07)**
- 🔴 **Verbatim: "JPM's tech team now estimates TOTAL CSP CAPEX OF $950bn FOR '26 (+94% y/y), $1.40tr FOR '27 (+47% y/y), and $1.55tr FOR '28 (+11% y/y)… implying (an acceleration) to 42% h/h GROWTH IN CAPEX IN 2H26. And while the supply response is building, IT STILL FALLS SHORT OF DEMAND GROWTH — so our base-case remains that the S/D DEFICIT IN SILICON WILL CONTINUE TO EXPAND NEXT YEAR, supporting a continued ASP UPCYCLE THROUGH 2027."**
- ➤➤ **THE SHAPE IS THE STORY AND IT IS BEING QUOTED AS A BULL DATAPOINT WHEN IT CONTAINS THE BEAR CASE: +94% → +47% → +11%. JPM's own numbers have CSP capex GROWTH FALLING TO NEAR ZERO REAL BY 2028 while the absolute level keeps rising. That is precisely the "capex growth slows (which we expect in '28)" that MS's AI Guidebook uses THE SAME DAY to justify rotating out of semis and into software. 🔴 TWO HOUSES, SAME DAY, THE SAME 2028 DECELERATION — AND OPPOSITE PORTFOLIO CONCLUSIONS. JPM says buy the silicon into the 2027 deficit; MS says the deceleration is the reason to leave. Neither is adopted; the disagreement is recorded because it is the cleanest statement this page has of WHERE the argument now sits: not on whether capex grows, but on how a decelerating second derivative should be priced.**
- **Reconciliation with what this page already carries**: JPM's $950bn/2026 sits ABOVE Jefferies' CY26E $818bn (logged 09-03) — but the baskets differ (JPM says "CSP", Jefferies says "hyperscalers + [[ORCL]] + [[META]]"). ⚠️ **DO NOT TREAT THE GAP AS A REVISION. Establish the constituent list before comparing any two capex aggregates on this page.**
- **JPM's three top-down reasons to stay long the AI supply chain into year-end**: (1) demand growth outpacing supply, with the silicon S/D deficit expanding in 2027; (2) an EARNINGS DISCONNECT — "our Asia Semis basket fell 17% peak-to-trough… this time by 38%, DESPITE AN UNRELENTING MOVE HIGHER IN EPS FORECASTS"; (3) positioning — Asian semis at a **20-30% premium to the 200 DMA, down from 80% in June**, a level that on the prior three occasions "was a good time to buy the sector." The basket is **+27% off the late-July low, having retraced 50% of the June-to-July decline**, with the TWSE Electronics Index **19% off its June high and now just 3% below it.**

- 🔴 **MS Internet (Brian Nowak, Stephen Byrd, Adam Wood, Joseph Moore, Michelle Weaver, Ariana Salvatore + 10 more — "The Morgan Stanley AI Guidebook: Navigating the Age of Inference", 2026-09-07, Industry View Attractive): "we continue to believe we are in the EARLY DAYS OF A FUND FLOW ROTATION following the typical multi-year tech cycle… as flows move MARGINALLY OUT OF HARDWARE/SEMIS/MEMORY AND INTO THE ENABLER AND SOFTWARE LAYERS. While we don't necessarily expect the hardware layer stocks to categorically fall materially (as they are not expensive and this investment cycle is still going to be multi-year), we expect LARGER REVISIONS AND MULTIPLE EXPANSION OPPORTUNITY FURTHER UP THE STACK AS CAPEX GROWTH SLOWS (WHICH WE EXPECT IN '28) and revenue/EBIT beats grow up the stack. This makes us constructive [[AMZN]], [[META]], [[GOOG]], [[MSFT]] and other GenAI enablers."** The eight debates it frames: **hyperscaler capex trajectory; GW compute capacity coming online; GenAI ROIC; the $50-60tn KNOWLEDGE-WORK AND CONSUMER TAMS to be digitized; early adoption signal; the importance of OPEN-WEIGHTS models; AI CREDIT FINANCING; and regulatory/bottleneck risk.** It stays constructive **onsite generation ([[BE]], INIO, SEI) and powered shells (WULF, HUT, CIFR, RIOT)**, and flags **"political risk to data center expansion from the executive branch if the Democratic Party wins" the '28 election.**

- ➤ **SAME FIRM, SAME DAY, SAME INDUSTRY VIEW LABEL, OPPOSITE ROTATION CALLS. Both should be carried, and anyone quoting "Morgan Stanley says" about AI rotation this week must say WHICH Morgan Stanley.**

**🔴🔴 2026-09-07 (primary deck folded 2026-09-09, /run-inbox) — THE NUMBERS UNDER MS’S ROTATION CALL: DATA-CENTER CAPEX +60% ($550bn) IN ’27 THEN +12% ($170bn) IN ’28, AND THE MECHANISM IS **“PRE-BUILD”** — WHICH REVERSES AT EVERY HYPERSCALER EXCEPT [[MSFT]].**

_Source for this whole sub-block: Morgan Stanley · Brian Nowak, CFA, with Stephen C Byrd / Adam Wood / Joseph Moore / Michelle M. Weaver / Ariana Salvatore / Lindsay A Tyler / Vishwanath Tirupattur / David Arcaro / Julian Herrera / Gregory Gao — “The Morgan Stanley AI Guidebook: Navigating the Age of Inference”, 2026-09-07, 99pp, Industry View Attractive. The qualitative half of this note is already logged immediately above (the fund-flow-rotation quote and the eight-debate list, folded by /wiki-ingest 2026-09-07); what follows is the PRIMARY DECK’s actual numbers, which were not._

- ⚠️ **TWO AGGREGATE FRAMINGS IN ONE DECK, BOTH QUOTED, DELIBERATELY NOT MERGED — THEY CARRY DIFFERENT LABELS.** (a) Key debate #1, verbatim: *“Forward Hyperscaler Capex: We see **DATA CENTER CAPEX** growing 60% ($550bn) in ‘27 but see a deceleration in capex growth going forward, with ~12% growth in ’28 ($170bn). Expect this (and accelerating hyperscaler/software revenue) to lead to investor fund rotation.”* (b) The capex section’s own “Our View” line: *“We see **HYPERSCALE CAPEX** growing ~$500bn y/y (60%) in ’27 reaching $1.5tn.”* ➤ **ARITHMETIC CHECK (ours, not MS’s): a $550bn increment at +60% implies a ~$917bn ’26 base and ~$1.47tn in ’27; a ~$500bn increment at +60% implies a ~$833bn base and ~$1.33tn — which does not reach the stated $1.5tn. The two framings are ADJACENT BUT NOT ARITHMETICALLY IDENTICAL, and MS never reconciles them in the deck. Carry both with their own labels; do not average them and do not net either against the other aggregates on this page.**
- **RECONCILIATION AGAINST WHAT THIS PAGE ALREADY CARRIES — AND IT IS A CONSTITUENT PROBLEM, NOT A REVISION.** MS’s **$1.5tn ’27 hyperscale** sits above **J.P. Morgan’s $1.40tn ’27 CSP** mark logged the same day (block above) and above the **~$1.4tn ’27 convergence** logged 08-31, and above **MS Global Credit’s own ~$1.4tn 2027 hyperscaler aggregate** (08-20, constituents AMZN/GOOGL/META/MSFT/[[ORCL]]/[[SPCX]]). ⚠️ **The Guidebook’s per-name exhibits cover only AMZN/GOOGL/META/MSFT — no ORCL, no SPCX — so its “hyperscale” basket is NARROWER than the credit desk’s while its total is HIGHER. Establish the constituent list before treating any of these as a revision to another.**
- **THE PER-NAME TABLE — DATA-CENTER CAPEX, MS ESTIMATES (narrative bullets on the four company slides, NOT chart values):**

| Name | ’26 DC capex | ’27 growth | ’28 growth | ’26 capex that is “pre-build” | Pre-build in ’27 | Pre-build in ’28 |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|---|---|
| [[GOOG]] | $205bn | **+83%** | +7% | **~5%** (lowest) | **grows ~6x, reaching $61bn** | shrinks (“like AMZN”) |
| [[AMZN]] | $180bn | +50% | +6% | ~38% | +20% (rising by $14bn) | **−35% (or $30bn)** |
| [[MSFT]] | $175bn | +43% | **+20%** | ~37% | +14% (or $36bn) | **+8% (or $24bn) — still growing** |
| [[META]] | $145bn | +55% | +11% | ~32% | **−19% (or $9bn)** | “minimal” |

- ➤ **THE PRE-BUILD REVERSAL *IS* THE ’28 DECELERATION CALL, AND IT IS THE MOST REUSABLE THING IN THE DECK.** MS defines pre-build in its own words: *“We estimate a substantial portion (in some cases 30-40%) of ‘26 capex is being spent on LONG-DATED LAND, BUILDINGS, EQUIPMENT, POWER for data centers to open in ‘27/‘28/‘29. Hyperscalers are building further out for STRATEGIC reasons (being able to light up compute as it comes available) and POLITICAL reasons (getting ahead of potential further political backlash against data center builds if the Executive branch changes parties). While forward building will continue, THERE ARE LIMITS AS TO HOW FAR OUT THESE PRE-BUILDS WILL GO.”* So MS’s ’28 slowdown is **not a demand call and not a saturation call** — it is one line item, bought years ahead, ceasing to grow and in most cases contracting. ⚠️ **THE PRACTICAL CONSEQUENCE FOR THIS PAGE: a ’28 capex-growth deceleration of this construction is NOT evidence of AI demand cooling, and should not be quoted as such. It is also the reason MSFT screens best on ’28 growth (+20%) — Microsoft is the only one of the four whose pre-build keeps growing, so its ’28 is a TIMING artefact, not a demand differential.**
- **MS’s three OTHER reasons the growth slows, and its two ways of being wrong — both worth carrying because this page has the level debate but not the mechanism debate.** Also slowing it: (1) **bottom-up chip/rack limits** — MS builds capex from chip and rack counts and *“there are limits on the numbers of chips that are built/available to purchase”*; (2) **physical limits** (labour, materials, power) *“holdi ng back further substantial growth off of already large numbers”* [sic, extraction]; (3) **[[META]] optionality** — if META leverages AWS/Azure to scale its models and API *“as it did in the past with Llama”*, that *“would lower long-term compute needs and potentially put downward pressure on ‘28 capex”*, and **META has $279bn of leases yet to be commenced (much of which is for powered shells), “which will bias downwards the long-dated portion of capex.”** Where MS could be wrong: **component inflation** (memory, energy) staying high, with next-gen chips *“also more expensive”*; and **larger-than-expected ’29 shipments**, which *“could lead to more prebuild in ‘27/‘28.”*
- **THE CAPACITY LEG (debate #2), which is the physical counterpart of the dollars.** *“We see the capex spend leading to ~80 GW of capacity coming online in ‘27/‘28 (38 GW/44 GW)… we reach ~145 GW of TOTAL COMPUTE CAPACITY IN ‘28, UP ~4X FROM ‘25”* (~35 GW in ’25). Ranking: **GOOGL adds the most capacity in ’27/’28 (~9 GW/~11 GW), “followed by AMZN, MSFT and META”**, on Gemini training, GCP growth and GenAI in Search/YouTube. Mix: **custom ASICs go from 34% of incremental compute (~2 GW) in ’25 to 66% (~17 GW) by ’28**, with **GOOGL TPU alone bringing on ~15 GW in ’27/’28**; *“GOOGL’s TPU and AMZN’s Trainium lead this shift”*, while **“META and MSFT: ASIC and AMD deployments ramp over time, with >50% of incremental ‘27 capacity (internally owned) still NVDA.”** AMZN is *“ramping NVDA capacity in ‘26/‘27 to meet AWS demands, while also forward selling Trn3/Trn4 1-2 years out”* (majority for AWS); ~50% of GOOGL’s ’26/’27 additions are for Google Cloud. ⚠️ **EXTRACTION DECODE, STATED SO IT CAN BE CHECKED RATHER THAN TRUSTED: this PDF drops the glyphs M, N and D inside certain bold runs (“A Z” = AMZN, “ ETA” = META, “ SFT” = MSFT). These two bullets read “Ramping  V A capacity” and “ASIC and A   deployments” in the raw extraction, which decode to NVDA and AMD under the same rule — and the slide’s own chart legend, “Other ASIC + AMD”, prints INTACT on the same page and corroborates the second. Decoded, not guessed; re-check against the final PDF.**
- ⚠️ **THE PER-GW UNIT ECONOMICS IN THIS DECK ARE A RE-PUBLICATION, NOT NEW WORK — RECORDED SO NOBODY COUNTS THEM TWICE.** They are MS’s 2026-07-27 *“The Paths to 25-50% GenAI ROIC”* frameworks (already carried in full on [[AMZN]] and [[MSFT]]), restated inside the Guidebook with only cosmetic drift (**IT depreciation $5bn → $4.6bn per GW; total opex relabelled $7.6bn → “~$8bn” per GW; the 3P case rounded $40.5bn → “~$41bn” per GW**). The three cases, each with its OWN base: **(a) HYPERSCALER MONETISING RENTED GB300 CAPACITY — ~$23bn/GW of revenue, $8bn/GW of operating costs (including $4.6bn of IT depreciation) → ~$15bn/GW incremental EBIT at ~67% incremental margin → ~$12bn/GW NOPAT, ~30% ROIC** (the slide heading says ~31%; the bullet says ~30% — quoted as printed). **(b) MODEL BUILDER SERVING APIs ON ITS OWN INFRASTRUCTURE — ~$30bn/GW of revenue, the same $8bn/GW of operating costs → ~$23bn/GW EBIT at ~75% → ~$18bn/GW NOPAT, ~46% ROIC.** **(c) THE SAME MODEL BUILDER ON 3P/RENTED INFRASTRUCTURE — ~$41bn/GW of revenue less $28bn/GW of RENTAL COSTS → ~$13bn/GW EBIT → ~$10bn/GW NOPAT (~25% ROIC per the slide heading).** MS states how (b) is derived: *“we sensitize token throughput and pricing per million tokens to arrive at $30bn/GW in base case revenue”*, after allowing that some GB300 capacity goes to training and so earns no inference revenue. ⚠️ **BASE DISCIPLINE: (a) and (b) are REVENUE per GW to different owners of the stack; (c)’s $28bn is a COST OF RENTING a GW; none of them is a BUILD COST per GW, a CHIP-CONTENT per GW, or a COST-TO-RUN per GW. Four bases now circulate on this wiki and they must never be netted or chained.**
- 🔴🔴 **A GENUINE CROSS-SOURCE CONVERGENCE ON $30bn/GW, LOGGED EXPLICITLY — AND THE ONE PLACE IT DOES *NOT* HOLD.** MS’s **$30bn/GW base-case REVENUE**, derived bottom-up by sensitizing **token throughput × price per million tokens**, is independently matched by a SUPPLIER CEO the following day: **[[AVGO]]’s Hock Tan told the Goldman Sachs Communacopia + Technology Conference on 2026-09-08 that “one gigawatt could generate $30 billion ARR”** (BBG **INITIAL DRAFT** transcript, slug `AVGO_GS_08-09-26`; see [[AVGO]]). **A sell-side bottom-up model and a merchant-silicon CEO landing on the same $30bn of annual revenue per gigawatt, from opposite ends of the stack, is real corroboration of the revenue side of the AI unit economics — the first this page has had.** ⚠️ **THREE CAVEATS, ALL MATERIAL.** (1) **PROVENANCE — the “within 24 hours” reading overstates it: MS’s $30bn/GW is NOT new on 09-07. It first published on 2026-07-27 and is re-stated here, so it was ~6 weeks old and already public when Hock spoke. The convergence is INDEPENDENT (different method, different vantage point) but it is NOT SIMULTANEOUS.** (2) **THE OPEX BASES DIFFER AND NOTHING BRIDGES THEM: MS says $8bn/GW of OPERATING COSTS (including $4.6bn of IT depreciation) inside a model-API P&L; Hock says the cost to run a gigawatt is “about $10 billion a year.” Adjacent, not identical — different scopes, different accounting, no reconciliation in either source. DO NOT MERGE THEM, and do not compute a margin by pairing one’s revenue with the other’s cost.** (3) **THE TRANSCRIPT IS AN INITIAL DRAFT and will be superseded by a FINAL.**
- **THE TAM (debate #4), for the record and with its split:** **$50-$60tn**, comprising **a $20-$30tn opportunity in global KNOWLEDGE-WORK spend to be digitized and augmented through GenAI tools**, plus **~$30tn of CONSUMER spend** *“(across retail, travel, autonomous, advertising, etc) to be digitized.”* ⚠️ **This is a TAM, not a revenue forecast, and MS reaches it by analogy (“hyperscaler and Facebook adoption curves”). It belongs on this page only as the demand-side justification MS offers for the capex, not as a number to model against.**
- **THE CREDIT LEG (debate #7) — A FIFTH “AI FINANCING” TOTAL ON THIS PAGE, AND THE PERIMETER IS DIFFERENT AGAIN.** Verbatim: *“Despite **~$450bn of AI-related debt issuance globally year-to-date**, we think there’s still significant capacity to absorb additional issuance, especially from high-quality hyperscalers. Rather than constraining issuance, THE MARKET ADJUSTMENT IS LIKELY TO COME THROUGH WIDER SPREADS. Hyperscaler ‘27/‘28 OCF is also expected to be **7-8X LARGER THAN REQUIRED DEBT**.”* Separately: **AMZN, GOOGL, META and MSFT “begin to monetize newly built capacity and generate over $1tn of OCF in 2028.”** ⚠️ **PERIMETER WARNING, CONSISTENT WITH THE FOUR ALREADY LOGGED (08-21): MS Internet’s ~$450bn is “AI-related debt issuance GLOBALLY” and is NOT the same measure as Barclays’ ~$338bn all-asset-class YTD (08-20), MS Credit’s $271bn IG YTD (08-20), GS’s $194bn five-name hyperscaler count (25 July) or JPM’s $53bn IG 144A project finance (07-07). Five perimeters, five numbers. Do not stack them, and do not read a rise from $338bn to $450bn as new issuance.** ✅ **Directionally it CORROBORATES the credit blocks below rather than contradicting them — the same “absorbable, but through wider spreads” conclusion MS Global Credit reached on 08-20.**
- **The remaining debates, completing the set already listed above.** **#3 GenAI ROIC:** *“We paint paths to 25-50% GenAI ROIC across hyperscalers and models with APIs. The highest returns accrue to THE MODEL LAYER RUNNING ON TOP OF OWNED INFRASTRUCTURE ([[META]], [[GOOG]], [[SPCX]]).”* **#5 Adoption signal:** *“We focus on diffusion across the economy. So far, we see most signal within the TECH sector, followed by FINANCIALS, HEALTHCARE and INDUSTRIALS.”* **#6 Open-weights:** *“Leading cost to serve will matter through the age of inference… open-weights and lower-cost models are critical to driving GenAI adoption across the economy. These will pressure leading labs to innovate to capture share of inference spend, while also reenforcing the value of THE HYPERSCALER CONCIERGE LAYER (AWS Bedrock, MSFT Foundry, GOOGL Vertex) that optimizes compute efficiency by task.”* **#8 Regulatory/bottlenecks:** state and local pushback to data centres *“largely manageable”*, with the **’28 election** the political risk and **time to market on power** *“a significant bottleneck, which leaves us constructive on power suppliers.”*
- ⚠️ **HYGIENE, LOGGED SO IT IS NOT UNDONE.** (1) **Every figure above comes from a NARRATIVE bullet or a quoted sentence. Nothing was taken from a chart region** — the deck’s ROIC and OCF exhibits carry bar values that this page deliberately does not use. (2) **ONE INTERNAL INCONSISTENCY, LOGGED NOT RESOLVED:** a later “mega-cap” comparison exhibit marks **“Infrastructure Capex”** at **GOOGL $205bn/$375bn** and **META $145bn/$225bn** for ’26E/’27E — both of which reconcile exactly to the +83% and +55% growth rates above — but marks **AMZN at $140bn/$170bn**, which does NOT reconcile to $180bn/+50%. ## 🆕🔴 2026-09-06 — **CHINA'S CSP CAPEX INFLECTS (+105% Y/Y, +95% Q/Q IN 2Q26) BUT ITS CAPEX INTENSITY IS STILL ONLY 25% VS 33% IN THE US — AND THE WHOLE BRIDGE RESTS ON AN *ASSUMED* 200K H200. SEPARATELY, THE BIGGEST ODM PRINTS AUGUST AT +52.0% Y/Y AND SAYS Q3 VISIBILITY HAS IMPROVED.**

- ⚠️⚠️ **SOURCE CLASS FIRST, BECAUSE IT GOVERNS HOW MUCH WEIGHT THE CHINA BLOCK CAN CARRY: it is **Jefferies Asia TMT SPECIALIST SALES · Conor O'Mara / "Edison" — "JEF Asia morning TMT ideas", 2026-09-06. SALES-DESK COMMENTARY, NOT RESEARCH** — the note itself states it *"is not a product of Jefferies Research Department"*. No rating, no price target, no house model.
- 🔴 **THE LEVEL AND THE RATE, VERBATIM: *"Chinese CSPs' **2Q capex jumped 105%/95% YoY/QoQ**, driven likely by strong inference demand and the opportunities to buy NVDA H200. In the last 4 quarters, **Chinese CSPs' capex rose only 30% YoY vs 76% for US CSPs**. Despite the lumpy jump, Chinese CSPs' **2Q26 capex intensity (capex to sales) is only 25%, vs US CSPs at 33%**."*** ➤ **The headline growth rate and the intensity point in OPPOSITE directions, and the intensity is the more durable read: China's jump is LUMPY off a four-quarter run-rate that trails the US by ~46pts, and China is still spending 8pts less of its revenue than the US names.** ⚠️ **BASIS WARNING: capex-to-SALES is a different denominator from the capex-to-OPERATING-CASH-FLOW (>90% both regions) and capex-to-NON-GAAP-EARNINGS (>100% both regions) marks this page already carries from Jefferies · Thomas Chong ("AI Series #67", 2026-08-24). The three ratios are not interchangeable and must not be quoted against each other.**
- ⚠️⚠️ **THE BRIDGE — AND ITS LOAD-BEARING ASSUMPTION, WHICH IS NOT A DISCLOSED NUMBER: *"2Q26 BAT's capex rose **~US$9bn QoQ**… **If we assume China has bought 200K H200 (= 25K HGX H200 servers) in 2Q26, it would cost an est. US$8bn to US$9bn (~US$350K/server)**. Assuming Bytedance accounts for 30% of the purchase, the amount attributable to BAT could be roughly **US$5bn-6bn, which would be 55%-65% of the QoQ capex increase**. The remaining **US$3bn-4bn would be spending on local chips**. We believe **Huawei is the biggest beneficiary** of BAT's stepped-up efforts to buy local chips."*** 🔴 **THE 200K H200 IS JEFFERIES' OWN ASSUMPTION USED TO BACK INTO THE BRIDGE — explicitly labelled "if we assume", not a reported, disclosed or corroborated figure. Every number downstream of it INHERITS it: the US$5-6bn BAT attribution, the 55-65% share of the q/q capex increase, the US$3-4bn local-chip residual, and therefore the "Huawei is the biggest beneficiary" conclusion. If the 200K is wrong, the local-chip residual moves one-for-one against it.**
- ⚠️⚠️ **UNIT BASIS, STATED EXPLICITLY PER THIS PAGE'S STANDING HAZARD: the ~US$350K is an ASSUMED ALL-IN PRICE PER HGX H200 SERVER SYSTEM. It is NOT a per-GPU price, NOT vendor revenue per GW, and NOT a datacentre build cost per GW or per MW. It must never be netted against, or chained into, any of the per-GW / per-MW series already on this page.**
- 🔴 **AND IT LEAVES AN UNRECONCILED CONTRADICTION AGAINST COMPANY PRIMARY, LOGGED HERE AND ON [[NVDA]]: NVIDIA's own Q2 FY27 CFO commentary states China Data Center Hopper shipments were *"less than 1% of Data Center revenue"* in the same calendar quarter, with **no** China DC compute revenue assumed in the outlook. A US$8-9bn China H200 purchase in 2Q26 cannot be reconciled with that from anything on either page. Candidate reconciliations nobody has evidenced: different timing, channels whose revenue NVDA does not recognise (grey-market / resale / prior-period inventory), or the 200K assumption simply being wrong. **BOTH RECORDED; NEITHER ADOPTED.**

Do not treat it as an audited disclosure — but it is specific, attributed and quantified, which is more than the bear case has.** Cross-ref [[MSFT]], where the same quote is filed alongside the 09-04 Azure $101.9bn TTM disclosure and its old-definition caveat.

- 🔴🔴 **THE ARGUMENT, VERBATIM, AND IT IS AN ARITHMETIC ONE: *"[[AVGO]] just guided AI Revenue to **double in FY27 — and then double again in FY28**. My guess is no. Certainly, the Street's remain too low.** Take [[AMZN]] for example. **The Street currently estimates FY27 AWS revenue growth to DECELERATE NEARLY −200bps TO +34% Y/Y IN 2027 DESPITE CAPEX RAMPING NEARLY +30% Y/Y (which is also almost certainly too low).** …in recent conversations with investors, **BULLISH AMZN AWS REVENUE GROWTH ESTIMATES NOW START WITH A '5'.**"*** (JPM · Mark Schilsky, US TMT specialist sales — **desk commentary, NOT research; no rating, no PT, no estimates**, 2026-09-04.) ➤ 🔴 **THE FALSIFIABLE VERSION, WHICH IS WHY THIS BELONGS ON A THEME PAGE RATHER THAN JUST ON [[AMZN]]: the desk's own test is *"if AWS continues to accelerate for the next several quarters, and **some of those quarters sport 50%+ growth rates**"*. **A printed AWS quarter above 50% is the check. The Street's FY27 number to beat is ~34%. The spread is ~16 points of growth on the largest single cloud revenue line in the world** — and the implicit claim is that the capex being modelled cannot produce only 34%.**
- 🔴🔴 **AND THE DISCLOSURE THAT MAKES THE OTHER SIDE OF THAT COMPARISON POSSIBLE FOR THE FIRST TIME: *"[[MSFT]]'s cloud unit **Azure's revenue was $101.9 BILLION in the twelve months ending in JUNE… UP 40%** from the year prior… Microsoft… revealed it **WILL START REPORTING AZURE'S REVENUE QUARTERLY**… The new accounting structure will make Microsoft's Azure disclosures **DIRECTLY COMPARABLE to similar revenue reported by [[AMZN]] and [[GOOG]].**"*** (The Information · Aaron Holmes, 2026-09-03.) ➤ **From F1Q27 this page can run all three hyperscaler cloud lines on comparable absolute revenue for the first time. ⚠️⚠️ **BUT NOT YET, AND NOT WITH THIS NUMBER: $101.9bn is on the OLD Azure definition — before GitHub cloud, other developer cloud services and Security Copilot move to M365 commercial cloud and Healthcare & Life Sciences moves to Industry solutions. **Do not use it as a growth base against what Microsoft reports from F1Q27.** Microsoft's own restated bridge (Azure ~45% cc → 44-45% cc; M365 commercial cloud ~15% → ~17% cc) is the only compliant span.**
- 🔴 **THE CHINA LEG OF THE SAME CAPEX CYCLE IS BEING REVISED UP, WITH A BUILD-COST BASIS ATTACHED: *"**ByteDance plans to add 5-6GW of data centre capacity in Ulanqab, Inner Mongolia, by 2028**… we believe this represents **INCREMENTAL demand rather than previously announced projects**, as ByteDance has already tendered roughly 2GW this year… **Using our estimate of RMB220bn of capex PER GW, a 5.5GW buildout would require roughly RMB1.15tn (US$190bn)**… our current **2028 demand forecast of 7GW appears increasingly conservative.**"*** (Jefferies · Edison via Asia TMT, 2026-09-03.) **ByteDance separately borrowed ~$30bn — its largest-ever loan, upsized from $20bn, the second-largest dollar-denominated loan in Asia this year after SoftBank's $40bn bridge** (Bloomberg via The Information, 2026-09-04). ⚠️⚠️ **RMB220bn/GW ≈ US$31bn/GW is a FULL BUILD COST. Never chain it to [[AVGO]]'s ~$13bn/GW of silicon content, to Hock's $20-30bn/GW of silicon-plus-systems, or to any $/MW REVENUE mark.**
- ⚠️ **THE REGULATORY ITEM THAT ATTACHES TO THE CLOUD LAYER ITSELF, AND IT IS NEW AND DATED: *"Brussels will **VERY LIKELY designate [[MSFT]] AZURE and [[AMZN]] WEB SERVICES under the DIGITAL MARKETS ACT THIS FALL**, following a preliminary decision in late June"* — part of a broader push to bring AI into the EU platform-regulation toolkit alongside the DSA designation of [[OPENAI]]'s ChatGPT. ⚠️ **The DMA is the heavier regime: it *"regulates companies' BUSINESS MODELS"*, unlike the DSA's risk-management and transparency duties. ## 🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-03 (source 2026-09-02) — **JEFFERIES PUTS CONSENSUS AT CY26E $818bn / CY27E $1,121bn FOR HYPERSCALERS + [[ORCL]] + [[META]] — AND THE "+73% / +123%" PRINTED ON THE SLIDE ARE **12-MONTH ESTIMATE REVISIONS, NOT CAPEX GROWTH.** THE SAME GROUP GOES **FCF-NEGATIVE IN AGGREGATE IN CY26E**, WHILE OPERATING CASH FLOW AND CLOUD MARGINS BOTH KEEP EXPANDING.**

**Source: Jefferies "AI Deep Dive: Addressing Key Debates Across Hyperscalers and Software" · Brent Thill (+ team), 58 pages, priced intraday 9-2-26.** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/AI_Deep_Dive_Jeffries.html)

⚠️⚠️ **EXTRACTION WARNING: this deck's PDF text layer pairs chart values with the wrong labels on several slides. Every figure below was read off the RENDERED PAGE IMAGE (`relatórios bons/_assets/AI_Deep_Dive_Jeffries/pNNN.jpg`) with the page number cited, not off the text extraction.**

### 🔴🔴🔴 (1) THE HEADLINE — AND THE MISREAD THAT IS BUILT INTO THE SLIDE (p13)

**"Capex Estimates for Hyperscalers + ORCL + META ($B)" — source Visible Alpha, i.e. SELL-SIDE CONSENSUS, not Jefferies' own model:**

| | Estimate as of 1YR AGO | Estimate TODAY | Printed on slide |
|---|--:|--:|--:|
| **CY26E** | **$473bn** | **$818bn** | **"+73%"** |
| **CY27E** | **$503bn** | **$1,121bn** | **"+123%"** |

- 🔴🔴 **THE GUARD, AND IT IS THE WHOLE POINT OF CARRYING THIS SLIDE: the +73% and +123% are the **12-MONTH REVISION IN THE ESTIMATE FOR THAT YEAR** — how much consensus for CY26 and CY27 has moved in a year. They are **NOT y/y capex growth.** Implied y/y growth from the "today" bars is CY26E → CY27E = **+37%**, a completely different number. Anyone quoting "+123% capex growth in 2027" off this deck is wrong by ~86 percentage points, and the slide's own title (*"The AI Race is Driving a Historic Capex Surge"*) invites exactly that error.**
- ➤ **Where the levels sit against everything else on this page: **$818bn CY26** sits essentially on top of [[NVDA]]'s own ~$800bn 2026 aggregate (08-26 block) but on a WIDER perimeter (this one includes ORCL and META explicitly). **$1,121bn CY27** sits BELOW MS's ~$1.4trn (08-20/08-31 blocks) and below Citi's >$1trn big-four-only series (08-29), and roughly at NVDA's own $1.3trn.** ⚠️ **Perimeter discipline as always: "hyperscalers + ORCL + META" is not the same set as MS's (which now includes [[SPCX]]) nor Citi's big four. The convergence around ~$1.1-1.4trn for CY27 is real; the precision is not.**

| Same group (hyperscalers + [[ORCL]] + [[META]]), $bn | CY24 | CY25 | CY26E |
|---|--:|--:|--:|
| **Total debt** (p14) | **$226** | **$319** | **$502** |
| **Total FCF** (p15) | **$238** | **$189** | **−$2** |

Every financing block on this page — GS's 35%-debt-financed-share path (08-19), the $300bn of 2027 project finance, the ~$3trn of off-balance-sheet obligations (08-17), the 300-600bp counterparty spread (08-20) — is downstream of this single crossing.**
- ⚠️ **BASIS NOTE: p15 is FCF (post-capex), p14 is gross debt. The −$2bn is an AGGREGATE across the group and therefore hides enormous per-name dispersion — it does not say any individual name is FCF-negative. Do not attribute it to a company.**

### ✅ (3) THE OFFSET JEFFERIES PUTS AGAINST IT — AND ALL THREE SERIES ARE STILL IMPROVING (p36, p35, p28)

| Total hyperscaler | 1Q25 | 2Q25 | 3Q25 | 4Q25 | 1Q26 | 2Q26 |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| **Operating cash flow, $bn** (p36) | **$90** | **$103** | **$129** | **$143** | **$119** | **$140** |
| **Cloud revenue growth, y/y** (p35) | **25%** | **28%** | **30%** | **35%** | **40%** | **49%** |
| **Cloud operating margin** (p28) | **36.3%** | **33.8%** | **35.9%** | **36.7%** | **37.4%** | **38.9%** |

- ⚠️⚠️ **READ THE OCF AND MARGIN ROWS OFF THE IMAGES, NOT OFF A SORTED LIST — BOTH SERIES ARE NON-MONOTONIC AND A TEXT DUMP RE-ORDERS THEM. OCF **falls** from $143bn in 4Q25 to $119bn in 1Q26 (seasonal) before recovering to $140bn; the margin series **starts at 36.3% in 1Q25, troughs at 33.8% in 2Q25**, and only then rises for four straight quarters. Jefferies' *"expanded for 4 straight quarters"* is measured from the 2Q25 trough, and the 2Q26 print of 38.9% is **+2.6pts above the 1Q25 starting point**, not +5.1pts.**
- **Jefferies stamps 2Q26 OCF as **+36% y/y** ($140bn vs $103bn) and uses the margin series as its answer to Debate #2 (rising memory prices vs AI ROIC) — cross-filed to [hbm-memory.md](hbm-memory.md). The supporting quote on the same slide is the pass-through mechanism: **[[CRWV]] CFO, 2Q26 call — *"We're also passing through component price increases."***
- ➤ **The bull framing that follows: cloud revenue growth **accelerated for five straight quarters to 49%** while margins expanded — i.e. the capex is being absorbed by an accelerating, margin-expanding revenue line, not by a flat one. That is the strongest single-page ROIC defence on this wiki, and it is worth noting it uses NO forecast: all six quarters are reported.**

Anyone treating the +70% as an unbiased point forecast is misreading the object. It cuts both ways: it supports "the number is a conservative floor," and it concedes "the number was chosen for effect."**

The two are not additive and must never be summed — hyperscaler capex BUYS the output that this capex builds the capacity for — but the ~$220bn forward number is the cleanest available check on whether the supply side is committing on a scale consistent with the demand aggregates on this page. It is.** ⚠️ *Foundry capex, not WFE: it includes shells, land and facilities, so it is not comparable to a shipped-WFE figure either.*
