(est.)" — a 50x gap** — with the **Musk framing that *"current global fabs produce ~2% of what Tesla/SpaceX will need for robotics + orbital AI."***
  - ⚠️⚠️ **UNIT DISCIPLINE, AND IT IS THE WHOLE POINT ON THIS PAGE: the "1 TW" is a STATED DEMAND AMBITION from an Elon Musk inauguration presentation, on a chart whose axis is GW. It is NOT a facility-GW figure, NOT an IT-load GW figure, and NOT a contracted or financed build. It must not be netted, compared or chained against ANY of the GW bases this page tracks (see `_meta/assumptions.md` — never mix GW bases). The comparable is the ~20 GW "industry availability" estimate printed beside it, and nothing else.**
  - ⚠️ **The deck's own sentence — *"targeting 1 TW/yr compute; ~70% of TSMC's current output"* — FUSES that compute-demand figure with a WAFER-OUTPUT ratio (the ~70% is the ratio for 1 million wafer starts per month, as [semicap-wfe.md](semicap-wfe.md) already documents from a separate source). Two different claims in one line; do not chain them.**
  - ➤ **NET: this is a NON-HYPERSCALER demand pool being offered as a floor under the buildout. Logged as an ARGUMENT, not a datapoint — Stifel explicitly declines to size Terafab in dollars, and this wiki still carries Morgan Stanley modelling only ~$2bn of SpaceX spend in 2028 against Mizuho treating Terafab as a material WFE offset. The debate is unresolved and this note does not resolve it.**
- ⚠️ **AND THE RISK STATED FROM THE OTHER SIDE, WHICH IS THIS PAGE'S CENTRAL QUESTION SEEN FROM THE FOUNDRY: Stifel's third listed risk to its TSM thesis is *"AI CapEx Digestion & Customer Concentration: **a hyperscaler pause would hit the AI-accelerator CAGR**"* — against a TSMC long-term model of **~25% revenue CAGR with AI-accelerator revenue at a mid-to-high-50s% CAGR (2024-29)**, a **through-cycle gross-margin floor lifted to 56%+**, and **2026 revenue guided +30%+ in USD**, all built on a **$265bn US buildout (Arizona GIGAFAB cluster)**. Stifel also notes that *"mgmt frames expansion as **customer-driven**, [though] TSMC has historically invested in PP&E during **upcycles**"* — i.e. the supply side is not waiting for the orders. ➤ **A mid-to-high-50s% accelerator CAGR out to 2029 is a strictly stronger demand assumption than any hyperscaler capex path on this page implies, and it is underwritten by capex the foundry is spending ahead of the order book. That asymmetry is where a "capex digestion" year would bite first — at the manufacturer, not at the buyer.**
- **Operationally, Stifel's own watch-list ties the two sides together explicitly: *"TSM & ASML bookings are key, alongside hyperscaler capex"*, with **ECOC the first optical read**, **OCP and SEMICON West framing CPO demand**, and **IEDM previewing the A16/A14 and High-NA roadmap.**

## 🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-01 — [[NVDA]]'s OWN NUMBERS FINALLY SPLIT THE DEMAND: **HYPERSCALE ~$49bn (+102% y/y) vs ACI&E ~$40bn (+138% y/y)** — THE NON-HYPERSCALER CHANNEL IS GROWING ~21pts FASTER. PLUS THE SUPPLY CONSTRAINT IS SIZED FOR THE FIRST TIME AT **~30pts OF FOREGONE GROWTH**.

**(Barclays · Raimo Lenschow, *"rAImo's AI Newsletter #125: Unpacking NVIDIA Earnings Readthroughs"*, relayed by Barclays desk · Jeffrey Rand, 2026-09-01)**

- 🔴🔴 **THE SPLIT THIS PAGE HAS NEVER HAD, AND IT REFRAMES THE CONCENTRATION BEAR: *"of NVIDIA's **$89bn in total data center revenue (~18% q/q, ~117% y/y)**, approximately **$49bn came from HYPERSCALE revenue (~13% q/q, ~102% y/y)**, and **~$40bn (~25% q/q, ~138% y/y) came from NVDA's ACI&E segment, which includes NEOCLOUD, INDUSTRIAL AND ENTERPRISE customers.** We highlight the increasingly balanced data center contribution with a growing share of DC revenue coming from the ACI&E segment, which grew **~5pts and ~21pts FASTER on a q/q and y/y basis** respectively, compared to the hyperscaler segment. To us, this highlights **NVIDIA's growing reliance on NON-HYPERSCALER CUSTOMERS as it looks to DIVERSIFY its customer base.**"*
- ➤ 🔴🔴 **WHY THIS MATTERS FOR A *HYPERSCALER CAPEX* PAGE: the hyperscalers are now **~55% of the largest AI-silicon revenue pool, not ~80-90%**, and their share is FALLING. Any model on this page that treats big-four capex as the sole driver of AI silicon demand is now measurably incomplete — the non-hyperscaler half is compounding faster. ⚠️ It also cuts the other way for credit: the marginal buyer is increasingly a NON-INVESTMENT-GRADE neocloud, which is exactly what the WSJ flagged about the [[ANTHROPIC]]/Lambda structure (*"Nvidia is quietly underwriting the infrastructure behind AI's biggest compute deals, **one non-investment-grade customer at a time**"*).**
- 🔴 **THE CONSTRAINT, SIZED: *"mgmt called out that demand is accelerating, and **customers' forecasts suggest to NVDA's growth DOUBLING next year (FY28)**, but given the supply constraints (which are expected to persist **until AT LEAST FY28 END**), NVDA expects to grow **~70%**. Hence, this suggests **growth would be ~30pts HIGHER absent the constraints.**"* ➤ **~30 points of foregone growth is the cleanest available measure of unmet demand in the whole complex — and it is management-derived rather than analyst-modelled. It is also the number that says 2027 capex is supply-set, not demand-set.**
- **AND THE CAPACITY DENOMINATOR: neocloud partners are *"bringing online capacity **FASTER and at a LOWER TOKEN COST**, expected to **exit the year with ~8 GWs in total installed capacity, up from ~3 GWs at the end of 2025**."* ✅ **NVDA vouching for neocloud execution *against* the hyperscalers is the allocation argument, stated by the party that controls the allocation.**
- **THE PER-GW LADDER, WITH THE GUARD ATTACHED: *"[NVDA's] revenue opportunity has grown from approximately **$18bn per GW (Hopper) to $25bn with Blackwell and $40bn with Vera Rubin**, which also now spans **Vera CPU, Rubin GPU, NVLink, InfiniBand or Ethernet and Groq LPU** (language processing unit)… these disclosures should help investors **refine assumptions around the COST SIDE of the unit economics for the hyperscalers/neoclouds.**"* ⚠️⚠️ **UNIT GUARD, MANDATORY PER THIS WIKI'S STANDING RULE: this is NVDA **REVENUE** per GW. It is NOT total build cost per GW (~$30bn five years ago → ~$60bn today on this wiki's separate series) and NOT the compute-contract revenue per MW below. **THREE DISTINCT BASES — never chain them.**
- **MARGIN PATH AND THE TOKEN-ECONOMICS OFFSET: gross margins *"compress to **~71-72% (from ~75% in Q2)** before returning to **~72-73% in FY28 as price increases take effect in 1Q28**"* on memory-led component inflation — with the offset that **Vera Rubin has *"30x HIGHER THROUGHPUT PER MW and 35x LOWER TOKEN COST relative to Grace Blackwell Ultra."*** ➤ **Barclays' framing is the right one for this page: NVDA absorbing some component inflation plus a 35x token-cost improvement are *"two relative POSITIVES for the compute players"* — i.e. the hyperscalers' cost per unit of useful output is falling even as the price per GW rises.**

**(MS Public Policy Strategist · Ariana Salvatore, 2026-09-01, via MS desk · Tom Wigg)**

- **THE FRAME: *"**Data center political pushback remains the KEY POLICY RISK to the AI infrastructure buildout**, but MS still views it primarily as a **STATE + LOCAL constraint rather than an imminent FEDERAL capex threat.** Federal policy remains supportive… **MS analysts expect hyperscaler capex to EXCEED $1T next year.** The more likely consequence is a **'CONDITIONAL BUILDOUT' rather than broad capex destruction — data centers CONTINUE TO GET BUILT, but increasingly MIGRATE TOWARD JURISDICTIONS WILLING TO ACCOMMODATE THEM.**"* **Timing: *"the **2026 MIDTERMS are an important SIGNAL, not necessarily the inflection point**… **material federal policy risk is more likely AFTER THE 2028 ELECTIONS.**"*
- 🔴🔴 **THE HARD ATTRITION NUMBERS, WHICH ARE NET-NEW TO THIS PAGE: *"**EXC reduced 'high-probability' Datacenter demand ~40% to 11 GW**, while **AEP Ohio pipeline fell >50%** after Ohio introduced rules including **connection-study fees of up to $100K.** In PA, **>100 data centers have been proposed, but ONLY 20 HAVE APPLIED for necessary permits.** Most of those proposals apparently **LACK SECURED POWER OR END-CUSTOMER.** However, even after rooting out the more 'speculative' projects, **there is still a VERY REAL STRAIN ON THE GRID, per PJM.**"* ➤ ⚠️ **READ IT PRECISELY: this is SPECULATIVE pipeline being culled, not funded hyperscaler projects being cancelled. A 100-proposals-to-20-permits ratio measures how much of the ANNOUNCED pipeline was never real — which is reassuring for utility planning and disqualifying for anyone sizing TAM off announcements. Cross-filed `themes/ai-datacenter-power.md`, where the full political marker set sits.**
- **THE MITIGATION LINE IS NOW ITEMISED AND IT IS A REAL COST: *"Hyperscalers are already responding with **direct community/political mitigation spending: RATEPAYER PROTECTIONS and SELF-SUPPLIED GENERATION, [[GOOG]]'s WATER FRAMEWORK, [[AMZN]]'s new GLOBAL DC WATER-USE DISCLOSURE, and [[META]]'s $1B HOST-COMMUNITY FUND.**"*
- 🔴 **AND THE CAPEX-VS-CLOUD-REVENUE CHECK, WHICH IS THE ONE THIS PAGE SHOULD KEEP RUNNING: US hyperscale cloud revenue momentum **accelerated from +23% y/y in 2Q25A to +43% y/y in 2Q26A** (AWS/[[MSFT]]/GCP combined, per JPM · Jay Kwon, 08-31) — against Bloomberg's **+43% 2027E gross capex growth** for the big four. ➤ **Revenue growth and capex growth are running at the SAME rate (~43%). That is the single most reassuring line item on this page: the build is not yet outrunning the monetisation. ⚠️ NVDA's own FY28 framework of +70% is *"well ABOVE"* both — which is either NVDA taking share of the capex dollar, or a warning that its guide requires capex to re-accelerate beyond what the hyperscalers have signalled.**
- **[[MSFT]] SUPPLIES THE BEST SINGLE BACKLOG MARK: **RPO ~$700bn, +84% y/y**, with Azure growth *"accelerated to 43% this qtr and guide for 45%"* and **M365 Copilot paid seats at 30M with net additions more than doubling q/q** — and MSFT *"committed to POSITIVE FCF in '27"*, which BofA notes is not true of every hyperscaler (*"there are obviously other hyperscalers with LESS, OR NO, FCF"*) (BofA · Tal Liani, PO to $600 on 28x CY27, 2026-09-01).
- ⚠️ **THE FINANCING-STRUCTURE ITEM TO KEEP WATCHING, because it is how capex is being moved OFF hyperscaler balance sheets: *"**The $5.5 Billion Perk SoftBank's Data-Center Venture Offered.** Ahead of its IPO, **SB Energy handed its main frontier tenant $5.5bn in WARRANTS to lock it in as an anchor tenant — even though the company has ZERO DATA CENTERS OPERATING and a $3.2bn NET LOSS.** Concerns persist on circularity for the Ohio megasite"* (WSJ, via JPM, 09-01). ➤ **Warrants to an anchor tenant is vendor financing in equity form. Together with [[NVDA]] holding leases, [[GOOG]] guaranteeing Hut 8's debt, and the [[AVGO]] XPV structure, this page now has FOUR distinct off-balance-sheet mechanisms funding "hyperscaler" capacity that will not appear in hyperscaler capex. Any capex aggregate on this page understates the build by whatever these total.**

## 🆕🔴🔴 2026-08-31 — THE 2027 CAPEX NUMBER CONVERGES AT ~$1.4trn ACROSS HOUSE AND BUY-SIDE — AND THE BOND MARKET STARTS CHARGING FOR IT

- 🔴🔴 **J.P. MORGAN'S HOUSE SERIES, PUBLISHED IN FULL: *"JPM's tech team now estimate **total CSP capex of $950bn for 26 (+94% y/y), $1.40tr for 27 (+47% y/y), and $1.55tr for 28 (+11% y/y)**… implying (an acceleration to) **42% h/h growth in capex in 2H26**"* (J.P. Morgan APAC Thematics · Matthew See, 2026-08-31).**
- 🔴🔴 **AND THE BUY-SIDE IS IN THE SAME PLACE, MEASURED INDEPENDENTLY: *"**investors now assuming 2027 hyperscaler capex will reach $1.2–$1.5t, up from ~$800b in 2026**"* (Deutsche Bank · Melissa Weathers et al., 2026 DB Technology Conference feedback, 2026-08-31).** ➜ **JPM's $1.40trn sits dead centre of the buy-side range. **This is one of the few numbers in the AI complex where the sell-side house view and the surveyed buy-side view agree — which means 2027 capex is NOT where the disagreement is. The disagreement is entirely about 2028+ (JPM decelerates hard to +11%) and about what the money buys.**
- ⚠️⚠️ **BASIS GUARD, AND IT IS NOW SHARPER THAN WHEN THIS WIKI FIRST FLAGGED IT ON 08-31: J.P. Morgan's memory team separately carries *"memory value share within CSP hardware capex (using **OMDIA** estimates)… **$669bn (31%) in 26E → $991bn (49%) in 27E**"* — percentages that imply a total base **FALLING** from ~$2.16trn to ~$2.02trn. **That is the OPPOSITE DIRECTION to J.P. Morgan's own tech-team series above, which has capex RISING 47%. Two JPM series, same firm, same day, incompatible implied bases.** **The dollars are usable; the SHARES are not, and the OMDIA "CSP hardware capex" base is a different and much broader quantity than "total CSP capex". Never difference them.**
- 🔴 **DEMAND HAS BROADENED BEYOND THE BIG FOUR, AND DB NAMES THE NEW BUYERS: *"demand has **broadened beyond hyperscalers alone, with NEOCLOUDS, ENTERPRISES, SOVEREIGN AI INITIATIVES, AND EMERGING AI LABS all ramping spending and becoming an increasingly important part of the demand landscape**. As such, **demand is not the limiting factor; rather, MEMORY and, to some extent, POWER are the primary bottlenecks**."* **Evidence the same week: [[AMZN]]/Humain 50MW in Saudi (Dec-2026), [[AMD]]/[[CSCO]]/Humain +250MW from 2027 toward 1GW by 2030, [[SPCX]]'s $6.7bn of early-3Q contracted cloud revenue with [[GOOG]] and [[ANTHROPIC]] named, and [[DELL]]'s AI-server estimate raised ~10% *"reflecting industrywide order momentum among NEOCLOUD providers"* (UBS).**
- 🔴🔴 **THE BOND MARKET IS NOW REPRICING THIS, AND IT IS THE MOST UNDER-COVERED ANGLE ON THIS PAGE (J.P. Morgan Asia Pacific **CREDIT** Research · Alvin Au / Soo Chong Lim / Natalie Leung, "Asia TMT: Preparing for DC Supply Shocks", 2026-08-31).** ⚠️ **Credit desk — every rating in it is a BOND rating, not an equity one.**
  - **Supply: APAC (ex-China) DC capacity *"will expand **~3x to 53GW by 2030E**"*, generating *"**US$95bn of APAC (ex-China) DC bond supply over 2027-30E**"* and lifting annual APAC IG bond supply by **~10%** for four years.**
  - **Price: APAC DC deals *"would have to be priced **~200bp wider** than Asian TMT names"*, and APAC DCs carry a yield premium over US DCs (7%) *"due to their potentially weaker structure and higher geopolitical risk exposure."***
  - 🔴 **The spread damage is already visible in the US: *"**US hyperscalers widen (over 40bps YTD, vs JULI: +6bps) due to HEAVY AI BOND SUPPLY**"*, and **US Tech now trades 50-165bps WIDER than Asia TMT**.**
  - **The Chinese hyperscalers are the balance-sheet casualties: **Alibaba** net cash Rmb379bn (4Q24) → **Rmb208bn (2Q26)**, capex +1.5x q/q in 2Q26, FCF **−Rmb45bn**, an HK$80bn August placement, and *"funding deficit… **Rmb100bn/year**, the largest among Chinese hyperscalers"* (JPM's China Internet analyst Alex Yao, on FY27E capex of Rmb200bn). **Tencent** capex **+60% q/q to Rmb59bn**, FCF −Rmb14bn, net cash −60% q/q to Rmb58bn. **Baidu** *"earnings have been declining since 2Q24 as **AI SEARCH CANNIBALIZES THE TRADITIONAL SEARCH BUSINESS**"* — a read-through worth carrying to [[GOOG]] as the only clean natural experiment on that question.**
  - ➜ **THE CONCLUSION FOR THIS THEME: the marginal AI datacentre is increasingly funded in the bond market, and the bond market has started charging a spread for it — 40bps at the US hyperscalers already, ~200bp demanded on APAC DC paper. **Capex forecasts of $1.4trn assume that financing stays available at a price. The credit curve is the earliest observable place where that assumption breaks.**

### ⚠️ (2) WHERE THIS SERIES DISAGREES WITH THE HOUSES ALREADY ON THIS PAGE — and the [[META]] gap is the one to trade

- 🔴🔴 **META 2027E: CITI $205.3bn vs ROTHSCHILD & CO REDBURN $142.2bn — a ~$63bn / ~44% GAP on the same year, and the widest single-name capex disagreement this wiki carries.** Redburn's own note says the buy-side bar is *"over $200bn in 2027"*, and Citi's $205.3bn sits almost exactly on that bar — i.e. **Citi's model IS the buy-side consensus Redburn is arguing against**, which makes the pair a clean, dated, falsifiable divergence rather than two loose estimates. Wells Fargo (Gawrelski, 07-09) independently reads market expectation as *"roughly $200B."* ➤ **Filed to [wiki-edge](../_meta/edge.md): the META 2027 capex question now has a bull mark ($205bn, Citi/buy-side), a bear mark ($142bn, Redburn) and a stated resolution mechanism (Meta's own 2027 guide).**
- **GOOG is modelled as the LARGEST spender of the Big Four in both 2026E and 2027E ($204.5bn / $321.6bn)** — ahead of MSFT and AMZN. That is consistent with the Wells Fargo read logged on [[GOOG]] that *"only Google will deliver more than 7GW of capacity in '27, powered by TPU capacity."* **Two independent sources, different methods, same conclusion: Google is the capacity leader in 2027.**
- ⚠️ **The "Other (incl. RoW)" line is the one to treat with most caution: it steps from $16.4bn (2027E) to $190.1bn (2028E) to $478.7bn (2029E). That is a modelled RoW ramp with no disclosed build, and it accounts for most of the difference between the Total US Public and Total lines in the out-years. Do not quote the 2029 total without it.**
- **[[CRWV]] is modelled as FLATTENING — $34.0bn (2026E) → $35.7bn → $37.5bn → $39.4bn — i.e. Citi has the largest listed neocloud growing ~5%/yr after 2026, while the hyperscalers compound at 20-50%. ➤ An implicit call that neocloud capex share PEAKS in 2026; worth testing against CoreWeave's own contracted backlog.**
- **SpaceX (SPCX) is modelled at $94.7bn of AI capex in 2027E rising to $246.3bn by 2029E — larger than [[META]]'s 2027 line. ⚠️ Flagged rather than endorsed: SPCX has no BBG consensus line on this wiki and this is a single house's private-company model.**

## 🆕 Recent signals (Aug 28, /run-inbox — **BARCLAYS BUILDS THE AI-LAB→HYPERSCALER PASS-THROUGH AS AN ACTUAL P&L, NAMES AWS AND GCP AT $37bn EACH IN '26E, AND DATES THE SHARE LOSS TO '28; AN EX-BEDROCK PM SAYS AZURE IS ITSELF RENTING NEOCLOUD CAPACITY**) — 🔴🔴 **THIS PAGE TRACKS CAPEX GOING OUT. BARCLAYS SUPPLIES ONE — AND ITS TERMINAL YEAR CARRIES A SHARE-LOSS CALL THAT LANDS ON EXACTLY THE BACKSTOPPED-INFRASTRUCTURE FINANCING THIS PAGE HAS BEEN DOCUMENTING.**

### 🔴 (1) THE RETURN LEG, SIZED: $35-40 OF EVERY $100 OF AI-LAB REVENUE, AT 35-45% OI MARGIN

- **The pass-through: "around $35-$40 of every $100 in AI Lab revenue flows to hyperscaler revenue in '26, on which the hyperscalers generate nearly $10-$20 in OI (at high ~35%-45% operating margin)."** By lab archetype: **API-weighted "Lab A" → $35 of hyperscaler revenue at 34% margin; subscription-weighted "Lab B" → $41 at 47%**, the difference being a **20%-of-revenue strategic-partner fee** — though *"the actual profit on a per token basis is likely the same excluding these fees."*
- **The industry ladder ($bn): AI-lab revenue 7 / 26 / 137 / 376 / 690 ('24/'25/'26E/'27E/'28E) → hyperscaler AI revenue 11 / 36 / 124 / 289 / 502.** Crucially the RATIO falls: **153% / 136% / 90% / 77% / 73%.** *"Right now, just about every dollar of AI lab ARR is finding its way to the hyperscaler revenue line, because of the high mix of training relative to inference in '26."*
- **Named splits: AWS AI revenue $3 / $10 / $37 / $87 / $151bn and GCP AI revenue $3 / $10 / $37 / $87 / $151bn — an assumed FLAT ~30% share each, with "Other Cloud" (incl. Azure) at 40% ($49 / $116 / $201bn '26E-'28E).** ⚠️ **Azure is NOT broken out. And the AWS=GCP parity is asserted, not derived — record it as a modelling convention, not a share call.**
- **🔴 THE DATED DISCONTINUITY, AND IT IS THE ITEM FOR THIS PAGE: "Hyperscalers likely start to LOSE MARKET SHARE of AI lab training and inference starting in '28 when BACKSTOPPED AI INFRASTRUCTURE PROJECTS come online and become the first option for AI labs."** ➤ **That is the residual-value-guarantee / vendor-financing architecture this page has been cataloguing (NVDA's "up to 25%" residual support, AVGO's ~82% guarantee, the ~$5.9bn-per-100MW guarantee math) re-read as a COMPETITIVE THREAT to the hyperscalers rather than as a credit risk. Same structures, opposite sign, and Barclays puts a year on it.**

(Barclays "U.S. Internet: A Primer on AI Lab & AI Hyperscaler Unit Economics", Ross Sandler / Alex Hughes, 2026-08-28.) [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/US_Internet_A_Primer_on_AI_Lab_AI_Hyperscaler_Unit_Economics_US_Internet_A_Primer_on_AI_Lab_AI_Hyperscaler_Unit.html)

### 🔴 (2) AZURE RENTS FROM NEOCLOUDS — AND THE ENTERPRISE ROUTES 90/10 THROUGH THE CLOUDS ANYWAY

From an AlphaSense expert call with a former AWS Bedrock PM (also ex-Google Vertex, ex-Meta, ex-Microsoft Azure ML). ⚠️ **Single expert, left AWS 2024; directional.**

- **🔴 "If you look at Azure, it rents data centers from Lambda or any NeoClouds today"** — framed as deliberate diversification, and used to argue the labs will keep renting too even after building their own. ➤ **A portion of hyperscaler AI capacity is LEASED, not owned — which flatters owned-capex optics, shifts the cost from capex to opex, and means [[CRWV]]/[[NBIS]] demand is partly hyperscaler demand rather than competing demand. Directly relevant to this page's off-balance-sheet-commitments work (~$3.1tn) and to the capex-vs-OCF ratios.**
- **The demand-routing claim underneath all the cloud AI revenue lines: "enterprises are… mostly 90/10 that they will go to Azure, Vertex, or Bedrock to get their API access and not go to OpenAI and Anthropic,"** on multi-geography support and *"six or eight nines"* network reliability.
- **On the neoclouds as competitors: "currently serve as supplemental infrastructure rather than direct competition, but could become competitors if demand stabilizes and they move up the stack"** — and *"if you look three years down the line, I do see CoreWeave and Nebius"* as such. Today the hyperscalers are not fighting them: *"let's increase the pie and not compete."*
- ➤ **🔴 NOTE THE CONVERGENCE: Barclays dates hyperscaler AI-lab share loss to '28 from a financing/backstop argument; the operator independently arrives at ~3 years from a stickiness argument. Two unrelated sources, same window — the strongest cross-corroborated forward risk on this page this week.**

(AlphaSense expert call, former Product Manager Technical at AWS (Bedrock), buy-side-led, 2026-08-27.) [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/--_-_ExpertCall_document_dated_27-08-2026_1.html)


## 🆕 Recent signals (Aug 27, /run-inbox — **[[META]] 2027 CAPEX GETS A BUY-SIDE FLOOR (~$200bn) AND A REUTERS-DERIVED CEILING (MID-$200bns), OFF ~$145bn THIS YEAR — AND THE COVERING ANALYST SAYS THE HIGH CASE REQUIRES A CAPITAL RAISE**) — 🔴🔴 **THIS PAGE TRACKS AGGREGATES. WHAT IT HAS LACKED IS A SINGLE HYPERSCALER'S 2027 RANGE WITH THE FINANCING CONSTRAINT ATTACHED. BofA'S POST-SETTLEMENT CALL SUPPLIES BOTH, PLUS THE GW→DOLLARS ASSUMPTION USED TO BUILD IT.**

**Source: BofA Securities · Justin Post, hosted client call on the Meta AG settlement, 2026-08-27. PT $800.** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_08_27_meta_call_baml_27_ago_26.html)

- 🔴 **THE RANGE, WITH ITS ARITHMETIC EXPOSED: *"There were articles from **REUTERS** saying they could be adding **up to SEVEN GIGAWATTS of capacity next year**. **If you assume 40 BILLION A GIGAWATT, that could put you in the MID-2s**, even up from **145 THIS YEAR**."* Post's own view is that *"that's probably **UNLIKELY to be that high**"*, but he puts the floor at *"**200 billion, which is kind of the MINIMUM**"* — citing an internal BofA buy-side survey taken before the last print. ⚠️ **The 7GW is REUTERS reporting, not company guidance; the $40bn/GW is BofA's assumption, not a disclosed figure; and the mid-$200bns is the product of the two. Carry the range (~$200bn floor / mid-$200bns ceiling off a ~$145bn 2026 base) and NOT the point estimate.**
- ⚠️⚠️ **UNIT DISCIPLINE, PER THE STANDING `$/watt` RULE ON THIS WIKI — AND THIS ONE IS A LIVE TRAP THIS WEEK: the "$40 billion a gigawatt" here is a **TOTAL BUILD-COST-per-GW** assumption used to gross up a CAPEX number. [[NVDA]] separately put its Vera Rubin platform at **~$40bn of NVDA REVENUE per GW** in the same fortnight, and its CFO commentary implies **~$35-47bn of NVDA revenue per GW** at PORTS-Pike. **The two $40bn figures measure completely different things and their coincidence is an accident. They must never be netted, chained, or used to infer NVDA's share of Meta's spend.** Jensen's own total-build figure is ~$60bn/GW.**
- 🔴 **THE FINANCING CONSTRAINT IS THE REAL CONTENT, AND IT IS TIME-LIMITED: *"If it's that high, we'd think they would probably need to **RAISE SOME CAPITAL** to fund it, or they're really going to keep doing pretty large deals with **BLACKROCK and others** to finance this build, **which people are VERY NERVOUS ABOUT**."* Asked to confirm the at-earnings message that no near-term equity raise was planned: *"I think that's fair for **THREE MONTHS**, but I wouldn't say that's HARD AND FAST over the next year."*** ➤ **A three-month half-life on a "no capital raise" message from the covering analyst is the most specific financing-timing mark this page carries for any single hyperscaler.**
- 🔴 **AND THE OFF-BALANCE-SHEET PROBLEM IS NAMED FROM THE DEMAND SIDE FOR ONCE: *"we're getting **a lot of requests for the amount of EXTERNAL FINANCING that's NOT NECESSARILY ON THE BALANCE SHEET, HOW TO QUANTIFY THAT**."*** ✅ **Directly corroborates the Morgan Stanley argument this page carries from 08-25 — that the accounting no longer shows you where the money is — but from the perspective of investors trying and failing to size it, not from a strategist arguing it.**
- ⚠️ **THE IRREVERSIBILITY POINT, WHICH SHOULD TEMPER ANY "THEY CAN JUST CUT" ARGUMENT: *"all these commitments that they have you can read about in the K, both on **EQUIPMENT PURCHASES and/or LEASES**. **This is NOT something you can fix with a 500, 5,000-person layoff.** It buys you some time, but **they seem to be ALL IN**."***
- 🔴 **BUT THE TWO NAMED PLAN Bs ARE THE ITEMS THIS PAGE SHOULD ACTUALLY TRACK, BECAUSE NEITHER IS IN CONSENSUS: *"if the products don't work… **Meta would either SCALE BACK CAPACITY GROWTH next year or START LEASING CAPACITY TO OTHERS**, which are pretty good plan Bs."*** ➤ **Plan B(i) is a direct demand risk to [[NVDA]] and the whole supply chain this wiki tracks. Plan B(ii) would turn a hyperscaler into a NEOCLOUD COMPETITOR — supplying the very capacity market that [[CRWV]], [[NBIS]] and NVDA's $500bn platform are financing. Both are cheap options for Meta and expensive for everyone else, and neither appears in any aggregate on this page.**
- **The valuation frame BofA attaches, which is the reason it is constructive despite the above: *"if we assume a **15 MULTIPLE on the core business**, the **CAPACITY INVESTMENT HAS CLOSE TO ZERO VALUE in the stock**, while other stocks are getting significant value for their capacity."* ➤ A testable statement about how the market is capitalising one hyperscaler's build versus its peers'.**

## 🆕 Recent signals (Aug 26, /run-inbox — **NVIDIA PUTS ITS OWN CAPEX AGGREGATE ON THE RECORD: ~$800BN IN 2026 AND $1.3 TRILLION IN 2027 FROM THE TOP FIVE, AGAINST A CLOUD BACKLOG NOW ABOVE $2 TRILLION**) — 🔴🔴 **THIS PAGE HAS CARRIED SIX DIFFERENT HOUSES' AGGREGATES AND THREE INCOMPATIBLE PERIMETERS. THE VENDOR THAT COLLECTS MOST OF THE MONEY HAS NOW PUBLISHED ITS OWN, AND IT SITS *BELOW* MORGAN STANLEY'S.**

- 🔴 **THE NUMBERS, FROM THE Q2 FY27 EARNINGS CALL: *"Our hyperscale customers delivered strong financial results in the quarter with accelerating revenue growth and expanding margins. **With cloud industry backlog now GREATER THAN $2 TRILLION. CapEx by the top five hyperscalers is expected to reach nearly $800 BILLION IN 2026 and $1.3 TRILLION IN 2027.**"*** (Colette Kress, NVIDIA, 2026-08-26; Jensen Huang repeats the $2tn backlog in Q&A — *"they now have backlogs of $2 trillion… when they stand up NVIDIA compute, their revenues go up, their earnings contribution go up"*).
- ⚠️⚠️ **BASIS DISCIPLINE — DO NOT NET THIS AGAINST THE OTHER AGGREGATES ON THIS PAGE. NVIDIA says "top five hyperscalers" and does not name them or define the perimeter. Against the marks already here: MS's aggregate reaches **~$1.4tn for 2027** but EXPLICITLY INCLUDES [[SPCX]], which it promoted into the set on 08-20; JPM's 2027 cash-capex figure is **>$1.2tn**; Barclays' hyperscaler set (GOOGL/AWS/META/MSFT/ORCL) reaches **~$1.16tn by 2028E**. NVIDIA's $1.3tn for 2027 therefore sits BELOW MS's $1.4tn on a probably-narrower perimeter and ABOVE JPM's >$1.2tn. **Four numbers, four definitions, none a correction of another — the same discipline this page already applies to the $445bn/$338bn/$194bn financing perimeters.**
- 🔴 **THE BACKLOG NUMBER IS THE GENUINELY NEW ONE AND IT IS THE MORE USEFUL HALF. ">$2 trillion of cloud industry backlog" is a DEMAND-SIDE contract stock, not a spend forecast, and it is the first time this page has had one. It reframes the ROI argument that runs through every block below: the question stops being "will the capex be earned back" and becomes "is the backlog real and can it be served" — which is a supply question. NVIDIA's own answer is that it cannot fully serve it: FY28 revenue guided +~70% against customer forecasts pointing to ~100%, explicitly *"a supply constrained outlook."*** ⚠️ **NVIDIA does not source the $2tn, does not define "cloud industry," and does not say what share is AI. Treat as a vendor aggregate.**
- 🔴 **A SECOND, SEPARATE CAPACITY SERIES THAT BELONGS ON THIS PAGE: NEOCLOUDS. *"Using NVIDIA DSX reference designs, our neocloud partners are bringing capacity online faster and at lower token cost. **They are expected to exit the year with EIGHT GIGAWATTS in total installed capacity, up from approximately THREE GIGAWATTS at the end of 2025.**"*** ➜ **A ~2.7x in installed neocloud capacity in one calendar year, from the vendor that ships into all of it. ⚠️ "Installed capacity" is not defined as facility GW or IT-load GW — per the standing GW-basis rule it must NOT be added to the facility-GW or critical-IT-load figures already on this page (e.g. the 3,024MW of critical IT load across the two [[META]] JV bonds).**
- 🔴 **THE PRIVATE-CAPITAL LEG IS NOW SIZED BY THE VENDOR TOO, AND IT IS THE SAME $500BN PLATFORM THIS PAGE LOGGED ON 08-24 — NOW WITH THE COUNTERPARTIES NAMED AND STILL NOT SIGNED. *"We recently announced partnerships with six of the world's leading infrastructure capital providers — **Apollo, BlackRock, Blackstone, Brookfield, Goldman Sachs and KKR** — to establish financing platforms that will raise **over $500 billion of third party capital**."* NVIDIA's press release adds the qualifier the call does not: *"subject to definitive agreements."*** ✅ **That CONFIRMS the 08-24 entry on this page — "NVIDIA's $500bn platform… has signed MOUs ONLY, NOTHING EXECUTED" — from the company's own filing language two days later. The counterparty list is net-new; the execution status is unchanged.**
- 🔴🔴 **AND THE VENDOR SIZED ITS OWN EXPOSURE TO THE FINANCED PORTION OF THE BUILD, WHICH NO AGGREGATE ON THIS PAGE HAS EVER HAD: *"we expect demand from the AI labs **for which we expect to leverage our balance sheet** to contribute toward **roughly A QUARTER OF OUR BUSINESS NEXT YEAR**."* Plus: ~$50bn already invested in frontier AI labs; a take-or-pay + revenue-share structure where *"we're not making loans"* and NVIDIA *"gets paid twice"*; **~12GW of NVIDIA compute committed by [[OPENAI]] through 2030**; a **~4.25GW AI-factory campus at PORTS-Pike, Ohio with SB Energy, to be utilised by OpenAI, at ~1.5mn NVIDIA GPUs per generation**; and **selective credit enhancement for ~2GW of compute at a second, unnamed frontier lab.**** ⚠️ **Three different GW denominators in that sentence — facility capacity (4.25GW) vs "of NVIDIA compute" (12GW, 2GW). They are not summable. Full write-up on [[NVDA]].**

⚠️ **SOURCE NOTE: the NVIDIA call PDF is a Bloomberg LIVE TRANSCRIPT with a zero-character text layer; 17 pages were rendered to images and read visually, speaker names are not in the file, and the speaker mapping plus ASR decode list live on [[NVDA]]'s page. Figures appearing in both the call and the press release were cross-checked against the release.** [Source — call transcript](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/NVDA_conf_call_transcript_ago_26.html) · [Source — press release](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/NVIDIAAn.html)

## Recent signals (Aug 25, /wiki-ingest — THE FUNDING GAP IS NOW SIZED: >$1.2tn OF 2027 CASH CAPEX AGAINST ~$1tn OF OPERATING CASH FLOW, AND >$3.1tn OF OFF-BALANCE-SHEET COMMITMENTS BEHIND IT) — 🔴🔴 **TWO HOUSES PUBLISH THE SAME DAY. JPM RAISES THE CAPEX NUMBERS AND ARGUES THE ROI EVIDENCE HAS ARRIVED; MORGAN STANLEY ARGUES THE ACCOUNTING NO LONGER SHOWS YOU WHERE THE MONEY IS.**

**LEG 1 — THE LEVEL (JPM · Harlan Sur, *"Improving Cloud Economics Reinforce the Case for Sustained AI Capex and an Extended Positive Semis Cycle"*, 2026-08-25).**
- 🔴 **Consensus C26 CSP capex now $950bn (+94% y/y) and C27 $1.4tn (+47% y/y)** — *"materially above where Street estimates stood entering Q2, and a **+42% HoH C26 step-up**."*
- **The monetisation evidence is the actual argument:** *"cloud/AI revenues are accelerating sharply (**[[AMZN]] backlog now $496bn, contracted at 5-year terms with 2-3 year payback**), and **cloud operating margins at AWS, GCP and [[MSFT]] IC are EXPANDING** — green shoots on ROI that should sustain and grow spending."*
- **Supply is still the binding constraint:** *"CSPs remain **compute-constrained despite ~$544bn in LTM spend**, with **AMZN explicitly flagging it still won't have enough capacity to meet 2026 demand even with its new $220bn capex budget**. **Legacy A100/H100 SPOT PRICING has also RISEN**, corroborating the tightness."*
- **JPM's picks off the thesis: [[AVGO]] and [[MRVL]] on custom ASIC momentum (Trainium demand improving, [[GOOG]] TPU commitments surging), with [[MU]] and [[SNDK]] as the memory plays — "hyperscalers willingly absorbing higher memory costs (AMZN attributed its $20bn capex raise partly to memory)."**

**LEG 2 — THE FUNDING (MS · Todd Castagno, GVAT, via MS TMT · Thomas Wigg, 2026-08-25). 🔴 This is the more useful half.**
- **MS expects hyperscalers to spend **>$1.2tn of cash capex in 2027 against ~$1tn of aggregate operating cash flow**, forcing the industry toward **leases / SPVs, public debt, lower buybacks / equity issuance, customer prepayments, and vendor-supported financing**.
- 🔴 **"Headline hyperscaler leverage MATERIALLY UNDERSTATES the economic funding commitment. Hyperscalers plus [[NVDA]] and [[AVGO]] have **>$3.1tn of disclosed off-balance-sheet commitments/guarantees, including $1.1tn of future LEASE PAYMENTS and $1.7tn of PURCHASE COMMITMENTS**. Hyperscalers alone have **>$2.7tn of undiscounted commitments — ~3 years of current operating cash flow**."**
- 🔴 **"FCF is becoming a LESS CLEAN MEASURE of AI investment intensity.** Data centers can be financed through **private-credit-backed SPVs and initially remain off balance sheet**; operating leases can also keep the associated investment out of reported capex/FCF. MS argues investors should **economically treat many of these structures like debt-financed capex**."
- **The worked example, and it is specific: "[[MSFT]] is the clearest accounting example: extending data-center useful lives from 15 to 25 years causes more 15-year leases to be classified as OPERATING rather than FINANCE leases. Because MSFT's FCF definition EXCLUDES operating leases, reported capex FALLS and FCF RISES without a corresponding change in the underlying economics."** ⚠️ **A characterisation of an accounting-policy consequence, not an allegation — but it is directly checkable against MSFT's disclosures and should be.**
- **[[GOOG]] is the equity-funding case: buybacks cut from >$60bn/yr to ZERO and $50bn of equity issued in 2Q26, creating >$110bn of incremental cash capacity.** **[[ORCL]] is the prepayment case: $4.6bn of customer prepayments used to finance capex, which "economically resemble financing because interest accretes against deferred revenue using ORCL's incremental borrowing rate."** **NVDA and AVGO "are increasingly becoming part of the AI financing architecture, not merely equipment suppliers," supporting chip-leasing SPVs through residual-value guarantees so "unrated AI labs [can] obtain chips at financing costs closer to those of investment-grade suppliers."**

**LEG 3 — THE PRICE OF THE ARCHITECTURE IS NOW VISIBLE IN CREDIT.** **MS initiates NVDA credit at Neutral: "over $1 trillion of guided GPU/XPU-related ecosystem financing may come via creative structures"; ~$200bn of all-in NVDA exposure by CY28-end including ~$170bn of contingent obligations; "trades more like BBB than AA after recent widening"; CDS ~25bp wide of similarly-rated hyperscalers** (MS · Lindsay Tyler, 2026-08-25). **[[AVGO]]: "Bond traders have ramped up measures of credit risk… yields on Broadcom's 5.15% 2031 bonds rose about 14bps and its five-year CDS 28 basis points"** (Bloomberg via JPM, 2026-08-25); **"Broadcom CDS up 5bps to a new all-time high, NVDA also at record wide every single day now"** (@zerohedge, corpus, 2026-08-25).

**LEG 4 — THE NON-FINANCIAL CONSTRAINT THE DESKS SAY IS UNDER-PRICED.** *"**Data Center backlash — this one is under-appreciated by Tech investors, but perhaps the biggest risk in my view as we head toward the mid-terms. Texas [is] current ground zero on this issue given [a] pause in such a red state**"* (MS TMT desk · Thomas Wigg citing POLITICO, 2026-08-25). MS's thematics team makes the same point analytically in its [[CIEN]] preview: **"political and power constraints are increasingly shaping data-center development, with opposition rising NON-LINEARLY with campus size and potentially pushing workloads toward smaller, more distributed sites"** — with the important corollary that *"delayed or cancelled mega-campus projects may **REDISTRIBUTE capacity rather than eliminate it**"* (MS · Meta Marshall, 2026-08-25). ⚠️ **Note the sign flip: siting backlash is a bear item for the campus builders and a BULL item for long-haul interconnect.**

**LEG 5 — INTERNAL RATIONING CONTINUES.** MS TMT's litany of what sold the AI-capex complex off into 08-24/25 includes **more weekend equity raises, memory concerns (a loosely reported [[AAPL]]+CXMT approval headline post the Trump-Xi meeting in September, plus the YMTC IPO), rates, and "NVDA jitters into the print amid high expectations + weak reactions to the last four prints, and continued debate on their $500B financing vehicle."** **Staggering positioning stat from the same desk: "2026 has been the most volatile year for Tech Momentum (MSZZTMTM Index) with the basket closing up +5% or down −5% in a session 57x this year, and 2020 the next closest with 9x."**

## Recent signals (Aug 24, /wiki-ingest 21h — THE FINANCING ARCHITECTURE GETS ITS FIRST SIDE-BY-SIDE COVERAGE COMPARISON, AND SEMIANALYSIS PUTS A NUMBER ON THE DEBT STOCK) — 🔴🔴 **NVIDIA'S $500bn PLATFORM OFFERS RESIDUAL-VALUE SUPPORT OF "UP TO 25%" AND HAS SIGNED *MOUs ONLY, NOTHING EXECUTED*. BROADCOM'S JUNE DEAL, WHICH DID CLOSE, CARRIED A GUARANTEE COVERING ~82% OF THE DEAL. AND SEMIANALYSIS PUTS CUMULATIVE LIFETIME AI CAPEX AT ~$3TN BY END-2026 GOING TO $11TN+, WITH AI DEBT REACHING $7TN+ — MAKING AI THE SECOND-LARGEST ASSET-RELATED DEBT MARKET AFTER US RESIDENTIAL MORTGAGES *THIS YEAR*.**

- **Source 1: The Information · Dakin Campbell, "Filling In the Gaps in Nvidia's $500 Billion Financing Pitch", Special Report, 2026-08-24.**
- 🔴 **THE STATE OF PLAY, WHICH IS THE MOST IMPORTANT SENTENCE: _"The firms have SIGNED MEMORANDUMS OF UNDERSTANDING BUT HAVEN'T EXECUTED ANYTHING, and very few people with any knowledge of the actual plans wanted to talk."_** An NVDA spokesperson told the reporter the topic *"will likely come up in the company's earnings this week, 'so recommend giving that call a listen.'"* ➜ **As of 2026-08-24 the $500bn number is an intention, not a facility. The Aug 26 call is the first opportunity for an executable detail.**
- **THE TERMS AS DISCLOSED:** six financial giants **including Blackstone, Apollo and Goldman Sachs**; lenders *"could make loans against the CASH ITS COMPUTE GENERATES"*; underwriting assesses **residual value**; and **NVDA "would provide residual value support of UP TO 25%, covering part of the loss in the event of a default if assets backing loans fall — though it DIDN'T SPECIFY WHICH ASSETS."** Structuring expectation from a person close to the consortium: *"some of the debt issued would likely **mature in FIVE YEARS OR LESS and be paid down quickly by cash generated by the compute**… Any value the chips retain after the debt is repaid would be potential upside."*
- 🔴 **THE COMPARISON THAT MAKES THIS PAGE'S CREDIT WORK CONCRETE.** The precedent deal — **$35bn in June, Apollo + Blackstone raising debt to buy [[AVGO]]-designed [[GOOG]] TPUs and LEASE them to [[ANTHROPIC]]** — had **AVGO backstopping $29bn via a residual-value guarantee, "covering roughly 82% of the overall deal,"** with the riskiest tranche unbackstopped. ➜ **82% (AVGO, executed) vs up to 25% (NVDA, MOU only). Two readings, both live: NVDA has more pricing power and needs to guarantee less; or NVDA's structure is untested precisely because it guarantees less. Bloomberg's report that AVGO's second deal (">$60bn, possibly with a $30bn junior tranche") moves "closer to what Nvidia is envisioning" suggests convergence, but from opposite directions.** ⚠️ **Basis clash with this wiki's 08-24 AM UBS entry (~$100bn = $60-70bn senior + ~$30bn junior): either $60bn is the senior leg or the whole deal. Log the basis; do not average.**
- **WHY THE STRUCTURE EXISTS, in the lender's own framing:** *"So far, when GPU purchases have been financed… **there's usually a contract or other backstop from an INVESTMENT-GRADE CUSTOMER like Microsoft or Meta Platforms. Smaller AI companies without close ties to such benefactors LARGELY HAVEN'T BEEN ABLE TO ACCESS THE SAME FINANCING.**"* — and the credit analyst's version: *"**The challenge for the vendors of these GPUs is, how do we EXTEND THE RUNWAY FOR GROWTH? The industry collectively has been trying to build out more of this infrastructure ON THE BACK OF DEBT FINANCING**"* (Andy Li, CreditSights). ➜ **The vendor guarantee is a SUBSTITUTE for an investment-grade offtaker. That is the whole mechanism, stated plainly by the people underwriting it.**
- 🔴 **THE COLLATERAL PROBLEM, AND A DATED EVENT THAT WOULD PARTLY SOLVE IT.** Lenders' objection is not that GPUs die but that they are not fungible: performance *"can vary widely based on factors such as **power supply, how they are networked, and what optimization software is used**. That worries lenders who would prefer a standard chip with a long life that can act as stable collateral." Against that: Huang's blog post arguing software/networking make GPUs "fungible," and [[CRWV]] disclosing **a contract for A100s — a 2020 part — "that extends into 2029."** 🔴 **And the event: "CME Group, in partnership with Silicon Data, hopes to launch FUTURES CONTRACTS for RENTAL PRICES of Nvidia's H100 and B200 chips in OCTOBER," letting lenders short GPU rental prices as a hedge. Ornn and Silicon Data already publish index-form forward curves. An October listing is checkable and would be the first screen-priced mark on AI infrastructure residual value.** ⚠️ Caveat from the source: the underlying financing market probably has to exist first.

- **Source 2: @SemiAnalysis_ thread, 2026-08-24 — *independent research, public thread; no methodology published in the excerpt seen*.**
- 🔴 *"By the end of this year, **cumulative lifetime AI Capex will have reached nearly $3T USD** and we expect this number to grow to **$11T+ by the late 2020s**, with **total outstanding AI Debt reaching $7T+**. **2026 will be the FIRST YEAR in which AI Debt Financing becomes the SECOND LARGEST ASSET-RELATED DEBT MARKET, after only the US Residential Mortgage Market.** It is also the **LAST year in which total AI related debt issuance will be less than 10% of total US Fixed Income Issuance** (this includes state and federal bonds, MBS/RMBS, corporate bonds, agency, muni and ABS)."*
- ➜ **Cross-check against what this page already carries and the numbers are compatible in direction, not in basis: GS credit's $5.8trn of FY2025-FY2030 capex across FIVE names, the debt-financed share on a 27% → 33% → 35% path with $300bn of project finance on top in 2027, ~$510bn of incremental hyperscaler debt capacity before index-concentration limits bind, and WSJ's ~$3trn of off-balance-sheet AI obligations. SemiAnalysis's $7T+ debt stock is a LIFETIME/CUMULATIVE figure across the whole ecosystem including neoclouds and SPVs — it is NOT comparable to GS's hyperscaler issuance path. Do not stack them.** ⚠️ **No methodology, no name list and no year-by-year path in the excerpt. Logged as the most aggressive published framing of the debt stock, with its basis flagged as unverified.**
- **And the mechanism that makes the debt bigger without making the compute bigger — [[NVDA]]'s ~17% price increase** (The Information, 2026-08-24), which **Vital Knowledge sizes as "could raise the price of 1GW of data center compute by $5B+, EXACERBATING THE FREE CASH FLOW SQUEEZE facing hyperscalers and neoclouds"** (Adam Crisafulli, 2026-08-24). ⚠️ *Incremental PURCHASE PRICE per GW — not revenue/W, not a total build-cost anchor; do not chain it into a $/W series.*
## Recent signals (Aug 24, /wiki-ingest — BofA CAPEX AGGREGATE + JEFFERIES CHINA/US COMPARISON) — 🔴🔴 **SELL-SIDE 2027-2028 CLOUD CAPEX WAS REVISED ~20% HIGHER IN A SINGLE MONTH, WITH GOOGL / SPCX / AMZN / META THE BIGGEST MOVERS — AND JEFFERIES SHOWS CHINA AND THE US HAVE BOTH CROSSED THE SAME TWO THRESHOLDS: CAPEX >90% OF OPERATING CASH FLOW AND >100% OF NON-GAAP EARNINGS.**

- 🔴 **The revision (BofA sales · Brian Fenske color relayed by Claudio Franco, 2026-08-24 — ⚠️ SALES COMMENTARY, EXPLICITLY NOT RESEARCH; chart built off Bloomberg consensus):** *"CLOUD CAPEX FOR 2027-2028 HAS BEEN REVISED ABOUT 20% HIGHER IN THE PAST MONTH"* across **AMZN, GOOGL, META, MSFT, ORCL and now [[SPCX]]** — with the **biggest upward revisions at GOOGL, SPCX, AMZN and META.**
- ⚠️⚠️ **THE CAVEAT THAT MUST TRAVEL WITH THE NUMBER, carried verbatim: "Recall MSFT made an accounting change to amortize leases over a much longer period, optically reducing 'total' capex."** **Do not read [[MSFT]]'s line as a demand signal, and do not compare its 2027-28 capex to its own prior vintages without adjusting.**
- **[[SPCX]] is now a permanent member of the aggregate**, which is the second house to promote it in (MS's aggregate reached **~$1.4tn for 2027** on the same logic — logged 08-20). Elon's own framing on the 07-Aug call — *"lets say closer to 10 gigawatts of compute than 5"* in 2027 — is *"still being treated as a 'show me' part of the story"* (BofA sales, 2026-08-24).
- 🔴 **CHINA VS US, POST-2Q, ON THREE RATIOS (Jefferies · Thomas Chong, "AI Series #67: China CSP Takeaways; China/US Comparison; AI Lab Earnings", 2026-08-24 — China: Alibaba, Tencent, Baidu; US: Amazon, Microsoft, Google, Meta; source Visible Alpha):** **(1) Capex-to-operating-cash-flow: "Both China and the US reached high levels of over 90%."** **(2) Capex-to-non-GAAP-earnings: "Both China and the US surpassed the 100% level."** **(3) Capex-to-revenue: "China is still lower than the US, but the ga[p is narrowing]."** 🔴 **Capex exceeding 100% of non-GAAP earnings on both sides of the Pacific simultaneously is the single most compact statement of this cycle's financing dependence — it means the marginal dollar of buildout is not coming from earnings.** That is the demand-side mirror of the credit-widening logged in themes/ai-datacenter-power.
- **BABA specifics from the same note:** revenue growth *"expected to accelerate to over 50% YoY in Sep quarter, and accelerate further in the Dec/Mar quarters"*; **AI ARR of RMB16bn in August with management optimistic about USD4.5bn (RMB30bn) at year-end**; and *"capex payback period of 3 years could be shortened on GPM improvement from better monetization, and product mix."* Corroborating desk colour: management **guided Cloud to +50% in coming quarters**, with **AI Labs losses moderating** and *"3-year breakeven periods on capex potentially falling to 2-2.5 years"* (Bernstein · David Patafio / Tyler Seidman, BABA EPS recap, 2026-08-21). ⚠️ **Alibaba simultaneously raised equity in Hong Kong, which the Asia desk flagged as the investors' main question: *"Why the BABA fund raise when $70bn cash"*** (Jefferies · COMARA, 2026-08-23/24).
- ⚠️ **THE DEMAND SIGNALS DIVERGE THIS WEEK AND SHOULD NOT BE AVERAGED.** Jefferies' **Token Expenditure Index "continues [to] be soft, with mid-Aug showing the lowest levels YTD"** while, in the same note, **"OpenRouter showed token consumption increased 24% vs prior week."** Separately, **open-source token share at Vercel went 28% → 62% in two months even as "the sum of [[OPENAI]] and [[ANTHROPIC]] accelerated in July"** (@GavinSBaker, Atreides Management, 2026-08-23). **Honest synthesis: volume up, price per token down, share rotating toward open weights — which is capex-positive and model-layer-margin-negative.** See themes/tokenmaxxing.
- **The enterprise leg, from a standing survey launched this week (FundaAI, 13 interviews, 10-20/week ongoing, 2026-08-23 — independent Substack expert-network research):** *"spend is still growing, and the interesting part is where the increment lands… Seats are close to saturated at the companies already committed, so the growth is moving into API calls and production workflows."* Named: **a large global bank from ~$150-160mn/yr to $200-300mn**; **a large European telecom at $30-50k/month, up from $15k at the start of the year, expecting just under $100k within six months**; **a mid-to-large biopharma from ~80/20 subscription-to-API to 70/30.**
- 🔴 **THE DELIVERY BOTTLENECK IS NOW THE BINDING CONSTRAINT, NOT THE BUDGET.** JPM's OCP APAC work has **AI datacentre build schedules extending from ~8 months to 12-18+ months**, warning that *"any slippage in construction timelines could trigger a temporary inventory-adjustment cycle across the supply chain"* (JPM · Albert Hung, 2026-08-23); UBS sees **[[AVGO]]'s AI revenue ramp *"tied to readiness of powered-shell infrastructure rather than compute silicon availability"*** (2026-08-24); and MS puts **October 2026 as the last date to sign contracts for capacity energised before year-end 2027** (MS · Stephen Byrd et al., 2026-08-24). **A capex revision that cannot be physically delivered shows up as a revenue-timing problem, not a demand problem.** See themes/ai-datacenter-power.
- **Positioning cross-read:** *"Hedge funds and mutual funds differ on AI but converge on Financials"* (GS · Ben Snider, US Weekly Kickstart, 2026-08-21; also GS · "What Matters Today", 2026-08-24), while in software specifically *"Long Onlies have been aggressively selling down positions in the biggest SaaS names all year"* — ADBE, ADSK, [[CRM]], INTU, NOW, WDAY, per LO active-manager positioning through 30-June (BofA US TMT spec Brian Fenske, via BofA · Frederic Boulan, 2026-08-24).

## Recent signals (Aug 20, /run-inbox — the credit-market block) — 🔴🔴 **THE CREDIT MARKET NOW PRICES *COUNTERPARTY QUALITY* ON PHYSICALLY IDENTICAL DATA CENTRES: ~8% FOR AMAZON OFFTAKE vs 11.5-14.2% FOR CoreWeave OFFTAKE — A 300-600BP OBSERVABLE. AND MORGAN STANLEY PROMOTES [[SPCX]] INTO ITS HYPERSCALER-CAPEX AGGREGATE, WHICH NOW REACHES ~$1.4 TRILLION FOR 2027 (NEARLY DOUBLE THE NOVEMBER-2025 ESTIMATE)**

_(Sources this block: **Morgan Stanley Global Credit Strategy & Securitized Products** — Fernanda Lima / Carolyn L Campbell / Vishwanath Tirupattur / Vishwas Patkar / James Egan / Aron Becker / Jonathan Loke / Mayank Verma / Lindsay Tyler / Kate Konetzke, **"AI Debt Financing Tracker: The Summer Spreads Turned Wider", 2026-08-20**, 36pp · **Morgan Stanley US Credit Strategy** — Vishwas Patkar / Fernanda Lima / Vishwanath Tirupattur / Carolyn Campbell / Eva Baurmeister / Christina Sigler, **"A Break from the Heat, For Now", 2026-08-14**, 24pp · **Goldman Sachs Credit Strategy Research** — Amanda Lynam / Shamshad Ali / Sara Grut / Spencer Rogers, **"Credit Notes: Expectations for Hyperscaler Debt Issuance: A Top-Down Approach", 2026-07-27** and **"Credit Notes: New Debt Issuance: Why This Time Is Different", 2026-08-09** · **Barclays** — Ross Sandler (US Internet, POSITIVE) + Tom O'Malley (US Semis & Semicap, NEUTRAL), **"Charts Worth A Look: Unpacking AI Infrastructure 'Backstop 101'", 2026-08-13** · **Ash & Seed Press** — Shanaka Anslem Perera, **"THE CONTINUITY STACK: NVIDIA Did Not Raise $500 Billion", 2026-08-18**. ⚠️ **CREDIT-STRATEGY notes: no ratings, no PTs, no company estimates.**)_

What is genuinely net-new is flagged 🔴.**

**① 🔴🔴 THE SINGLE MOST USEFUL TABLE IN THIS RUN, AND IT IS HARDER EVIDENCE THAN ANY EQUITY-SIDE OPINION ON COUNTERPARTY RISK: the bond market is charging roughly 300-600BP MORE FOR CoreWeave OFFTAKE THAN FOR AMAZON OFFTAKE ON PHYSICALLY COMPARABLE DATA-CENTRE PROJECTS** (MS "AI Debt Financing Tracker: The Summer Spreads Turned Wider", 2026-08-20). **None of these issuers has a wiki page — plain text with the bond identifier.**
