- ➤ **WHY THIS MATTERS MORE THAN THE SPREAD LEVEL ITSELF: this page has spent a month arguing about whether hyperscaler offtake is a genuine credit enhancement or a narrative one. Here it is priced, on comparable steel and comparable megawatts, by lenders with money at risk. The Amazon-offtake paper clears at 7.9-8.8%; the CoreWeave-offtake paper at 8.2-14.2%. That IS the market's estimate of the difference between an IG offtaker and a leveraged one, and it is the cleanest available input to the "who is actually underwriting the build" question. Cross-filed to [ai-datacenter-power](ai-datacenter-power.md), where the site-level detail sits.**
- **The corporate-loan and IG legs of the same table, for the funding-cost ladder this page tracks: [[CRWV]] term loan (S+550), drawn 7/30/26, 9/30/31 maturity, BB, 9.15% all-in, spread 584 → 527bp (−57bp); QTS (S+225) BBB−, 5.90%, 236 → 231; "LAMBCM" (Lambda) 8/12/26, 6.65%, 303 → 287. New IG issues: [[GOOG]] 8/6/26 — the '31 at 4.88% (spread 60 → 56) and the '36 at 5.45% (85 → 81); EQIX '31 5.25% (95 → 96) and '36 5.80% (120 → 127)** (MS, 2026-08-20). ⚠️ **Note the divergence inside one table: CoreWeave's loan spread TIGHTENED 57bp while Equinix's IG bonds WIDENED — so "AI credit is widening" is not uniform, and the direction differs by instrument and by issuer, not just by theme.**

**② 🔴 THE AGGREGATE MS IS NOW FORECASTING AGAINST — AND A TAXONOMY CHANGE THIS PAGE SHOULD LOG EXPLICITLY.** **MS's 2027 capex estimate is ~$1.4 TRILLION, and "2027 combined capex estimates have NEARLY DOUBLED versus November 2025 estimates."** 🔴 **The constituent list is [[AMZN]], [[GOOG]], [[META]], [[MSFT]], [[ORCL]] and [[SPCX]] — MS states plainly that "capex estimates now include SPCX"** (MS "AI Debt Financing Tracker", 2026-08-20). ➤ **THE PROMOTION OF SPACEX INTO THE HYPERSCALER AGGREGATE IS ITSELF THE DATAPOINT. This page already carries three different "five hyperscaler" perimeters — GS's ([[MSFT]]/[[AMZN]]/[[META]]/[[GOOG]]/[[ORCL]], 07-09), MS's own July "five primary companies" which already included SPCX (07-12), and the big-four JPM series — and the membership now differs by house AND by note. Any $/GW, memory-share or debt-share cross-check run off "the hyperscalers" must state which perimeter it uses. ⚠️ NO PER-NAME SPLIT IS TAKEN FROM THIS NOTE: the capex exhibit is a column-interleaved chart whose per-name values cannot be read reliably from the PDF text layer.** ✅ **Directional consistency check that passes: ~$1.4tn for 2027 on a six-name basis sits exactly on MS's own 07-12 and 07-27 marks ($1.2tn/$1.4tn '27/'28) and on the ~$1.2tn 2027 consensus this page carries from three houses — the six-name aggregate is at the top of that range, as it should be.**

**③ 🔴 THE SUPPLY IS NOT SLOWING EVEN AS IT GETS MORE EXPENSIVE, AND MS's TACTICAL VIEW IS THE OPPOSITE OF THE OBVIOUS ONE.** **HQ hyperscalers are ~30bp wider YTD, yet $54BN PRICED IN JULY AND $41BN IN AUGUST MONTH-TO-DATE. Across credit channels, ~$445BN OF AI-RELATED ISSUANCE YTD against $217BN FOR ALL OF 2025, with FY26E at $500-550BN.** MS expects *"a busy fall and continued spread widening"* — but on positioning: *"we think issuance out of hyperscalers could slow in the near term, but **we would fade a move back to the tights** given 2027 capex estimates (~$1.4tr)"* (MS, 2026-08-20). ➤ **Compare the 08-03 GS podcast item already on this page, where the desk argued a capex slowdown could make credit RALLY. MS agrees issuance may pause and disagrees about what to do with it: GS says less supply = tighter; MS says do not pay for tights because the 2027 bill has not gone away. Both are on the page; the arbitrating observable is the same one the GS desk named — new-issue concessions.**
- **The YTD-2026 bucket table (US$bn), which is the like-for-like base MS forecasts off:** **HQ Hyperscalers 132 · [[ORCL]] 25 · [[SPCX]] 25 · Semiconductors 42 · REITs 5 · Data Center Construction 43 · TOTAL IG 271.** Monthly hyperscaler path Jan→Aug: **0, 20, 37, 25, 0, 0, 25, 25** — versus **$5bn in the prior-year period, +2540%.** Oracle is **a single $25bn February deal** (vs $8bn prior year, +223%). Semiconductors: **5, 0, 0, 8, 0, 25, 0, 5 → $42bn, +57%.** 🔴 **DATA-CENTER CONSTRUCTION WENT FROM $0 TO $43BN** — a funding channel that did not exist in the prior-year period. **FY26E: hyperscaler total $250-300bn; public IG total $350-400bn; total across all global credit markets $500-550bn.** Securitized: **US ~$33bn YTD-26 vs ~$27bn prior** ⚠️ (the note prints the row as "18 33 27 23%" — treat the securitized line as approximate and do not over-precise it). Non-USD: **corporate credit 69 (prior-year 50, FY25A 15); securitized 2; total non-USD 70.**
- ✅ **CORROBORATION, NOT NET-NEW: MS's FY26E hyperscaler total of $250-300bn brackets the ~$250bn this page already derives from GS's "2026 ≈ 33% of capex" path (08-03), and UBS's $275-285bn public-issuance forecast (08-01). Three houses, three methods, one range. That convergence is the datapoint; none of the three is a revision of another.**

**④ 🔴 THE DISPERSION IS THE STORY, AND THE NUMBER IS STARK — THE HYPERSCALER COMPLEX HAS WIDENED ~30x THE BROAD IG INDEX.** **YTD spread change by bucket (bp): HQ Hyperscalers +29 · Hyperscalers incl. [[ORCL]] and [[SPCX]] +26 · [[NVDA]]+[[AVGO]] +20 · Semis +2 · Tech +11 · IG +1** (MS "A Break from the Heat, For Now", 2026-08-14). ➤ **This is the quantification of a claim this page has carried qualitatively since 07-28 ("hyperscaler credit spreads are widening"). +29bp against +1bp for the index says the market is repricing the THEME, not credit generally — and the +20bp on the NVDA/AVGO bucket says the vendor-backstop leg is being repriced alongside the buyers, which is exactly the transmission channel this page flagged on 08-06.**
- **MS's framing thesis, worth carrying verbatim because it names the release valve: medium-term AI financing needs remain substantial despite the potential for a near-term slowdown; financing should shift INCREASINGLY TOWARD CHIPS AND COMPONENTS with a larger role for private capital and high-quality credit support; and balance-sheet/market capacity remain ample but "increasingly price-sensitive, MAKING WIDER SPREADS THE NATURAL RELEASE VALVE."** ➤ **Read against the GS "$510bn absorption ceiling" already on this page: GS frames the constraint as a QUANTITY ceiling; MS frames it as a PRICE mechanism with no hard ceiling. Those are different models of the same market and they imply different failure modes — a funding stop versus a funding tax. Both retained.**

**⑤ 🔴🔴 THE STANDOUT NET-NEW ITEM OF THIS RUN: A BRAND-NEW FUNDING CHANNEL HAS OPENED FOR SEMICONDUCTORS — "CHIP FINANCING" VIA SECTION 4(a)(2) PRIVATE PLACEMENTS WENT FROM $0 TO $35BN.** Issuance by channel, prior-year YTD → current YTD (% change): **US HY 15 → 41 (+176%) · US leveraged loans 2 → 10 (+394%) · non-USD IG hyperscalers 15 → 62 (+313%) · EUR HY ([[CRWV]]) 0 → 2 · TOTAL 189 → 382 (+102%) · Section 4(a)(2) chip financing 0 → 35** (MS, 2026-08-14). ➤ **This is the CONCRETE INSTANCE of MS's "financing shifts toward chips and components" call, and it is the first time this page has had a dollar figure on a private-placement channel dedicated to silicon. It also matters for what is VISIBLE: 4(a)(2) paper is not index-eligible and does not consume the USIG absorption capacity GS sizes at ~$510bn — so the ceiling this page has been treating as binding can be partly routed around without appearing in any public-supply series. ⚠️ Counterparties, tenors and pricing are not disclosed in the note; the $35bn is a channel total, not a deal list.**

**⑥ 🔴 THE SHARPEST SINGLE-NAME CREDIT CONSTRAINT IN THE SET, AND IT IS [[ORCL]] WITH BOTH FUNDING CHANNELS IMPAIRED AT ONCE.** MS, verbatim: *"wider spreads alone might be insufficient for lower-rated companies to maintain access to the credit markets. Our credit sector analysts **remain cautious on Oracle given its forward financing needs, with bond yields approaching 8% and the stock down ~22% YTD, reducing the attractiveness of future equity raises as a source of funding.** Here, high debt costs might work as a natural stabilizer for future issuance"* (MS, 2026-08-14). ➤ **Set that against the DB work already on this page (08-07): ~$40bn of incremental FY28 capital needed on ~$120bn of gross capex, with a bear case assuming 100% of the ~$80bn FY27-28 financing comes from EQUITY. MS is saying the equity leg is now impaired at the same moment the debt leg is priced near 8% — the bear case's escape hatch and its main door are closing together. This is the single most actionable credit item in the run.** **Also flagged for the diary: *"For Single Name, ORCL and AVGO remain front and center ahead of their respective September [reporting dates]"*** (MS, 2026-08-20). See [../ORCL.md](../ORCL.md), [../AVGO.md](../AVGO.md).

**⑦ 🔴 THE MECHANIC THAT TIES THE CREDIT PAGE TO THE VENDOR-BACKSTOP PAGE, IN MS's OWN WORDS: *"HQ issuers will continue to FLEX THEIR RATINGS MUSCLES TO PROVIDE CREDIT SUPPORT TO OTHERS"* as new structures emerge** (MS, 2026-08-20). ➤ **That is the same mechanic as the [[NVDA]]/[[AVGO]] backstop thread this page has tracked since 08-06 and the [[GOOG]] $44bn lease-backstop item (07-27) — now stated by a credit desk as a forward EXPECTATION rather than as a set of one-offs. The balance sheet being lent is a RATING, not cash; the thing being consumed is the AA/AAA issuer's own scarcity value. MS's stated preference on the back of it: FAVOUR COLLATERAL-BACKED RISK, INCLUDING DATA CENTERS — i.e. take the asset, not the promise.**

**⑧ ✅ EUROPE AND THE NON-DOLLAR MARKETS: THIS PAGE ARGUED (via GS, 08-03) THAT EUROPE WAS "AN OBVIOUS CHOICE" AND UNDER-CONTRIBUTING. HERE IS THE EVIDENCE ON HOW FAR IT HAS ACTUALLY GONE, AND IT IS NARROWER THAN THE ARGUMENT IMPLIED.** **Only [[GOOG]] and [[AMZN]] have used the non-dollar markets at all this year, so sector concentration is less pronounced outside USD for now; Alphabet's JPY deal was THE BIGGEST YEN BOND ON RECORD SOLD BY A FOREIGN ISSUER; and the hyperscalers have demonstrated EUR credit's ability to absorb QE-era-sized jumbo deals** (MS, 2026-08-20), against **non-USD IG hyperscaler issuance of 15 → 62 (+313%) y/y** (MS, 2026-08-14). ➤ **So the channel works and is being scaled hard, but it is being used by TWO issuers. The under-contribution GS identified is a fact about participation, not about capacity — which means the non-USD relief valve is available to the group and has simply not been drawn on by [[MSFT]], [[META]], [[ORCL]] or [[SPCX]] yet. That is an option, and it is unpriced on this page.**

**⑨ 🔴 SOURCING PRECISION ON A NUMBER THIS PAGE ALREADY CARRIES — AND THEN A RECONCILIATION FLAG THAT MATTERS MORE THAN THE NUMBER.** The **$194BN of hyperscaler YTD debt** already on this page is now traced to its primary written source: *"So far in 2026 (as of July 25th) the hyperscalers have issued $194 billion of debt"* — **excluding data-centre financings issued by SPVs**, and on a **FIVE-NAME definition: [[AMZN]], [[GOOG]], [[MSFT]], [[META]], [[ORCL]]** (GS "Credit Notes: Expectations for Hyperscaler Debt Issuance: A Top-Down Approach", 2026-07-27). GS's method is stated in three steps: frame the capex trajectory from GS GIR equity research's five-name FY2026-FY2030 forecasts plus FY2025 actuals, match it against actual and forecast **cash flow from operations** to assess sources vs uses, then estimate a **debt-financed share of capex** and apply it forward. ✅ **NOT A NEW DATAPOINT — this page already carried $194bn from the 07-09 note and the 08-03 podcast. What is added is the DEFINITION.**
- ⚠️🔴 **AND THE DEFINITION IS THE POINT, BECAUSE IT MAKES GS AND MS NON-COMPARABLE. GS counts $194bn as of 25 July on five names EXCLUDING SPV data-centre financings. MS counts, through mid-August, HQ Hyperscalers $132bn + [[ORCL]] $25bn + [[SPCX]] $25bn + Data Center Construction $43bn. Those are DIFFERENT PERIMETERS ON DIFFERENT DATES, and neither is a correction of the other. Anyone netting them will produce a fake contradiction — which is exactly why AI-issuance figures appear to conflict across the sell-side when they do not. THE RULE FOR THIS PAGE: quote an AI-issuance number with its (a) issuer perimeter, (b) SPV treatment and (c) as-of date, or do not quote it.**

**⑩ 🔴 THE STRUCTURAL POINT UNDER THE ELEVATED GROSS SUPPLY, AND ONE LEG OF IT IS A GENUINELY DIFFERENT MECHANISM FROM ANYTHING ON THIS PAGE** (GS "Credit Notes: New Debt Issuance: Why This Time Is Different", 2026-08-09). GS names three patterns sitting "under the surface":
- **(1) A sharp acceleration of "JUMBO" new issues in 2026, extending 2025's momentum — GS defines jumbo as a deal valued at $10 BILLION OR MORE AT ANNOUNCEMENT.**
- 🔴 **(2) THE SHARE OF JUMBO DEALS THAT ARE ACQUISITION-RELATED IS LOW — AND GS EXPLAINS WHY THAT IS BAD RATHER THAN GOOD: *"M&A-related debt deals are telegraphed roughly FIVE MONTHS IN ADVANCE, on average, which gives market participants time to prepare by analyzing credit metrics and adjusting portfolios."* ➤ TODAY'S JUMBO SUPPLY ARRIVES WITH FAR LESS WARNING THAN THE M&A-DRIVEN JUMBO SUPPLY OF PAST CYCLES. LESS LEAD TIME = MORE INDIGESTION RISK PER DOLLAR. That is a mechanism this page has not had: it says the "$X bn of supply" figures understate the disruption because the market cannot pre-position for capex-driven issuance the way it does for announced M&A. It is also the missing explanation for why concessions blew out from 2-3bp to 20bp (08-03) on deals that were not, in aggregate size, unprecedented.** Cross-filed as the PRIMARY route to [macro-cycle](macro-cycle.md).
- ✅ **(3) The weighted-average tenor of Technology-sector issuance runs ROUGHLY 3.5 YEARS LONGER than the broader USD IG market. THIS IS NOT A SEPARATE FINDING — it is the SECTOR-LEVEL QUANTIFICATION of the duration point already at the head of this page ("40% of 15-year-plus IG issuance this year by AI companies"; [[AMZN]] now the highest duration weight in the IG index, [[GOOG]] 18th from 86th). Same phenomenon, same desk, expressed as a tenor gap instead of a share-of-long-end. Logged as such, not as new evidence.**

**⑪ 🔴 BARCLAYS SIZES THE VENDOR-BACKSTOPPED CHANNEL AT ">$1 TRILLION" AND LANDS ON 2027 AS THE CONSTRAINT YEAR — BY A COMPLETELY DIFFERENT ROUTE FROM GS.** *"The AI build-out is heading in a new direction with **upwards of $1T in new compute financing backstopped by Nvidia and Broadcom**. This new approach allows major buyers of AI compute (AI labs) to take more control of their business models, at a time when **hyperscaler capex is approaching limits**"* — with **[[NVDA]]'s Monday announcement and [[AVGO]]'s XPU announcement of June 9th** marking the new stage (Barclays "Charts Worth A Look: Unpacking AI Infrastructure 'Backstop 101'", 2026-08-13). **The limits argument, quantified: hyperscaler capex has borne $1.5 TRILLION SINCE 2023 (through '26) with several trillion more in coming years; the hyperscaler "FUNDING GAP" — operating cash flow less capex — COULD EXCEED $200BN IN '27; and the industry "has natural limits around debt levels and power agreements, and we seem to be approaching those limits in '27." [[GOOG]]'s equity raise — its first since the IPO — is cited as the milestone that stamps it.**
- 🔴 **THE CONVERGENCE WORTH STATING AS SUCH: Barclays' ">$200bn funding gap in '27" and GS's "debt share of capex PEAKS at 35% in 2027" (08-03, already on this page) are TWO INDEPENDENT HOUSES LANDING ON 2027 AS THE BINDING YEAR BY DIFFERENT ROUTES — one from the cash-flow identity, the other from a debt-intensity path with a monetisation-lag mechanism. Add MS's "$300bn of 2027 project finance" and the picture is three separate quantifications of the same year. ⚠️ That is convergence on the DATE, not on the number, and it should not be read as three confirmations of one estimate.** ⚠️ **Barclays' ">$1T backstopped" is a forward channel-size claim with no deal list behind it in the captured text — carry it as a sizing, not as committed capital, exactly as this page treats the [[NVDA]] $500bn platform.**

**⑫ ⚠️ THE $500BN PLATFORM GETS DECONSTRUCTED BY AN INDEPENDENT AUTHOR — AND THE ATTRIBUTION WARNING IS ITSELF THE LESSON.** ⚠️⚠️ **THE INGEST ROUTER TAGGED THIS FILE'S BROKER AS "GOLDMAN." IT IS FALSE, AND INSTRUCTIVELY SO: Goldman Sachs is NAMED IN THE BODY as one of the six financing partners, which is what the detector latched onto. The source is an INDEPENDENT PAID SUBSTACK (Ash & Seed Press · Shanaka Anslem Perera, "THE CONTINUITY STACK: NVIDIA Did Not Raise $500 Billion. Wall Street Is Financing the Right to Keep AI Billing.", 2026-08-18) with NO RATING AND NO PRICE TARGET. Do not lend it bank authority.**
- ✅ **ALREADY ON THIS PAGE, LOGGED AS CORROBORATION FROM A DIFFERENT SOURCE: the announcement date (2026-08-10), the six partners (Apollo, BlackRock, Blackstone, Brookfield, Goldman Sachs, KKR), the >$500bn figure, and the "possible 25 percent" residual-value support — this last one already carried from @ParadisLabs (08-18) and from the Aug-11 block on [ai-datacenter-power](ai-datacenter-power.md). ⚠️ Record the 25% as THIS AUTHOR'S CHARACTERISATION of "an undisclosed recovery instrument," explicitly NOT a disclosed NVIDIA term — two independent non-company sources now describe it the same way, which raises confidence that the number is circulating, not that it is disclosed.**
- 🔴 **NET-NEW AND WORTH HAVING, BECAUSE IT IS THE MOST DISCIPLINED STATEMENT OF WHAT THE $500BN IS NOT: *"$500 Billion Is a Market Design, Not a Cash Balance."* The author's central claim is that it was **"not revenue, committed capital, a single fund, or money assigned to one customer,"** that **the memoranda of understanding remain subject to final agreements**, and that the platforms mobilise third-party capital over time and *"create neither $500 billion of orders nor cash at NVIDIA's disposal. Each project still requires underwriting, documentation, funding, construction, power, acceptance, and a paying customer."* What it DOES do: *"NVIDIA is trying to make that process REPEATABLE."*** ➤ **This page has carried the platform as "mobilization capacity, not committed capital" since 08-10; this is the fullest articulation of that distinction and it should be the standing framing. The author's own measurement caveat is the reusable part: *"maximum exposure, commitment, designed capacity, fair value, expected loss, revenue, and funded notional are DISTINCT MEASURES"* — six different numbers that headlines routinely collapse into one.**
- **The one dated test he names: 2026-08-26, [[NVDA]]'s print, as *"the first formal test."*** **And what he says would actually settle it: *"Executed term sheets can expose support and price. Operating data can expose the cash. A default can expose the recovery."*** ⚠️ **He labels his own references to circularity, concentration, wrong-way risk and opacity as *"analytical descriptions"* alleging no misconduct — carried with that caveat intact.** **The PORTS-Pike / SB Energy / [[OPENAI]] 8 GW structure detailed in the same piece is filed on [ai-datacenter-power](ai-datacenter-power.md), where this wiki already carries the signed terms; not duplicated here.**

**⑬ 🔴🔴 ADDED IN THE 2026-08-21 RUN — THE FOURTH INDEPENDENT MEASUREMENT OF THE AI FUNDING FLOW, AND THE FIRST ONE THAT SPANS *EVERY* ASSET CLASS IN ONE TABLE: BARCLAYS TRACKS ~$338BN OF AI/DIGITAL-INFRASTRUCTURE FINANCING YTD ACROSS IG, HY, ABS/CMBS *AND* CHIP/HARDWARE FACILITIES, AGAINST $179BN IN FULL-YEAR 2025 — AN INCREMENTAL $118BN IN THE TEN WEEKS SINCE THE DESK'S FIRST NOTE ON JUNE 9** (Barclays FICC / Credit Research · Andrew Keches CFA / Jeff Harlib / Lea Overby / Elana Lipchak CFA / Remy Neuhaus / Joseph Mulherin / Pulkit Khandelwal, "AI Infrastructure Financing: Tracking Issuance Across Asset Classes: August 2026 Update", 2026-08-20). ⚠️ **The note's own summary line says "~$340bn" while the body and Figure 1 say $338bn ($337,752mn); the body figure is used here and the rounding discrepancy is recorded rather than smoothed.**
- 💰 **THE FULL TABLE, prior-year-YTD → current-YTD ($mn), with each channel's share of its own asset class: INVESTMENT GRADE 133,794 → 224,640 (17% of the $1,335,598 IG asset class) — of which HYPERSCALER BONDS 106,500 → 182,000 and DATA CENTER BONDS 27,294 → 42,640 · HIGH YIELD 12,583 → 39,856 (18% of a $223,400 class) — of which data center bonds 8,583 → 33,606 and neocloud bonds 4,000 → 6,250 · SECURITIZED 26,627 → 18,056 (5%) — ABS 15,482 → 11,880 and CMBS 11,145 → 6,176 · HPC HARDWARE FINANCING 5,600 → 55,200 · TOTAL USD 178,604 → 337,752.** ➤ **Read the composition, not just the total: securitized issuance actually FELL (−32%) while HPC hardware financing went up ~10x. The growth is not "more of the same market" — it is a rotation into the chip-collateral channel, which is the youngest and least-tested part of the stack.**
- 🔴 **THE MOST QUOTABLE CONCENTRATION MARKER ON THIS PAGE: "Hyperscalers account for 14% OF USD IG CORPORATE SUPPLY YTD (~17% INCLUDING DATA CENTER BONDS)."** And the sharper cut inside the body: **"hyperscaler supply has totaled 21% OF IG NON-FINANCIAL SUPPLY in 2026, while Tech (inclusive of META/AMZN/SPCX) has been 37% of that total, WHICH IF MAINTAINED AT THAT LEVEL WOULD BE A RECORD FOR A SINGLE SECTOR."** ➤ **This is the crowding measure the ~$510bn index-absorption ceiling logged on 08-18 was reaching for, expressed as a realised share rather than a forecast headroom — and it is the number to quote when the question is "how much of the IG market is now one theme."**
- **THE FORWARD MARK: "issuance needs have been largely met for 2026, [but] issuers may choose to issue opportunistically ahead of needs, and we forecast ~$285BN OF HYPERSCALER ISSUANCE BY YEAR END" (USD-equivalent across currencies, against ~$248bn already done). Barclays' own investor survey puts 2026 hyperscaler supply at $275-325BN, with respondents saying "cadence and supply will be the key driver of performance over the next twelve months."** ✅ **That brackets UBS's $275-285bn and GS's implied ~$250bn already on this page — three houses inside a ~$75bn band on the same measure, which is as close to a settled number as this page has.** Duration: **~32% front-end (1-6y), ~32% belly (7-15y), ~36% long end (15y+), including ~$88bn (~36% of the YTD total) at 30 years and out** — the sector-level restatement of the duration-concentration thread already on this page, from a fourth desk.
- 🔴 **THE PER-ISSUER TABLE, AND THE MOST DECISION-RELEVANT CELL IN IT IS A ZERO: [[MSFT]] HAS ISSUED $0 OF IG DEBT YTD — no USD, no foreign currency.** The rest, all currencies ($mn): **[[AMZN]] 62,000 USD + 30,401 foreign = 92,401 · [[GOOG]] 45,000 + 35,313 = 80,313 · [[META]] 25,000 + 0 · [[ORCL]] 25,000 + 0 · [[SPCX]] 25,000 + 0 · total 182,000 USD + 65,714 foreign = 247,714.** ➤ **Two readings this page should carry. First, AMZN and GOOGL have each issued more IG debt YTD than META, ORCL and SPCX COMBINED — and they are the only two using the non-USD relief valve (already logged from MS on 08-20; Barclays supplies the per-issuer split behind it, including the CHF, EUR, CAD, JPY, GBP and AUD legs). Second, Microsoft is funding the largest capex programme on the page entirely without new IG bonds — which is the cleanest available piece of evidence for the [[MSFT]] IR position logged 08-13 that the FCF-positive commitment is "not… a line in the sand" but also not being met by leaning on the bond market.**
- ⚠️🔴 **THE RECONCILIATION THAT MATTERS, BECAUSE THIS PAGE NOW CARRIES FOUR "AI FINANCING" TOTALS AND THEY ARE FOUR DIFFERENT PERIMETERS. (1) Barclays $338bn = IG + HY + ABS/CMBS + HPC hardware, USD only, AI/digital infrastructure broadly. (2) MS ~$445bn YTD "across channels", which additionally counts non-USD IG hyperscaler supply and leveraged loans. (3) GS $194bn (as of July 25) = HYPERSCALER BONDS ONLY, five names, EXCLUDING SPV data-centre financings. (4) The ~$510bn USIG absorption ceiling is a CAPACITY figure, not a flow. ✅ AND THE ONE PLACE THEY TIE EXACTLY: Barclays' $182bn of USD hyperscaler bonds equals MS's YTD buckets of HQ hyperscalers 132 + ORCL 25 + SPCX 25 = 182. Two desks, same definition, same number — which is worth knowing precisely because almost nothing else on this page reconciles to the dollar.** ➤ **Rule for this page: never subtract or add across the four totals; always name the perimeter.**
- 🔴 **DATA CENTER BONDS — THE CHANNEL IS NOW INSTITUTIONALISED, WITH A STANDARD STRUCTURE AND A VISIBLE PIPELINE. YTD 2026 issuance ~$76.2bn ($33.6bn HY + $42.6bn IG) versus $35.9bn in all of 2025 ($8.6bn + $27.3bn) — representing 15% of total YTD USD HY issuance and 3% of IG. Total data-centre bonds OUTSTANDING went from $27.3bn (Oct-25) to $112.1bn (Aug-26 MTD), a ~4x expansion of an asset class in ten months.** Structures have **"standardized"** — **HY around ~5NC2 and partial-amortizing, IG with construction-risk mitigation and fully amortizing.** Pipeline: **"most developers have become repeat issuers"** with **~4.0GW of near-term pipeline tracked in HY alone**, and **Meta and Hut 8 have announced expansions of sites whose phase-one construction was already funded in the IG market (RPLDCI, HUTBPA).** ➤ **A repeat-issuer market with standard docs and a named GW pipeline is a different animal from the one-off project bonds this page was tracking in June; it means the marginal data centre is now financed by a functioning capital market rather than by a bespoke deal.**
- 🔴🔴 **CHIP FINANCING — THE CHANNEL BARCLAYS EXPECTS TO DOMINATE, WITH THE FULL FACILITY-BY-FACILITY TABLE. $68.6bn of visible HPC-hardware facilities issued to date, $55.2bn of it in 2026** — and Barclays' reason for expecting acceleration is a composition estimate this page should carry: **"HPC hardware comprises the majority of total AI data center capex (we estimate ~70%)."** The named facilities include **[[CRWV]] DDTL 4.0 at $8.5bn (March-26, counterparty [[META]] at $19bn+, rated Aa3/A, split $4.46bn floating at S+225 and $4.04bn fixed at 5.9%, 2032)**, the **AVGO/GOOGL JV facility at $34.2bn (June-26, counterparty [[ANTHROPIC]], private ratings A1 on $24bn and A2 on $4.2bn, tranches at 4.8% / 5.75% / 8.5%, 2028-2034)**, **[[CRWV]] DDTL 5.0 at $3.1bn (May-26, S+450, counterparties [[OPENAI]] $6.5bn and Cohere $1.0bn) — the FIRST broadly syndicated chip facility** — plus **[[CRWV]] DDTL 5.5 $2.6bn at S+550 (Aug-26)**, **Lambda DDTL 2.0 $926mn at S+300 against an undisclosed IG customer (Baa2)**, **IREN DDTL 1.0 $3.7bn against [[MSFT]] (A/A− Fitch/DBRS, S+213/S+225)**, **Nebius DDTL 1.0 $0.8bn at S+250**, and **Nscale DDTL 1.0 $1.4bn**. ➤ **The trend Barclays names is the one that matters: these instruments "to date have mainly come in the private credit market (limiting visibility to the total universe)" and are now being broadly syndicated — "a repeatable template for all neoclouds to potentially access funding at attractive rates." Compare the spread compression across CoreWeave's own facilities: S+962 (2023) → S+600/650/1,300 (2024) → S+400/425 (2025) → S+225 fixed-and-floating (March-26) → S+450 (May) → S+550 (Aug).** ⚠️ **NOT THE SAME SERIES AS MS's "chip financing $0 → $35bn" logged in item ③ above: MS counts only the Section 4(a)(2) private-placement channel, Barclays counts all visible DDTL facilities including syndicated and private-credit. Complementary perimeters, not a disagreement — and Barclays says the private universe is not fully visible either way.**
- **HY NEOCLOUDS AND SECURITIZATION, both with a ceiling attached. HY: "the only neocloud to have accessed the regular-way HY market is CoreWeave ($10bn total); with CRWV unsecured yields NOW IN THE 11% RANGE, we view it and other emerging neoclouds as UNLIKELY TO ISSUE HY DEBT IN THE NEAR TERM."** ➤ **An 11% unsecured yield is a closed door, and it explains mechanically why the flow has rotated into secured chip facilities and project bonds. Read alongside the ~300-600bp offtaker spread in item ① — the same credit hierarchy showing up in a different instrument.** **ABS/CMBS: the data-centre securitization market has grown to >$66bn outstanding with $18bn of YTD volume, but Barclays frames it as "a financing alternative for STABILIZED ASSETS" where "market size/depth is limited to small to mid-sized financings" — data-centre CMBS is 9% of 2026 SASB CMBS issuance and data-centre ABS 13% of esoteric ABS.** Also net-new: **chip financing "is beginning to emerge in IG as well, following the recent AVGO/Blackstone deal."**
- ⚠️⚠️ **BASIS GUARDRAIL, AND IT IS THE MOST IMPORTANT LINE IN THIS ITEM: every figure above is a FINANCING FLOW — bonds and loans issued — not capex. It must never be added to, netted against, or compared with the `## State of play` capex table, and $338bn of financing does not fund $338bn of capex in the same year (the AVGO/GOOGL facility alone runs to 2034). The only legitimate joins are flow-to-flow: against MS's ~$445bn, GS's $194bn, UBS's $275-285bn and the ~$510bn absorption ceiling, each with its perimeter named.**

## Recent signals (Aug 19 ingest; source recorded Aug 3) — 🔴🔴 **THE SAME GS CREDIT DESK, ON THE RECORD AND IN MORE DETAIL: THE DEBT-FINANCED SHARE OF HYPERSCALER CAPEX IS PUT ON A PATH WITH A *PEAK YEAR* — 27% (2025) → ~33% (2026) → **35% IN 2027** — PLUS $300BN OF PROJECT FINANCE IN 2027 ON TOP, AND THE FIRST HARD EVIDENCE THAT THE BUY SIDE IS ALREADY PUSHING BACK (INSURANCE 30-YEAR ORDERS HALVED, CONCESSIONS 2-3BP → 20BP, THE AI BASKET 74BP → ~2×)**

_(Source: **Goldman Sachs Exchanges** podcast, *"How AI Debt Is Reshaping Credit Markets"* — **recorded Monday 2026-08-03**, host Allison Nathan with **Amanda Lynam** (leads GS credit strategy research) and **Zach Ablon** (GS credit sales desk, Global Banking & Markets). ⚠️ **Machine transcript** — it renders the strategist's surname as "Lynham"; this wiki uses **Lynam**, matching the 07-09 GS credit note already on this page. Podcast commentary: no ratings, no PTs, no company estimates. ⚠️ **Recorded 08-03 and ingested 08-19 — two weeks stale, and the spread/order-book colour is the most perishable part.**)_

**⚠️ READ THIS AGAINST THE 07-09 GS NOTE ALREADY ON THIS PAGE (Aug 18 block below) — SAME DESK, AND TWO OF ITS HEADLINE NUMBERS ARE THE SAME, SO THEY ARE CORROBORATION AND NOT INDEPENDENT CONFIRMATION: the **~$510bn** incremental USD-IG capacity ceiling and the **$194bn** hyperscaler YTD issuance both already appear there. What follows is what the podcast ADDS.** ✅ **Internal arithmetic check that passes: hyperscalers are said to be **~40% of ~$500bn** of tracked AI-related issuance ⇒ ~$200bn, against the **$194bn** stated separately. Consistent.**

- 🔴🔴 **NET-NEW AND THE MOST USEFUL ITEM: A DEBT-SHARE-OF-CAPEX PATH WITH A PEAK.** GS frames it as a percentage of capex rather than a dollar estimate *"given the pace of upward revision to capex estimates."* The path: **2025's $108bn of issuance = 27% of hyperscaler capex; 2026 ≈ 33% (one third), implying ~$250bn of DIRECT hyperscaler supply (project finance excluded); and 2027 PEAKS at 35%.** ➤ **The stated mechanism for the peak is a monetisation lag: "there's a delay between when they invest and when they can monetize that investment of anywhere from three months to two years"** — i.e. the debt share peaks while capex is still climbing, because cash conversion arrives later. **This page has had the funding-gap arithmetic for weeks; what it has NOT had is a peak year for the debt intensity. Now it does.**
- 🔴 **PROJECT FINANCE IS SIZED SEPARATELY AND IT IS BIG: "$300 billion in 2027" of project-finance and data-centre transactions, "above and beyond the direct issuance from the hyperscalers."** ➤ Pair with the WSJ ~$3tn off-balance-sheet item (Aug 17 block) and the [[NVDA]] $500bn third-party-capital platform (Aug 18): the **off-balance-sheet channel now has a 2027 flow number**, not just a stock.
- 🔴 **THE ECOSYSTEM IS 2.5× THE HYPERSCALERS: "nearly $500 billion of AI-related debt issuance" tracked YTD, of which "the hyperscalers have actually only been 40%."** ➤ **The concentration is in the THEME, not in the issuer list** — which is precisely why issuer-level concentration limits under-describe the risk. **IG share progression, net-new: $10bn = 1% of supply (2024) → $108bn ≈ 7% (2025) → ~18% (2026 YTD).**
- 🔴🔴 **THE DURATION POINT, AND IT IS THE ONE THIS PAGE WAS MISSING: "40% of 15-year-plus issuance in IG this year has been by AI companies or companies that are funding the AI thematic."** And the index has re-shaped around it: **[[AMZN]] is now the HIGHEST duration weight in the IG index (it was #20 last year); [[GOOG]] is now 18th (it was 86th).** ➤ **This is the mechanical link to the duration-sell-off thread on [macro-cycle](macro-cycle.md) (Aug 18): AI issuance is not just large, it is concentrated at the long end, which is where it collides with insurance/real-money duration budgets.**
- 🔴 **THEORETICAL CAPACITY vs WHAT THE MARKET WILL ACTUALLY TAKE — the distinction that matters, and GS makes it explicitly.** Taking the hyperscalers to **2× net / 3× gross leverage** (*"very reasonable... for IG ratings"*) implies **~$2 TRILLION of incremental debt capacity.** **"We actually don't think that's the binding constraint. The binding constraint in our view is how much can the USIG market reasonably absorb."** The benchmark behind the ~$510bn already on this page: **the three largest USIG issuers are US banks, and none has more than ~$175bn of index-eligible debt or more than ~2.3% of the index.** ➤ **So the gap between $2tn of RATINGS capacity and ~$510bn of ABSORPTION capacity is the whole reason private credit, non-USD markets and SPVs exist in this trade.**
- ⚠️🔴 **AND THE ~$510BN IS ITSELF PROBABLY GENEROUS — two client pushbacks GS reports, both of which cut the number DOWN.** **(1) Cross-asset exposure: clients "have a lot more equity exposure to the tech sector than they do to banks," so when they aggregate AI exposure across the whole institution it "tempers demand" for AI-related debt** — a dollar of hyperscaler IG is not fungible with a dollar of bank IG for an allocator who is already long the theme in equities. **(2) Duration is not dollar-for-dollar: if supply keeps skewing long, "the $510 billion of debt capacity may not be that large."** ➤ **Treat ~$510bn as a CEILING with a haircut, not a target.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE BUY-SIDE IS ALREADY PUSHING BACK, AND THE DESK PUTS THREE NUMBERS ON IT (Ablon) — this is the most perishable but most actionable part.** **(a) Spreads: the GS "AI leaders" basket has gone from tights of 74bp to "nearly twice that" over 12 months** — *"the marketplace has clearly expressed some indigestion with some of this financing."* **(b) Insurance demand at the long end: on large Q1 AI financings there were "anywhere from 15 insurance clients coming in to the 30-year part of that bond complex with $50 million plus orders"; by end-Q2 "that number was about half as big."** ⚠️ *"Real money... are clearly losing their appetite for this risk"* — performance, concentration limits, and rising capex all cited. **(c) New-issue concessions went from 2-3bp pre-indigestion to as wide as 20BP on "a very large hyperscaler deal."** ➤ **Concessions are the cleanest single tell to track, and the desk names them as its own signpost.**
- 🔴 **HIGH YIELD IS RUNNING THE SAME CONCENTRATION (~18% of YTD supply) AND HAS NOW STARTED TO CRACK: "17 out of the 23 data center JV deals are now trading wide to originated yields."** Structure notes: **most of these are 5-year** (vs the long-dated IG paper), deals are multi-billion so they have **pushed the average US HY deal size up materially**, and **BB (~165bp) "screens as very rich"** against data-centre deals *"coming significantly wider than that that have IG wrappers."* **Chip financing is flagged as lower-duration than data-centre financing.** ➤ Leveraged finance is no longer a bystander to this theme.
- ✅ **WHERE THE VOID GETS FILLED — the "waterfall of capital" framing, with a number: ~$4.5 TRILLION of dry powder across private credit, infrastructure, real estate and PE globally.** Lines are blurring (data-centre financing shows up as private credit, some as venture debt, some as PE) — GS is *"less focused on the actual categories... but more how much financing firepower is there."* Also: **non-USD markets already used (CAD, sterling, AUD, yen) with EUROPE explicitly UNDER-CONTRIBUTING = "an obvious choice"**, plus **ABS/structured credit once data centres are finished** (GS's Arun Manohar). ➤ **GS's bottom line, and it is not alarmist: "we're not concerned about access to capital for this theme" — the question is WHERE the risk gets taken and AT WHAT PRICE.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE POSITIONING CONCLUSION MOST LIKELY TO BE MIS-READ, AND IT INVERTS THE OBVIOUS HEDGE (Ablon): if capex slows, CREDIT CAN RALLY WHILE EQUITIES FALL.** *"I actually see a scenario where credit will rally... I can see a scenario where the hyperscalers rally in equities and go significantly tighter in credit. And so that'll be an interesting event. And for those thinking that credit is just a natural hedge and really asymmetric, I think there's some truth to that. But you're going to have to watch how things unfold because there are scenarios where credit will go tighter and stocks will go down."* ➤ **Mechanism: less capex = less supply = less indigestion, even as the equity story de-rates. So being short AI credit is NOT a clean expression of a capex-deceleration view.** Cross-filed to [macro-cycle](macro-cycle.md).
- ➤ **THE SIGNPOSTS THE DESK ITSELF NAMES: (1) the SECOND DERIVATIVE of capex — "at what point do CapEx numbers stop going up as much as they've been going up"; (2) new-issue concessions on upcoming hyperscaler deals; (3) whether issuers give GUIDEPOSTS.** Lynam's framing of the last one is the structural point: *"the credit market works best in funding periods of active re-leveraging when there's an end in sight, it's quantifiable, and ideally there's a period of time where companies can grow into their capital structures"* — and today **the hyperscalers publish neither ratings targets nor leverage targets** (GS's own review), which is what lets them add leverage strategically but also what denies credit investors the endpoint they need. **"If I'm issuing in this quarter and I'm committing to not coming back for the next few quarters, I think that could really give investors some confidence."** ⚠️ **Falsifier for the whole block: a hyperscaler publishing an explicit leverage or ratings target, or a 2027 issuance calendar.**

## Recent signals (Aug 19, /wiki-ingest) — 🔴 **THE CONVERTIBLE MARKET PRICES AI-DATACENTER DURATION EXPLICITLY: [[NBIS]] PAYS ~0% FOR 3.5 YEARS AND 4.0-4.50% CASH FOR 7.5 YEARS — AND CONSENSUS GOOGLE CAPEX GETS PUT AT $1.5-2.0 TRILLION FOR CY27-31**

- 🔴 **The financing datapoint, and it is the cleanest term-structure read this page has: [[NBIS]] launched $4.5bn of convertible senior notes on 2026-08-19 (UBS joint bookrunner) — $2,750mn due 2030-02-15 (3.5yr) at a 0.0-0.50% coupon with a 40.0-45.0% conversion premium, and $1,750mn due 2034-02-15 (7.5yr) at a 4.0-4.50% coupon with a 45.0-50.0% premium. Shares extended their decline to 7% premarket.** **Read the shape: the market will fund 3.5 years of this build essentially for free on equity optionality alone, but demands a REAL mid-single-digit cash coupon AND a higher premium to go out 7.5 years. Convert buyers are not willing to underwrite 2034 AI-datacenter cash flows on optionality — which is a financing-conditions signal for every name funding GW-scale build in the capital markets, not just for Nebius.**
- 🔴 **A consensus capex anchor for a single hyperscaler, published in the context of the [[MRVL]]/[[GOOG]] deal (BofA · Arya, 2026-08-19): consensus expects [[GOOG]] to spend $1.5-2.0tn+ in AGGREGATE capex over CY27-31, with ~25-30% of it on TPU SYSTEMS — a $400-600bn+ TAM — versus a historical 20-25% share, the increase attributed to a rising mix of servers, compute and HBM.** **That composition shift (silicon systems taking a larger share of total capex) is the same directional claim this page carries from BofA's Aug-13 CY30 composition table, now stated for one name over a five-year window.**
- **The [[AVGO]] preview attaches customer-level GW figures to the same period: new [[META]] and [[OPENAI]] custom ramps at ~1GW each at >$10bn, beginning CY27, on top of the TPU base** (BofA · Arya, 2026-08-19). ⚠️ **And the expectations gap on this page's most-tracked AI revenue line: consensus already models ~$120bn of AVGO FY27 AI sales versus management's ">$100bn" guide — the buy-side is carrying ~20% above the company's own number into the 2026-09-02 print.**
- **The siting/permitting risk gets a specific counterexample rather than a general reassurance (@MF_Camillus, 2026-08-19): the Pennsylvania data-centre pushback is "probably some degree of pre-Midterms political posturing," and "[[NBIS]]'s Pennsylvania location… was selected partly because it ALREADY HAD PROACTIVE LOCAL ZONING permitting DC development, so the project may actually be among the BETTER-INSULATED large projects in the state" — with the further point that "if a few states do become increasingly problematic, a number of others would likely pick u[p the slack]."** Bernstein carried the same PA pushback the same day and scored it **neutral ("=")**; a **PA executive order on data-centre regulation** was also circulating (Axios, via Dauvin Peterson).
- **A capacity-side read on where the money lands (Jefferies · Sherman via the O'Mara desk note, 2026-08-19): TSMC CoWoS 70kwpm at end-2025 → 120 / 180 / 220 kwpm by end-2026/27/28, and ASE/SPIL OS capacity 35 → 85 / 145 / 185 kwpm over the same period.** ⚠️ A broker model built to value an equipment supplier, published one day after TSMC IR declined to give any capacity number — see [cowos-packaging](cowos-packaging.md).
- ⚠️ **The sentiment fragility that framed the whole session, stated by a bull (@MF_Camillus, 2026-08-19): "even many self-described AI bulls are still operating on surprisingly fragile assumptions that get shaken very quickly by just one day of bad price action or a poorly framed article on WSJ or FT. First, OpenAI's numbers are improving, but OAI'S ECONOMICS IS NOT AI ECONOMICS… plain and simple."** The −5.6% SOX / −5.8% KOSPI session was attributed to the long-bond sell-off plus an [[ANTHROPIC]] ARR figure (~$65bn end-July) **whose reporting basis is itself unverified** — see [tokenmaxxing](tokenmaxxing.md).

## Recent signals (Aug 18, /run-inbox) — 🔴 **GOLDMAN'S CREDIT DESK SIZES THE FULL BILL AT $5.8 TRILLION (FY2025-FY2030, FIVE NAMES) AND SAYS THIS YEAR'S CONSENSUS CAPEX ALREADY CONSUMES THE MAJORITY OF HYPERSCALER OPERATING CASH FLOW — SO DEBT IS NOT OPTIONAL**

_(Source: **Goldman Sachs Credit Strategy · Amanda Lynam** w/ Spencer Rogers, Sara Grut, Shamshad Ali — "Global Credit Trader: AI-related financing — A mix of markets will be required", 2026-07-09 — [link](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/458094ae-fd80-435d-b209-406bcfab4123.html). ⚠️ **CREDIT-STRATEGY note: no ratings, no PTs, no company estimates. Dated 07-09, swept late from P: — the sizing is the value.**)_

- 🔴 **THE HEADLINE SIZING, sourced to GS equity research and quoted by the credit desk: the FIVE hyperscalers — [[MSFT]], [[AMZN]], [[META]], [[GOOG]] and [[ORCL]] — invest a combined $5.8 TRILLION in AI capex over FY2025-FY2030. "This year's consensus capex estimates ALREADY ACCOUNT FOR THE MAJORITY OF HYPERSCALERS' OPERATING CASH FLOW," and GS believes these figures "likely UNDERSTATE the broader AI-related spending and investment across a range of other sectors, which may require incremental debt borrowing."** ➤ **Note which five: [[ORCL]] is inside the perimeter and [[AAPL]] and [[TSLA]] are outside it. Any $/GW or memory-share cross-check run off "five hyperscalers" on this wiki must use this membership or say which one it uses — the composition differs across the notes on this page.**
- 🔴 **THE FUNDING GAP, STATED AS ARITHMETIC RATHER THAN OPINION: if consensus capex already absorbs most of operating cash flow, incremental spend must come from debt — but the credit desk's own ceiling analysis puts only ~$510BN of additional hyperscaler debt capacity in the USD IG market before index-concentration conventions bind (using the largest US Banks as the upper-bound case). Hyperscalers have issued $194bn across currencies YTD, ~9% of ALL global IG supply, with 32% priced outside USD.** ➤ **$5.8tn of spend, most of internally-generated cash already committed, and under ~$510bn of incremental IG headroom. The gap is the entire argument for project-finance SPVs, private infrastructure capital and vendor-supported structures — see [themes/ai-datacenter-power](ai-datacenter-power.md) for the deal-level versions this wiki already carries.**
- ⚠️ **THE NUMBER THAT NEEDS RECONCILING AGAINST THIS ONE, and it is the sharpest open question from this run: UBS puts MEMORY INDUSTRY REVENUES ALONE at ~US$1.61 TRILLION in 2027E ([themes/hbm-memory](hbm-memory.md), UBS/Gaudois 08-07). Against $5.8tn of TOTAL hyperscaler capex across SIX YEARS, a single component category running $1.6tn in ONE year cannot be accommodated by any capex composition model currently on this wiki.** ➤ **One of three things must be true: memory revenue forecasts are too high; hyperscaler capex forecasts are too low; or memory is being sold well beyond the five hyperscalers (sovereign, neocloud, enterprise, consumer) in proportions nobody here has sized. Flagged as the run's most important unreconciled pair — it is not resolvable from either note alone.**

Carried as a magnitude, not a mark.**

## Recent signals (Aug 18, /wiki-ingest) — **THE NEAR-TERM FAILURE MODE IS A DURATION MISMATCH, NOT AN ABSENCE OF DEMAND**

**Ben Thompson's capital curve is the clean synthesis: hyperscalers started with free cash flow, moved rapidly through debt, Alphabet issued equity, and NVIDIA's $500bn structure reaches pension/insurance capital; the next required step is for AI revenue to rotate the system back to free-cash-flow funding.** The risk is an "air gap" before that handoff. Today's compute shortage reflects underinvestment in 2023-24, while capital committed now becomes usable compute mainly in 2028-29; measuring payback during scarcity can therefore overstate returns when simultaneous builds arrive. Once shells are built, fixed-cost owners have the classic commodity incentive to fill them down toward marginal cost. Even a financing break would not end AI, but it could transfer assets and destroy equity before demand catches up (Ben Thompson, *Invest Like the Best*, 2026-08-18). **No canonical capex figure changed; this is a timing/market-structure framework around the existing table.**
## Recent signals (Aug 17, /wiki-ingest) — 🔴🔴 **WSJ PUTS A NUMBER ON WHAT IS *NOT* ON THE BALANCE SHEETS: ~$3 TRILLION OF OFF-BALANCE-SHEET AI OBLIGATIONS ACROSS NINE NAMES VS ~$600BN OF ACTUAL CAPEX IN THE LAST YEAR — WITH [[META]] THE POSTER CHILD AT $347BN OF *UNCOMMENCED* LEASES**

**1. The claim, and what is genuinely new in it.** **"Nine of the largest AI spenders are carrying roughly $3T of off-balance-sheet commitments — including leases that haven't yet started and purchase obligations — versus ~$600bn of actual capex over the past year. WSJ called out META as the poster child, with $347BN OF UNCOMMENCED LEASES. The Hyperion build in Louisiana sits inside a Blue Owl-majority JV, meaning NEITHER the campus NOR the $27bn financing shows up on Meta's balance sheet, while Meta's rent services the bonds and THE MAKE-WHOLE GUARANTEE IS NOT BOOKED AS A LIABILITY"** (WSJ via TMT Breakout, 2026-08-17). JPM's desk summary of the same piece: "these structures let companies like **Alphabet, Meta, and Oracle** lock in massive future capacity while keeping the debt technically off-books, even as capex and negative free cash flow already raise questions about sustainability. **If AI demand doesn't materialize as expected, these largely NON-CANCELABLE commitments could turn into a very real, very hidden financial burden**" (JPM · Joshua Meyers, "Chips for Breakfast", 2026-08-17).
- ⚠️ **Three things this page should keep straight before the number gets quoted as leverage: (1) uncommenced leases are a DISCLOSED footnote item under ASC 842 — the complaint is about balance-sheet GEOGRAPHY, not concealment; (2) $347bn is a gross, undiscounted commitment across a multi-decade term, and setting it against ONE YEAR of industry capex is a duration mismatch that flatters the ratio; (3) the genuinely new and genuinely concerning item is the MAKE-WHOLE GUARANTEE not being booked as a liability — that is a contingent obligation, not a lease, and it is the piece that would convert a demand shortfall into a cash claim.**
- **META −3.5% on the day** and the name the desk got most questions on (TMT Breakout EOD, 2026-08-17).

**2. The financing machine keeps scaling — and one guarantee just got SMALLER.** **[[NVDA]]/[[OPENAI]]'s Ohio campus signed with the guarantee cut from the $250bn that leaked on 08-16 to "less than $120bn" for the first ~5 GW phase (Reuters: up to $105bn)**, against ~8 GW-IT under a 20-year SB Energy lease with first capacity in 2028 (WSJ / @nvidianewsroom / @firstadopter, 2026-08-16/17 — full structure on [themes/ai-datacenter-power.md](ai-datacenter-power.md)). ➜ **This is the first datapoint in this cycle where a vendor-financing commitment was revised DOWN before signature, explicitly "to address investors' concerns about its risk exposure." Whether it marks a discipline inflection or a one-off is the thing to watch.** Scale of the debt leg: **"AI / Hyperscaler / Datacenter issuance (IG + Loans) is now tracking at roughly 12× the annual average of the entire 2015-2024 period — and already 2× all of 2025, at $269bn YTD"** (@zerohedge, 2026-08-16); Goldman "in talks with investors on Nvidia financing deal after landing prized role" (@FirstSquawk, 2026-08-16).

**3. The AWS bridge gets its capacity number, and Trainium gets a unit forecast.** MS's $1trn-AWS work (logged in the Aug 16 section above) rests on **~8 incremental GW/year and monetisation rising from ~$8 to $12-15 of REVENUE per incremental watt**; MS estimates **[[AMZN]] brings on 6 GW in '26 and 8 GW in '27**. ## Recent signals (Aug 16) — 🔴 **THE FIRST SIZED BRIDGE FROM "$1 TRILLION AWS" TO A SHARE PRICE — AND THE SWING VARIABLE IS *REVENUE PER INCREMENTAL WATT* ($8 TODAY → $12-15), NOT GIGAWATTS**

**Source: Morgan Stanley · Brian Nowak / Julian Herrera / Kavya Narayanan / Gregory Gao / Nikhil Javeri — "Amazon.com Inc: AWS's Path to $1 Trln of Revenue and How AMZN Shares Can Double" (2026-08-16). OW, base PT $335 unchanged; bull path ~$500 by YE27.**

- **THE STARTING POINT IS MANAGEMENT'S OWN, quoted from the Q2'26 call: "We long believed AWS could become a few hundred billion dollar revenue business and now believe it'll be at least double that, and **VERY POSSIBLY BE A $1 TRILLION ANNUAL REVENUE BUSINESS** for us in time with very appealing accompanying free cash flow and return on invested capital" — against **~$170BN ANNUALIZED TODAY (~6x to go)**. And on returns: "we see the **MARGINS AND RETURNS IN AI TRACKING WHAT WE SAW WITH CORE AT THE SAME POINT OF EVOLUTION. ACTUALLY A LITTLE AHEAD**."**
- 🔴 **THE CAPACITY LEG: MS estimates **[[AMZN]] BRINGS ON 6 GW IN '26 AND 8 GW IN '27**, and takes **~8 INCREMENTAL GW/YEAR** as the base case thereafter — while conceding "limited visibility into how many GW AMZN will be bringing online beyond '27 given unknown server/rack purchase volumes, improving server/rack power draws, evolving regulatory unknowns, and physical limits to faster data center openings like **LABOR, CONSTRUCTION, AND POWER**."** ➔ **Note the honesty: the GW leg is an assumption, and MS says the swing factor is NOT this leg.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE MONETIZATION LEG IS THE ONE THAT MATTERS, AND IT GIVES THIS PAGE A CLEAN NEW METRIC: "AWS is currently monetizing at **$8 PER INCREMENTAL WATT** (full year '26 estimate)"; at **$12/INCREMENTAL WATT $1TRN ARRIVES IN 2035**, and at **$14-15/INCREMENTAL WATT AS EARLY AS 2034**. MS's prior GenAI-ROIC work argued implied incremental revenue per incremental GW "should rise from the current high single digit range to double digit or teens levels."** ⚠️⚠️ **UNIT HAZARD, and this page has been burned by it before: "$/incremental watt" here is **REVENUE PER WATT** (monetization), NOT capex per watt and NOT build cost. Do not net it against the $50-60bn/GW BUILD-COST anchors on [[themes/ai-datacenter-power]], and do not chain it into a $/GW capex series.**
- **THE MARGIN + VALUATION LEGS: if AI tracks core cloud at **~30% LT EBIT MARGIN**, $1trn of AWS revenue implies **~$300BN OF AWS EBIT** and, with reasonable retail assumptions (8% retail revenue CAGR 2030-36, 14% LT retail EBIT margin), **~$500BN OF TOTAL AMZN EBIT IN 2034-36** — a **16-20% company-wide EBIT CAGR**. Capitalise that at **~21x EV/EBIT** (a ~10% discount to the ~23x peer average of GOOGL/MSFT/META/WMT/COST/NFLX) and discount at a **10% WACC to '28** → **~$500/SHARE BY YE27, 80-100% UPSIDE**. Stated inversely: AMZN trades at **~11x** that discounted '35 EBIT (~$265bn), "**a ~50% DISCOUNT to its peers**."**
- ⚠️ **WHAT IS LOAD-BEARING, stated plainly: the EBIT leg and the multiple leg BOTH rest on management's claim that AI-era margins track core cloud. If AI-era AWS margins settle materially below core — the case the GS cloud-margin decomposition on [[AMZN]] (08-12) and the "REALLY REALLY D&A" framing argue for — the two legs fail together, not independently. This is a LONG-DATED frame (2034-36 EBIT discounted to '28); it changes nothing about the '26/'27 capex print.**

**Adjacent, same window — CONTRACT DURATION becomes an explicit strategic choice (The Information · Catherine Perloff, 2026-08-16):**
- **"[[NBIS]] and [[CRWV]] both pitched investors on… **SHORTER-TERM CONTRACTS**," to "cash in on **SURGING PRICES FOR AI COMPUTE CAPACITY AMID A CRUNCH FOR GPUs**" — shares in both moved sharply higher. Against that, [[AMZN]] used its call to highlight that it sells **MOST AI CAPACITY VIA FIVE-YEAR DEALS**, which Jassy framed as what ensures "AWS will generate enough revenue to make money on its data center build-out" (AMZN **+11% since the 07-30 call**).**
- ➔ **For this page the relevance is that the two models make OPPOSITE bets on where compute pricing goes, and the market paid for both in the same week. Long contracts de-risk the depreciation schedule and forfeit spot upside; short contracts do the reverse. Whichever is right determines whether the current capex wave is being underwritten against contracted revenue or against a spot price that has to hold.** ⚠️ **Single-outlet trade press; no sizes, durations or prices disclosed.**
