### 🔴🔴 THE DURABLE ADDITION FROM THIS DATE — DATA-CENTRE **PROJECT FINANCE**, DOCUMENTED END-TO-END BY AN IG CREDIT DESK, AND THE SECTOR-LEVEL CLAIM THAT IG TECHNOLOGY HAS GONE FROM THE LOW-BETA ANCHOR TO ONE OF THE LARGEST SOURCES OF SUPPLY RISK IN INVESTMENT GRADE

**(J.P. Morgan North America CREDIT Research · Erica R. Spear / Quinn Degen, "The Compute Must Flow: Project Finance 101 & Hyperscaler Ratings Refresh", 2026-07-07, 41pp)** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260707_JP_Morgan_ORCL_The_Compute_Must_Flow-_Project_Finance_101_-_Hyperscaler.html)

⚠️⚠️ **TWO FRAMING FLAGS BEFORE ANY NUMBER HERE IS QUOTED. (1) THIS IS INVESTMENT-GRADE *CREDIT* RESEARCH: it carries CREDIT ratings (Overweight / Neutral / Underweight on bonds and issuers) and spread views — **NO equity ratings and NO price targets**. Never present a JPM credit opinion as an equity rating. (2) **INGESTED ~2 MONTHS LATE (source 2026-07-07, folded 2026-09-01)** — every level, spread and issuance figure below is a JULY mark and is superseded by the August credit blocks already above on this page. It is folded in for the MECHANICS, which do not perish.**

- 🔴🔴 **THE SECTOR-LEVEL CLAIM, IN THE DESK'S OWN FRAMING: *"Two years ago, the IG Technology sector was where credit investors went for safety and spread stability. Today, it is one of the largest sources of supply risk in investment grade, with the names that once anchored the low-beta end of the market now setting the direction for the broader sector."*** JPM attributes the shift to *"the rapid emergence of data center project finance."* ➤ **This is the credit-market mirror of this page's whole thesis: the capex is now large enough that the buyers' own paper is the marginal risk asset in IG.**
- 🔴 **THE MARKET, SIZED AND NAMED DEAL BY DEAL (as of 2026-07-07): FIVE project-finance transactions tied to AI data centres have been financed with IG 144A senior secured notes, totalling $53bn of new supply.** The first was the **$27bn RPLDCI amortising bond, priced "last October"**; since then **$14bn RDMICH**, **$4.25bn HUTBPA** and **$3.25bn HUTRBA** (all amortising), plus a **$4.6bn BULLET from QTSQST**. ⚠️ **BASIS DISCIPLINE — DO NOT NET THIS AGAINST THE DATA-CENTRE-BOND NUMBERS ALREADY ON THIS PAGE: JPM's $53bn is the IG-144A *project-finance* perimeter only. Barclays' ~$76.2bn YTD-2026 data-centre bond figure (08-20, above) spans IG *and* HY across a wider set of structures, and Barclays' ~$338bn spans every asset class. Three different perimeters.**
- 🔴 **WHY IT EXISTS, WHICH IS THE PART THIS PAGE NEEDS: project finance *"does not eliminate the sector's funding needs"* — it shifts a portion of future issuance to project-level financings backed by contracted cash flows**, borrowing against the cash flows of a specific project rather than the sponsor's balance sheet, through a **bankruptcy-remote SPV** with **ring-fenced cash flows** and **non-recourse (or limited-recourse) debt**. JPM expects both issuance and the investable universe to keep expanding *"as additional campuses are announced and more projects become financeable."* Historically these assets were financed through **CMBS / ABS**; they have migrated to the **IG 144A** market as hyperscaler projects took on corporate-like credit characteristics (IG counterparties, long-dated triple-net leases, predictable cash flows, robust structural protections).
- 🔴🔴 **THE STRUCTURE INVESTORS HAVE REVEALED THEY WANT — usable as a checklist against any new deal this wiki logs: a long-term triple-net lease with a high-quality IG tenant, fully amortising debt, no incremental debt capacity, no termination for convenience and limited termination for cause.** Also valued: robust insurance, **fully procured power**, conservative capitalisation, and liquidity sufficient to absorb construction or operating disruption. **But: general-contractor guarantees, guaranteed-maximum-price contracts and RESIDUAL VALUE GUARANTEES *"provide additional protection but are not essential for a transaction to perform in the market."*** ➤ **That last clause bears directly on the residual-value-support debate this page has run since the [[NVDA]] $500bn-MoU block (08-24): as of July the IG market was not requiring an RVG to clear a deal.**
- 🔴 **AND THE PRICE OF STRUCTURE, OBSERVED RATHER THAN ASSERTED: *"Trading performance to date suggests that investors place a meaningful premium on fully amortizing structures relative to bullet maturities that rely on future refinancing."*** JPM also warns these bonds *"lack many of the traditional catalysts that drive corporate credit performance, particularly on the upside"* — i.e. a structurally capped total return.
- ⚠️ **THE INDEX PROBLEM, WHICH GATES WHO CAN EVEN BUY IT: most project finance has been EXCLUDED from the key indices for two reasons — 144A-for-life registration status and limited syndication.** That bounds the investor base and therefore secondary-market liquidity. ➤ **A structural reason this channel absorbs less than its headline size suggests.**
- 🔴 **THE VALUATION RULE, AND IT IS THE MOST TRANSFERABLE LINE IN THE NOTE: because these bonds amortise, WEIGHTED AVERAGE LIFE is the relevant duration measure, not stated maturity** — *"a bond with a ten-year maturity but significant amortization may have a WAL closer to six or seven years, making it more comparable to the tenant's intermediate-term corporate debt than its ten-year bonds."* And the benchmark: *"project finance bonds should typically trade wider than the tenant's unsecured debt at a comparable WAL to compensate investors for construction, power, concentration, liquidity, and structural risks."* ➤ **So the offtake-vs-corporate spread differentials this wiki tracks (the Meta- and Oracle-tenanted paper on [[ORCL]]) must be compared at MATCHED WAL, not at a matched maturity label.**
- 🔴 **TWO HARD STRUCTURAL REQUIREMENTS: (a) bond maturity MUST occur before lease expiration**, so the rent obligation covers the life of the debt — most deals are structured with expected maturity several years before lease expiry, and *"if a bond remains outstanding after lease expiration, the credit effectively shifts from a contracted rental stream to a real estate exposure"*; **(b) amortisation generally begins only after substantial completion and lease commencement**, so principal repayment is supported by contracted operating cash flow rather than an unfinished asset.
- 🔴 **THE SIZING METRIC TO USE INSTEAD OF CAP RATES: CONTRACT VALUE — the aggregate base rent payable over the initial lease term.** *"A bond-to-contract-value ratio set at a meaningful discount offers protection, as the cumulative rent collected during the initial term should be sufficient to repay the debt even if the tenant does not renew at expiration."* JPM prefers it to cap rates or appraised values because those move with market conditions while contract value is fixed at closing.
- 🔴 **WHAT THE NNN LEASE ACTUALLY IS, STRIPPED OF THE REAL-ESTATE LANGUAGE: *"For bond investors, the NNN structure functions primarily as a liability transfer mechanism. The result is a leveraged exposure to a long-dated rental stream, with credit quality driven largely by the willingness and ability of a single, typically IG counterparty to continue writing checks."*** **Utility costs are passed straight through to the tenant** — which is why power-price volatility does not land on the bondholder — and rent is *"largely independent of the tenant's actual utilization of the facility."* ➤ **Read against this page's utilisation and token-economics debates: the project bond does not care whether the GPUs are busy. The tenant's credit does.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE ASYMMETRY MOST LIKELY TO BE MIS-READ, AND IT RUNS OPPOSITE TO INTUITION: TERMINATION FOR CONVENIENCE IS THE SAFER ONE.** Convenience terminations *"typically require substantial termination payments that are sufficient to repay outstanding indebtedness"* — the debt is repaid directly out of the termination payment. **TERMINATION FOR CAUSE IS THE DANGEROUS ONE** — *"the tenant is generally not required to repay the outstanding debt upon termination,"* leaving lenders to re-lease or sell. Typical for-cause triggers named: **prolonged failure to deliver contracted power or cooling capacity**, material SLA breaches, failure to maintain required operating standards. ➤ **So the credit risk in these structures concentrates in DELIVERY OF POWER, not in the tenant walking away.**
- 🔴 **POWER IS THE ASSET, in the desk's own words: *"While data centers are often viewed through the lens of the building, servers and real estate, power has actually become the defining asset and the primary bottleneck for bringing capacity online. Buildings can be replicated, capital can be raised, and equipment can be procured; but reliable, scalable electrical capacity is increasingly scarce and cannot be created on demand."*** Projects with **secured interconnection rights, committed utility capacity and expandable infrastructure** *"command a meaningful competitive advantage"*, and timelines are *"often driven more by grid availability and utility infrastructure than building construction."* An **Energy Service Agreement with the local utility, in place at bond issuance, is named as a key de-risking milestone** — and JPM tells investors to confirm the ESA *"extends through or beyond the lease term."*
- ⚠️⚠️ **A UNIT-DISCIPLINE ITEM FOR THIS WIKI'S GW LEDGER, NOW STATED IN WRITING BY A CREDIT DESK: *"Critical IT load and utility capacity measure different aspects of a data center's power profile and should not be used interchangeably."*** Critical IT load is the power available to revenue-generating servers/storage/networking; **utility capacity is the total electrical service from the grid and EXCEEDS critical IT load** (the difference being cooling, power distribution, lighting and other M&E). *"Since lease commitments, pricing, and market comparisons are typically based on critical IT load rather than total utility capacity, it is the more relevant measure of a facility's economic value and revenue-generating potential."* ➤ **Same trap `_meta/assumptions.md` already guards — facility GW ≠ IT-load GW. Cite this when enforcing it.**
- **THE HY ADJACENCY, KEPT SEPARATE ON PURPOSE: the high-yield “high performance compute” (HPC) bond market sits next to this one but is a different risk.** Those deals are *"backed by GPU clusters, AI compute capacity, neocloud platforms"* — i.e. **shorter-lived equipment, GPU depreciation, customer concentration, utilisation and hardware-refresh risk** — *"making these transactions more akin to operating businesses than the IG transactions."* JPM also flags that the market may eventually see **shorter-dated financings tied to SILICON deployment**, which *"will likely differ from data center project finance bonds given the shorter useful lives, faster depreciation and different collateral characteristics of the underlying assets."* ➤ **The IG-vs-HY split is the cleanest available answer to “is a data-centre bond an infrastructure asset or a compute asset?” — it depends entirely on what is pledged.**
- **RATINGS MECHANICS, FOR READING ANY NEW DEAL'S PRESALE: Moody's** runs a scorecard weighted first to business profile and the predictability of net cash flows, then operating risk and leverage/coverage; **S&P** builds a standalone credit profile **separately across the construction and operating phases** before structural modifiers; **Fitch** is the most scenario-driven, *"developing bespoke rating case scenarios, calibrating stresses based on the relative strength of individual risk attributes."* All three treat **construction risk as *"temporary but binary"*** and **operating risk as *"longer term and driven by cash flow stability"***, making the construction→operations handover *"a critical inflection point in the project's credit profile."*
- **TWO SECOND-ORDER PROVISIONS THAT CHANGE THE CREDIT MID-LIFE: IG FALLAWAY COVENANTS** relax protections (additional-debt limits, distribution limits, enhanced reserve requirements) while the tenant holds an IG rating — *"particularly relevant for the HPC deals that have been issued in HY because, in most cases, successful completion of those projects should drive ratings upgrades to IG"*; and **SPONSOR SUPPORT FALLAWAY**, where completion support and funding commitments expire once the project hits operational or financial milestones, *"leaving bondholders primarily reliant on the project's assets, contracts and cash flows for repayment."*
- ⚠️ **THE ONE ILLUSTRATIVE CAPITAL STRUCTURE THE NOTE PUBLISHES — FLAGGED AS HYPOTHETICAL, BECAUSE JPM FLAGS IT ITSELF** (*"All figures, tables and illustrative examples are hypothetical and presented solely for explanatory purposes"*): a **~77% senior secured notes / ~23% sponsor equity** sources split, with uses at **84.6% construction capex, 11.9% interest during construction, 2.3% initial reserve funding, 1.2% transaction fees**. **DO NOT quote this as a market-observed gearing level for any actual deal.**

- 🔴 **AND THE CREDIT RATINGS ACTIONS THAT CAME WITH IT — DATED 2026-07-07, WHICH IS THE POINT, BECAUSE TWO OF THEM ARE ALREADY ON THIS WIKI DATED 07-20 VIA THE JOINT EQUITY-&-CREDIT CALL THAT RELAYED THEM. ⚠️ CREDIT RATINGS ONLY — NOT EQUITY RATINGS, NOT PRICE TARGETS:**
  - **[[MSFT]] DOWNGRADED to UNDERWEIGHT (credit)** — *"driven primarily by expected heavy supply. Microsoft is the only hyperscaler in our coverage that has yet to access the bond market to fund its capital projects"*, against **~$190bn of calendar-2026 capex, *"another step higher expected in 2027"* and ~$9bn of FY27 refi needs**. The 10yr *"remains the tightest point in IG TMT and continues to trade through peers, leaving little compensation for the issuance we expect."* Risk to the call: MSFT funding a large share **with equity issuance or cash on hand**. ⚠️ **The ~$190bn is a CREDIT-desk calendar-2026 mark from July, near the top of the street-high column in the State-of-play table above (cons 158 / st-high 193) — it is NOT a company guide and it is two months stale.**
  - **[[META]] DOWNGRADED to NEUTRAL (credit)** — the credit *"continues to screen attractively and has long traded wide for its rating and growth profile"*, but *"we no longer see a clear near-term catalyst for that gap to close"*: management *"has yet to convince the market that its accelerating AI spending will bear fruit in the form of ROIC"*, and Meta *"faces meaningful funding needs over the next eighteen months with no defined funding strategy, leaving the name perpetually exposed to prospective supply before the investment case has been fully established."* Reported external compute sales are read as *"a hedge on future monetization"* rather than a near-term strategic shift.
  - **[[ORCL]] UPGRADED to OVERWEIGHT (credit)** — the widest-trading hyperscaler in JPM's credit coverage and *"the only hyperscaler offering a genuinely attractive entry point at current spreads"*, but explicitly *"a longer-term call rather than one we expect to be rewarded immediately."* Full detail on [[ORCL]].
  - **UNCHANGED: [[GOOG]] stays OVERWEIGHT** — *"has pre-funded the majority of its upcoming needs for AI capex"*, with debt-issuance needs *"mitigated significantly by the firm's $85bn equity issuance announcement"*; **[[AMZN]] stays OVERWEIGHT** *"despite spreads that continue to screen somewhat tight."*
  - ➤ **THE CROSS-PAGE POINT WORTH CARRYING: on 2026-07-07 a single IG credit desk was UNDERWEIGHT the hyperscaler with the most cash and OVERWEIGHT the one with the most debt — because in credit the variable is PROSPECTIVE SUPPLY, not balance-sheet strength. Hold that inversion whenever this page quotes a credit view next to an equity view.**

(SemiAnalysis, via Vital Knowledge, 2026-07-06; see also [NVDA.md](../NVDA.md))
- **The primary source, quantified — AI capex is shifting from hyperscaler cashflow to debt; ">$7T AI debt by 2029" and the "AI Project Trinity" (SemiAnalysis "Nvidia GPU Debt Backstop Unleashes the AI Project Trinity", 2026-07-06).** The full SemiAnalysis note (Nishball/Cheang/Fong/Knuhtsen/Orejas/Nanos/Ong/Patel) behind the Vital Knowledge relay above. The core thesis for this page: **"up until now the majority of AI buildouts have been primarily cashflow funded by the hyperscalers (Google, Amazon, Meta, Microsoft, Oracle)" — but "over the last year that's started to turn with Oracle then Meta, and now even Google turning to debt"** (SemiAnalysis, 2026-07-06). Hard sizing: **AI debt financing becomes a multi-trillion-dollar credit market with >$7T outstanding by 2029** (~$7.1T), driven by both AI IT capex and AI datacenter capex — making it the **2nd-largest asset-backed debt market after the ~$13T US MBS market**; **annual AI capex "well north of $2T in 2028"**; and **cumulative AI capex 2024–2029 ~$11.1T, with credit markets the main funding source**. The financing mechanism: AI clusters were made financeable by **long-term take-or-pay compute contracts backed by IG hyperscalers (most common offtake period = 5 years)**, but that template only works while hyperscaler balance sheets can backstop the deals — "once hyperscalers exhaust their capacity to backstop deals, there will be no further projects to lend to." Executing any buildout requires all three legs of the **"AI Project Trinity" — Capital + Offtake + Datacenter** (each leg's financeability depends on the other two). NVDA's new **GPU take-or-pay "backstop"** (a minimum-revenue guarantee to Neoclouds — typically 6yr, ~18–20% avg take-rate on revenue above the floor, off-balance-sheet on a "cloud service agreement" line unless triggered — leveraging NVDA's **AA/Aa2 IG rating**) is the mechanism making non-hyperscaler ("Neocloud") clusters financeable; NVDA is also directly leasing datacenter capacity (>700MW signed in the last two quarters) to solve the hardest Datacenter leg, and competing against Google's external-TPU-via-backstop. NVDA models its cloud-services-agreement contingent guarantee reaching **$77.5B by end-F1/27 → $175.3B by end-F1/29**, with incremental backstop revenue of **$1.8B (F1/27) → $13.9B (F1/29)** (near-pure margin). Read for this page: the capex wall the table above tracks is increasingly **credit-funded**, and NVDA's backstop is the financing layer that keeps the marginal (non-hyperscaler) gigawatt bankable — a bull (broadens the buyer base beyond a few hyperscalers) and a systemic-risk flag (a >$7T ABS market on novel, price-risk collateral) at once. (SemiAnalysis "Nvidia GPU Debt Backstop Unleashes the AI Project Trinity: Capital, Offtake and Datacenters", 2026-07-06; see also [NVDA.md](../NVDA.md), [CRWV.md](../CRWV.md), [tokenmaxxing.md](tokenmaxxing.md))

## Recent signals (Jul 4)
- **Capex-related credit issuance now the concentration risk in IG — GS floor color (Goldman Sachs · Adam Crook desk, "Thoughts from the Floor", 2026-07-04).** GS's cross-asset floor note reframes the capex debate through the credit lens: **$1.3trn of IG supply in H1 2026 — "the same as ALL of '22/'23/'24"** — with issuance "consistently surprising to the upside," as "hyperscaler capex estimates keep getting refreshed higher + new issues are creating concentration-risk in the IG long end." GS pitches capex-issuance-widening hedges (a custom 30y IG basket short via TRS; CDX IG payer spreads) as a **"convex overlay-hedge"** for the scenario where "material new information challenges the bull-case for AI and/or capex/investment plans." Names **hyperscaler + AI capex expectations as the biggest risk to earnings this quarter** into the print season starting later this month. A financing/credit-market read on the same capex wall this page tracks — the bull table above is increasingly funded by record IG supply, which is itself the tail-risk. (Goldman Sachs · Adam Crook, sales commentary, 2026-07-04)

## Recent signals (Jul 2)
- **The SemiAnalysis primary source behind the Meta-Compute headline: 2027 capex "shockingly high," procurement *accelerating*, >5GW contracted H1'26 (SemiAnalysis "Meta Compute: Everyone Wants To Be A Cloud", 2026-07-02).** The report the Street reacted to argues *both* the aggressive-sell-off-of-neoclouds and the "overcapacity" takes are "erroneous": Meta's DC & compute procurement "will **accelerate, not slow down**," and **capex in 2027 will be shockingly high.** Hard data: **Meta contracted over 5GW of capacity across Cloud & Colo in just the first six months of the year** (excludes accelerating self-build), and its two largest campuses alone = **2.5GW under construction** — an explicit rebuttal of the "half of US datacenters are delayed / only 5GW under construction" narrative. After ~10GW of deals signed since early 2024, the bulk of Meta's additions now run through **3rd parties** — "Meta will be a huge source of RPO growth for the likes of Coreweave, Nebius and others" (a *bull* read for neoclouds, cutting against the Day-2 −8% neocloud move). The four monetization use-cases (MSL frontier training, RecSys >10x scale-up, Bedrock-type token-as-a-service via Anthropic, SpaceX-style on-demand compute at ~$50B/GW-of-annual-revenue where 200MW ≈ >$10B/yr) are why Meta can keep aggressively contracting — "a CFO's dream." (SemiAnalysis "Meta Compute", 2026-07-02; see also [META.md](../META.md), [ai-datacenter-power.md](ai-datacenter-power.md), [tokenmaxxing.md](tokenmaxxing.md))
- **AWS margins inflected on Anthropic/Bedrock mix while Azure/Oracle/CoreWeave lagged (SemiAnalysis "Anthropic Growth & Bedrock Mix", 2026-05-27).** Backfilled primary source on the CSP-margin divergence: AWS **EBIT margins +213bp Q/Q** in 1Q26 driven by customer spend on Claude through Bedrock (AWS's higher share of 3P model-API spend + Anthropic/Bedrock deal structure + Anthropic ARR outperformance) — "the only CSP with a true rising trend." Contrast: **Oracle and CoreWeave both disappointed** on cloud-arm profits, **Azure margins trending down**, and **Google Cloud's margin rise is "an illusion"** ("EBTIT" — Earnings Before Training, Interest and Taxes) because DeepMind/Gemini training expenses (~$5.4B 1Q26 "Alphabet-Level Activities") sit outside the GCP segment. Bedrock is a ~**$5.5B run-rate** business (80-90%+ Anthropic models), ~37% of AWS AI revenue (from 9% 1Q25); AWS AI mix 2%→10% of total revenue 1Q24→1Q26. The read-through for capex: TaaS (token-as-a-service) is materially higher-margin than IaaS rental, so the top hyperscalers' margin advantage — which underwrites their capex firepower — is *widening* vs neoclouds/Oracle (SemiAnalysis "Anthropic Growth and Bedrock Mix Drive AWS Margins Higher", 2026-05-27; see also [AMZN.md](../AMZN.md), [tokenmaxxing.md](tokenmaxxing.md)). _Note: dated 2026-05-27, backfilled into the wiki on the 2026-07-02 ingest run._
- **Meta-Compute Day-2 factor rotation (Rothschild & Co · Rowan Adley, TMT Daily, 2026-07-02).** META **+8.8%**, **semis −6.3%**, **neoclouds −8% (CRWV −14%, NBIS −17%)**, **Software +3%** (degrossing winner), **KOSPI −7.9%**, high-beta momentum −9.6% (one of the worst single-day moves, though not a 1-month low). Three reads on the pivot: (i) **bull "insurance policy"** — META renting 1GW at xAI/Anthropic rates ≈ **~$15bn/yr**, defuses Metaverse-2.0 anxiety; (ii) **bear structural-overcapacity** — if META has real excess to resell, so may the whole ecosystem → **capex run-rate cuts coming** (the read behind the semis move); (iii) **capital-markets** — Zuck may have "planted" the story to pop the stock and then do a **GOOG-style capex-derisking capital raise**. Net: hyperscale-capex-visibility debate "got a lot more nuanced." (Rothschild & Co · Rowan Adley, 2026-07-02; see also [META.md](../META.md), [CRWV.md](../CRWV.md), [NBIS.md](../NBIS.md))
- **BofA rebuts the Meta memory-cut fear (BofA · Simon Woo/Dai Shen/Vivek Arya, Global Memory weekly, 2026-07-02).** Chipmakers/supply-chain (NAND controller, substrate) say **Meta will NOT cut memory orders — its long-term memory/component orders are getting *stronger*** (more HBM/LPDDR5/eSSD for AI DCs); the "Meta Compute = excess capacity" Street speculation is "groundless." A direct fundamental counter to the bear leg of the Day-2 memory selloff. (BofA Global Memory Tech, 2026-07-02; see also [hbm-memory.md](hbm-memory.md), [SAMSUNG.md](../SAMSUNG.md))
- **NVDA to backstop customers' GPUs and take a cut of their cloud revenues (The Information, 2026-07-02).** Nvidia offering to guarantee GPU value for (neo)cloud buyers in exchange for a share of the cloud revenue those chips generate — deepens the vendor-financing/demand-guarantee entanglement underpinning the buildout (bull: de-risks customer capex; bear: circular-financing dependence). Rothschild Redburn keeps **NVDA Buy — "Growth Beyond Hyperscalers."** (The Information; Rothschild & Co Redburn, 2026-07-02; see also [NVDA.md](../NVDA.md))

Read for the capex theme: the house 2027 GOOGL line ($285-310B) is now below MS's $350B '27 — the street-high is migrating up as '28 TPU visibility improves. (MS TMT / Skyler Risom, 2026-06-30; see also [GOOG.md](../GOOG.md))
- **OpenAI Q1 cash burn ~$3.7B (~$15B annualized) (The Information, 2026-06-30).** A fresh quantified burn print on the largest single demand anchor behind hyperscaler/neocloud capex — feeds the "can the model companies fund the compute commitments" question underpinning the whole cycle, and the delayed-IPO (2027) capital-markets-tolerance debate. (The Information, 2026-06-30; see also [OPENAI.md](../OPENAI.md))
- **OpenAI leaning toward delaying IPO to 2027 — retail enthusiasm lacking at $1T+ valuation (Vital Knowledge / NYT, 2026-06-25).** OpenAI is now leaning toward delaying its IPO until **2027**, with both Vital Knowledge and the NYT citing that retail investor enthusiasm is insufficient at OpenAI's current private-market valuation ($1T+). This is a structural read-through for the AI capex cycle: (1) if OpenAI stays private longer, its capital raises remain private-markets-dependent (SoftBank Vision Fund, NVDA equity deals, Apollo/Blackstone SPVs) — capex-cycle continuity depends on PE/VC appetite, not equity-market sentiment; (2) the $1T+ valuation vs "retail enthusiasm lacking" frames a tension between model-company ARR ($47B+ run-rate per FundaAI) and public-market pricing tolerance — the same macro that is pushing hyperscaler PEs down. (Vital Knowledge / NYT, 2026-06-25; see also [OPENAI.md](../OPENAI.md))
- **MSFT positioned as indirect beneficiary of OpenAI Jalapeño (Broadcom ASIC) via IP ownership through 2031 (FinTwit, 2026-06-24).** Per QCOM Investor Day, OpenAI's custom chip "Jalapeño" (designed with Broadcom) has its IP owned by MSFT through 2031 under OpenAI investment terms. If Azure becomes the deployment fabric for OpenAI's Jalapeño-powered inference, the MSFT Azure capex-per-GW of inference effectively drops (substituting NVDA GPU $/kW with in-house-designed ASIC on a more favorable TCO curve). This is a **capex-mix story**, not a capex-magnitude story — MSFT's total DC spend stays elevated, but the composition shifts toward lower-cost inference per GW as Jalapeño ramps. Net: MSFT's capex spend is unchanged in aggregate, but the efficiency per dollar of inference improves. (@SouthernValue95; @PatrickMoorhead, 2026-06-24; see also [MSFT.md](../MSFT.md))
- **BofA framing: "internet stocks could rotate back" as AI capex compounds (BofA context, 2026-06-24).** The broader BofA tech-strategy read (from their AI 2030 note, referenced in their June flows) is that the AI capex cycle is now large enough that "internet stocks could rotate back" — with advertising and software beneficiaries (META, GOOG, MSFT apps) seeing a second-wave re-rate as AI monetization proves out, rotating capital away from the picks-and-shovels semis (NVDA, AVGO) that have already re-rated. For the capex theme: this reframe does not change the capital flows but suggests the **equity leadership in the cycle is rotating from suppliers to users**, which is a read-through for the relative trade (hyperscalers vs semis into the second half of 2026). (BofA "AI 2030", 2026-05-13; buy-side desk feedback, 2026-06-24)
- **AWS monetizing the shortage; Anthropic pushing back on price (BofA / Rothschild, 2026-06-30).** AWS is raising fees **+20% on select GPU workloads** (on top of the +20% EC2 Capacity Block hike effective Jul 1) — a **1-2pt benefit to 2H26 AWS growth** (BofA / Justin Post) — evidence that hyperscalers are recouping capex via compute pricing power as supply stays tight. Counter-signal on the model-layer economics: **Anthropic is renegotiating a key part of its Amazon partnership, changing how Amazon pays to use Anthropic's models next year** (R&Co: Anthropic "flexing pricing power over Amazon"; Neutral) — a reminder that the frontier labs can claw back some of the value in the capex-for-tokens exchange. (BofA / Justin Post; Rothschild & Co / Rowan Adley, 2026-06-30; see also [AMZN.md](../AMZN.md))

## House-model reconciliation (cross-check, 2026-06-25)
The dashboard's `capstone` line does **NOT** currently match the official Capstone models — and only GOOG + META have official models at all.

| Name | Dashboard house 25/26/27 | Official model (canonical Summary tab) | Verdict |
|---|--|--|--|
| GOOGL | 91.4 / 180 / 285 | 91.4 / **182.7** / **310** (Google model, Summary) | 2025 ✓; 2026 & 2027 ✗ (model Summary 2027 = 310; the 285 matches the model's *Chip-breakdown* tab, not Summary). Model is internally inconsistent: 2027 capex reads 280 / 285 / 310 across tabs. |
| META | 72 / 125 / 160 | 69.9 / **130.9** / **169.6** (Meta model, Meta Summary) | none cleanly match. 2025 dashboard (72) > actual (~69.7); 2026 (125) matches the model's *PP&E Detail / guidance* path but not Summary (131); 2027 (160) ≠ either tab (150 vs 170). |
| MSFT | 104.6 / 170 / 280 | — **no official model** | unverifiable |
| AMZN | 132 / 200 / 300 | — **no official model** | unverifiable |
| ORCL | 33.1 / 92 / 150 | — **no official model** | unverifiable |

**Takeaways:** (1) the dashboard house line is stale/decoupled from the official GOOG/META models; (2) the official models disagree *with themselves* across tabs (Summary vs PP&E/Chip-build/Scenarios) — pick one canonical capex row before wiring; (3) we have **no** official MSFT/AMZN/ORCL model, so those house lines are placeholders. This is the "improve later" work the dashboard needs.

Net-new demand leg: **EXPERIMENT COMPUTE** (architecture search, data mixtures, sweeps, ablations — "an order-of-magnitude fewer attempts" with fewer chips; ceiling becomes a function of available capacity). Falsifiers stated by the author: **continual learning far slower than expected (catastrophic forgetting unsolved — "the genuine soft spot in the thesis"), commercialisation lagging capability (the dot-com structure — "wrong by three years, which was enough to lose 70%"), and safety/regulation deliberately slowing things (evaluation thresholds, compute caps, deployment licensing).** ⚠️ Independent newsletter, not a broker note. TAM leg on [ai-tam-bofa](ai-tam-bofa.md); July drawdown leg on [macro-cycle](macro-cycle.md).

NO capex figure on this page superseded — the $1.4trn / ~120 GW anchor was already carried from MS's 12 Jul 2026 note; this run adds the primary 07-27 note that DEFENDS it and, more usefully, the arithmetic that connects gigawatts to dollars.** Source: **Morgan Stanley · Brian Nowak, CFA (with Stephen Byrd, Adam Wood), "The Paths to 25-50% GenAI ROIC," 2026-07-27**, Internet | North America, Industry View Attractive, slug `INTERNET_20260727_2024.html`. **Net-new and the reason to log it here: the $39bn-per-GW all-in capital cost decomposition — IT capex $23bn on a 5-year useful life plus non-IT (powered shell) capex $16bn on a 15-year useful life — flowing to ~$6bn/yr of depreciation for owned infrastructure, assuming ~$4mn per GB300 rack (tied to MS's Asia semis team's published rack BoM) and 410,256 GB300 chips in 1GW.** **This is the bridge this page has been missing: any GW figure can now be converted to capex on a stated, sourced basis, and run the other way ~120 GW of '28 capacity implies roughly $4.7trn of cumulative all-in spend at GB300 economics.** ⚠️ **Basis discipline recorded on the page: IT-load GW at GB300 rack economics, not facility GW, and a GB300-generation cost that moves with each chip generation — do not mix with the facility-GW marks elsewhere on this page.** **⚠️ THE BEAR FOOTHOLD IS MS'S OWN AND IS CARRIED VERBATIM RATHER THAN SMOOTHED, because it is the reconciliation anyone will need when hyperscaler margins print below the framework: "while we believe cloud providers could see 60-70% incremental EBIT margins on GB300 inference capacity, REPORTED near-term incremental margins will remain below that range due to 1) the mix of capacity being served (majority of near-term incremental business is likely from prior chip generations, as GB300 capacity is still scaling), 2) mix of workloads (traditional cloud workloads in addition to new AI-specific workloads), and 3) additional opex that may not be reflected in this analysis (i.e. headcount and other R&D)." MS concedes the near-term gap rather than arguing it away, and answers it with mix shift over time.** Structural conclusion logged for Key debates: **"These healthy potential returns are also an invitation for competition… which will only further 1) keep an importance on product and compute efficiency innovation and 2) highlight the value of compute infrastructure assets"** — i.e. returns compete away at the model layer and capitalise into the gigawatts, which is the cleanest available rebuttal to the "capex is value-destructive" framing this page tracks. Bullish **AMZN, GOOGL, MSFT** (infrastructure) and **GOOGL, META** (models). The three ROIC models themselves (IaaS ~31%, model-API owned ~46%, model-API 3P ~25%) are filed on [tokenmaxxing](tokenmaxxing.md); the power/shell and utilisation legs on [ai-datacenter-power](ai-datacenter-power.md).

(4) **The memory conversion channel quantified (BofA · Justin Post via Simon Woo, 07-23/24): Alphabet capex US$200bn/300bn in '26/'27 (vs US$91bn '25) → Google alone needs 50%+ more memory chips in 2027 than 2026**; Post's Alphabet FCF path (**-$16bn/-18bn '26/'27 → +$37bn** thereafter, ~$50bn cash p.a.) is materially less alarming than the tape implies.
